## ai-budget-assistant

> Turborepo monorepo with 5 packages:

# AI Budget Assistant

## Architecture

Turborepo monorepo with 5 packages:

| Package | Tech | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `apps/api` | NestJS 10 + Prisma 5 + PostgreSQL + Redis | REST API backend |
| `apps/mobile` | Expo 54 + React Native 0.81 + Zustand + SQLite/Drizzle | Mobile app (iOS/Android/Web) |
| `apps/admin` | Next.js 16 + React 19 + TailwindCSS 4 + shadcn/ui + Recharts | Admin web dashboard (port 3001) |
| `packages/shared-types` | TypeScript interfaces | Entities and DTOs shared between api and mobile |
| `packages/shared-utils` | Zod schemas + formatting + constants | Validation and utilities shared between api and mobile |

## Key Patterns

### API (NestJS)
- **Module structure**: `modules/<feature>/` contains `module.ts`, `controller.ts`, `service.ts`, `dto/index.ts`, `guards/`
- **Auth**: JWT via `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)`. Request type: `AuthenticatedRequest` from `common/types/index.ts`. Password reset via 6-digit email code (`POST /auth/forgot-password`, `POST /auth/reset-password`) with in-memory rate limiting and bcrypt-hashed codes. Email change also via 6-digit code (`POST /auth/change-email/request`, `POST /auth/change-email/confirm`) — JWT-guarded, requires current password on request, issues new tokens on confirm. **Google sign-in (ABA-282)**: `POST /auth/google` (public) takes a Google **ID token** from the client (mobile + web via `expo-auth-session`, no native module), verified server-side by `GoogleTokenVerifier` (`google-auth-library`, audiences from `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_IDS`, requires `email_verified`). Resolves by `googleId` → **auto-link by verified email** (sets `googleId`; rejects + does not link a deactivated account) → else creates a verified, passwordless user (name/email from Google, language from client, currency default USD) + default account. Returns the same shape as `/auth/login`. `User.googleId String? @unique` + `passwordHash` now **nullable** (migration `20260621000000_add_google_auth`); `login()` and `changeEmailRequest()` reject null-passwordHash accounts ("use Google sign-in"). Mobile: `authStore.googleLogin`, `useGoogleAuth` hook, "Continue with Google" on `(auth)/login.tsx`+`register.tsx`. **Web nonce (ABA-291)**: the web path uses the **generic** `expo-auth-session` `useAuthRequest` with `responseType: IdToken`, which (unlike `providers/Google`) does NOT add a `nonce` — Google's OpenID implicit flow requires one, so without it web sign-in failed with `Error 400: invalid_request / GeneralOAuthFlow` (native was fine — `signInNative()` builds its own URL with a manual nonce). Fix: `useGoogleAuth` generates a `nonce` once (`useMemo(() => rnd(), [])`) and passes `extraParams: { nonce }` to the web `useAuthRequest`. **Prerequisite**: Google Cloud OAuth client IDs (`GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_IDS` on API; `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_WEB/IOS/ANDROID_CLIENT_ID` on client)
- **Restore credentials / Android session restore, stage 1 = server (ABA-464), stage 2 = Android client (ABA-465)**: `modules/restore-credentials/` wraps `@simplewebauthn/server` — a restore credential is a real WebAuthn public-key credential, verified exactly as a passkey would be, for Google Play's April-2027 requirement that a signed-in session survive an Android device transfer. **Two controllers, deliberately not one**: `RestoreCredentialRegistrationController` (`GET auth/restore/register/options`, `POST auth/restore/register`, `DELETE auth/restore`) carries a class-level `JwtAuthGuard`; `RestoreCredentialAuthController` (`GET auth/restore/options`, `POST auth/restore`) carries none, because the caller is a freshly-restored device with no token — splitting means a public route can never inherit a guard and a guarded route can never quietly lose one. Both public routes pair `@UseGuards(ThrottlerGuard)` with `@Throttle` (20/60s, 10/60s) — this app registers no global throttler guard, so `@Throttle` alone is inert (same gotcha as `import-bank.controller.ts`/`guest.controller.ts`). **Table** `user_restore_credentials` (migration `20260830120000_add_user_restore_credentials`) deliberately allows **several rows per user** — the unique is on `credentialId`, not `userId`, because a `userId` unique would let a second device's registration overwrite the first, and restoring from that older backup would then fail against a public key the server no longer holds. **Challenges live in Redis**, `restorecred:reg:{userId}` / `restorecred:auth:{challenge}`, TTL 300s; the auth challenge is consumed via the new atomic `CacheService.getAndDelete` (`GETDEL`) **before** signature verification, so a replay can't race a slow verification and two concurrent submissions of one assertion can't both mint a session from it — fails closed (`null`) on a Redis error. **`signCount`**: 0 is accepted (means "this authenticator doesn't report counts", not "went backwards" — a restore credential's whole purpose is arriving on a device with no guarantee the old counter travelled), and a stored non-zero followed by a presented 0 is accepted too; only both-non-zero-and-not-advancing is rejected. `attestationType: 'none'` — trust comes from the origin↔assetlinks binding, not attestation provenance. **The trust chain has two encodings of one fingerprint** — `assetlinks.json` uses colon-separated uppercase hex, the WebAuthn origin an Android app reports is unpadded base64url of the same 32 raw bytes — converted in exactly one place, `restore-credential.config.ts`'s `fingerprintHexToApkKeyHash`; `rpId` is the apex `ai-budget.pl`, `expectedOrigin` is an array built from whatever `RESTORE_CREDENTIAL_CERT_FINGERPRINTS` lists — the config supports several fingerprints at once, but production ships exactly one, the release App Signing certificate; a debug fingerprint is deliberately NOT trusted (the template `debug.keystore`'s private key is public, and `attestationType: 'none'` means this origin↔assetlinks binding is the feature's only trust anchor — see decision 7 in `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-restore-credentials-design.md`). The service never takes the API down over misconfiguration: `resolveRestoreCredentialConfig` throws when `RESTORE_CREDENTIAL_CERT_FINGERPRINTS` is unset, the constructor catches + logs a warning + holds `config: null`, and a private `requireConfig()` throws `ServiceUnavailableException` per call (`deleteForUser` skips that guard so sign-out cleanup still works on a misconfigured deployment). The authenticated user is resolved from the **stored credential row's `userId`**, never the assertion's `userHandle`. `AuthService.buildAuthResponse(user, overrideDefaultAccountId?)` was extracted (public) so a restore login returns byte-identical session JSON to `/auth/login`/`/auth/google`. **`docs/ops/assetlinks.json`** (listing only `delegate_permission/common.get_login_creds` — deliberately NOT `handle_all_urls`, which would be a much broader, unneeded site-wide App Links grant) ships to `https://ai-budget.pl/.well-known/assetlinks.json` via the apex tree in `web-deploy.yml`, which fails the deploy on a missing file or a leftover `REPLACE_WITH_` placeholder — the release-key fingerprint is still a placeholder today, so that guard **currently blocks the entire `web-deploy.yml` job**, not just the apex rsync: its failing step runs before both rsyncs, so it also freezes the `app.ai-budget.pl` SPA deploy until the Play App Signing SHA-256 is filled in (runbook `docs/ops/restore-credentials-rollout.md`). **Stage 2 client (ABA-465) closes the loop stage 1 left open.** **Native module** `apps/mobile/android/.../restorecredentials/RestoreCredentialModule.kt` is a legacy Old-Arch `ReactContextBaseJavaModule` — no TurboModule spec, registered by hand in `MainApplication.kt`'s `getPackages()` alongside `NotificationCapturePackage()` (same Windows MAX_PATH / Fabric-codegen constraint that pattern exists for). `androidx.credentials:credentials` / `credentials-play-services-auth` are pinned to **1.6.0 stable**, not Google's own sample's `1.7.0-alpha03` — restore credentials only need >=1.5.0, so the alpha buys nothing. Uses the **callback (`*Async`) Credential Manager APIs, not the suspend ones** — a Promise is already a callback, and the suspend variants would tie the module to an Activity-scoped coroutine scope for no gain. Every `@ReactMethod` wraps its async dispatch in a `try` that ends exactly at the call, so a synchronous failure (e.g. malformed request JSON) can never race an already-scheduled native callback into double-settling the Promise. `createCredential` retries once with `isCloudBackupEnabled = false` on `E2eeUnavailableException` (no screen lock / no Google backup on the device), so that device still gets a local-only key that survives a cable transfer instead of nothing. **Bridge** `src/services/restoreCredentials/index.{ts,android,ios,web}.ts` — every exported function resolves, **never rejects**; `null` means "not available", the normal answer on iOS/web and on any Android device that was never restored. `getRestoreCredential` fails **silently** (it's the expected outcome on every cold start on an un-restored device — logging would be permanent noise); `createRestoreCredential`/`clearRestoreCredential` `console.warn`. **Flows** `src/features/auth/restoreCredential.ts`: `registerRestoreCredential(userId)` posts the attestation and marks `restoreCredentialFlag.markSynced` (MMKV, keyed by user id so a second account on the same device also registers) only AFTER the server confirms — marking earlier would let a network failure or server rejection permanently block retry; `attemptRestoreSession()` is bounded by `RESTORE_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000` because it runs inside `authStore.initialize()` before the first screen draws, and an unbounded native call would freeze the app on the splash (`clearTimeout` in `.finally()` so the loser doesn't leak a handle — same shape of problem as `captureCurrentLocation`'s 4s race). **Registration fires both after sign-in (`login()`, fire-and-forget) AND on every authenticated launch** (`useAuthenticatedBootstrap`'s `isAuthenticated` effect, gated on `!restoreCredentialFlag.hasSynced(userId)`) — **the launch path is the load-bearing one**: sign-in only reaches users who authenticate from now on, but `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` is 7 days and the app otherwise restores its session from local storage indefinitely, so without the launch path the entire installed base would silently never get a credential. **Restore** (`authStore.ts`'s `applyRestoredSession`) replaces only `initialize()`'s no-stored-token branch — the corrupted-stored-user branch is deliberately untouched — and on success marks first-run onboarding **seen**: a restored device's local SQLite is empty until the first sync pull, and `useFirstRunOnboarding` reads exactly that signal, so without this a user with years of history would be shown "add your first expense". `isAuthenticated` is gated on `isVerified`, mirroring the stored-session branch. `AuthResponse.user.isVerified` was added to `packages/shared-types` (optional) — the API always returned it and only the type omitted it, so `login()` had been reading it through an implicit `any`. **Sign-out**: `logout()` calls `api.deleteRestoreCredentials()` inside the token-valid guard, then `clearRestoreCredential()` (native) unconditionally outside it — an offline sign-out still wipes the local passkey so a signed-out device can never silently offer a credential and sign back in. **Verification is production-only — nothing in this environment can execute the real Credential Manager round trip**: the two signals that will confirm it are rows appearing in `user_restore_credentials` (proves registration works) and a non-null `lastUsedAt` on one of them (proves a real restore happened).
- **Last-active tracking (ABA-389)**: `User.lastSyncAt` is the **last-activity** stamp — it is what the admin Users table shows (labelled **"Last Active"**, not "Last Login"), what `AdminAnalyticsService` counts active-today/active-this-week from (`admin-analytics.service.ts:160-161`), and what `ReferralsService` uses to qualify a referral (`referrals.service.ts:152-154`). It is written by `JwtStrategy.validate()`, fire-and-forget, on **every** authenticated request, via the leaf `LastActiveService.touch(userId)` (`modules/users/last-active.service.ts`, provided+exported by `UsersModule`) — throttled to at most one write per user per `LAST_ACTIVE_THROTTLE_SEC` (15 min) through the new atomic `CacheService.setIfAbsent(key, ttlSec)` (`SET … EX … NX`; returns `false` when Redis is down, so an outage skips the stamp rather than writing on every request). The public `login`/`googleLogin`/`refreshToken` stamps stay (those routes never reach `JwtStrategy`); `GET /users/me` no longer stamps explicitly (redundant + unthrottled). **Do not re-add a per-route `updateLastSync` call.** Why it moved: previously only those four routes wrote it, and the ordinary registration path hits none of them — `register()`/`verifyEmail()` return tokens directly (never `/auth/login`), `authStore.register` doesn't call `getProfile()` (unlike `login`/`googleLogin`), `authStore.initialize()` restores the session from local storage, and `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` is `7d` so no refresh for a week — so an active user read as "Never" indefinitely, undercounting the dashboard KPIs and silently withholding referral bonuses. The 5th writer, `POST /sync/push` (`sync.service.ts:75`), is unreachable from mobile (`pushChanges` in `subscriptions.api.ts:59` has zero call sites). Not covered: bot traffic (Telegram/WhatsApp/Slack doesn't authenticate via JWT); no backfill for users already stuck at `NULL`.
- **Account scoping**: `AccountContextGuard` middleware reads `X-Account-Id` header, resolves membership, adds `accountId` and `accountRole` to request
- **Role-based access**: `AccountRoleGuard` with `@RequireRole('owner'|'editor')` decorator (DI-based, needs `AccountsModule`). `ViewerBlockGuard` (ABA-155) — zero-dependency guard in same file; use as `@UseGuards(new ViewerBlockGuard())` on any write method without importing `AccountsModule`. Reads `req.accountRole` set by `AccountContextGuard`. Apply to all POST/PATCH/PUT/DELETE endpoints that mutate account-scoped data. AI chat: viewer write-actions blocked in `chat.service.ts` before a pending action is queued. Bots: `TelegramUserState` and `WhatsAppUserState` include `accountRole`; link services join `AccountMember` on `getLink()`; write handlers check the role before executing.
- **Service signature**: `(accountId, userId, dto)` as parameters, all Prisma queries filter by `accountId`
- **Transaction attribution**: `Expense.userId` and `Income.userId` already store the creator's id (set from `request.userId` at create time). Both services include `user: { select: { name: true } }` in all Prisma queries and call a `toExpenseResponse()`/`toIncomeResponse()` helper to flatten it into `createdByUserName: string | null` on the returned object. No migration needed — just always pass the user include and map the result. **Incomes typed (ABA-359)**: `incomes.service.ts`'s `toIncomeResponse`/list-filter `where` are typed via a shared `Prisma.IncomeInclude`-derived `incomeInclude` const + `IncomeWithRelations`/`IncomeResponse`/`Prisma.IncomeWhereInput` (was `any`/`any`, the `expenses.service.ts` side of this — `toExpenseResponse(expense: any)` — is still open, tracked by tech-debt `expense-service-response-typed-any`). Gotcha: `Prisma.IncomeWhereInput['date']` isn't always an object (can be a bare `Date`), so `where.date = { ...where.date, gte/lte }` fails to typecheck once `where` is properly typed — build the date range in a separate local `Prisma.DateTimeFilter` instead of spreading `where.date`. `incomes.service.spec.ts` covers `create` (all `resolveCategoryId` branches, tags, gamification/cache/family-feed fire-and-forgets), `findAll` (filter building + pagination), `findOne`, `update`, `remove`, `getByClientId`.
- **Database**: Prisma ORM. Schema at `apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma`. Uses `@map("snake_case")` for column names. Prod `DATABASE_URL` includes `connection_limit=10` (see `docker-compose.prod.yml`) to cap the connection pool.
- **46 modules**: `accounts`, `account-transfers`, `admin`, `ai`, `analytics`, `anomaly`, `app-versions`, `auth`, `backups`, `budgets`, `categories`, `community-prices`, `currency-exchange`, `debts`, `encryption`, `expenses`, `family-feed`, `gamification`, `health`, `import-bank`, `import-batches`, `import-wise`, `incomes`, `insights`, `investments`, `mail`, `merchant-rules`, `notifications`, `price-history`, `projects`, `purchase-requests`, `receipt-split`, `referrals`, `reports`, `restore-credentials`, `shopping-list`, `slack`, `subscriptions`, `sync`, `tags`, `telegram`, `trip-settle-up`, `user-subscriptions`, `users`, `wallet`, `whatsapp`
- **Merchant-to-category auto-learning** (ABA-261): `modules/merchant-rules/` — learns from manual expense category corrections and applies them at import time. Table `merchant_category_rules` (`migration 20260615000000_add_merchant_category_rules`), `@@unique([accountId, merchantNormalized])`. `merchantNormalized = merchant.trim().toLowerCase()`. `MerchantRulesService` exported to `ExpensesModule`, `ImportWiseModule`, `ImportBankModule`. **Learning**: `ExpensesService.update()` fires-and-forgets `upsertRule(accountId, merchantNormalized, resolvedCategoryId)` after a successful category change on an expense with a non-empty merchant. **Applying**: both import commits (`import-wise` + `import-bank`) call `getRulesMap(accountId)` **before** the `$transaction`, then apply the user rule as a higher-priority override over static `MERCHANT_CATEGORY_HINTS` / `suggestCategoryFromMerchantPL`. Endpoints: `GET /merchant-rules` (list with category name/icon), `DELETE /merchant-rules/:id` (ViewerBlockGuard). Rules are cascade-deleted when their category is deleted. Mobile: `merchantRulesStore.ts` (in-memory, loads from API on demand), "Category rules" section in `app/settings/merchants.tsx`. 4 i18n keys (`categoryRules`, `noRules`, `ruleDeleteConfirm`, `ruleDeleted`) in all 9 locales. No rule migration on merchant rename/merge (import CSVs produce the same raw merchant names regardless).
- **Admin module** (ABA-175): `modules/admin/` has four focused services — `AdminService` (user management + audit log + system health; injects `PrismaService`, `CacheService`), `AdminAnalyticsService` (dashboard KPIs, analytics overview, AI usage trends, subscription stats; injects `PrismaService`), `AdminNotificationService` (push/email/broadcast delivery, scheduled notifications cron, notification history; injects `PrismaService`, `MailService`, `NotificationsService`), `AdminInvestorMetricsService` (ABA-340 investor-metrics endpoint IO + Redis cache + assembly + PL/other language segments; injects `PrismaService`, `CacheService`). `AdminGateway` injects both `AdminService` and `AdminAnalyticsService`. Controller injects all four directly — no facade layer. `estimateCost()` and cost constants are exported from `admin-analytics.service.ts` and imported by `admin.service.ts` for `getUserDetail` and by `admin-investor-metrics.service.ts` for the AI-COGS gross margin. The `PRO_MONTHLY_USD`/`BUSINESS_MONTHLY_USD` cents pair that file used to export is **gone** (ABA-433) — every MRR figure in the admin now prices through `MRR_MONTHLY_USD` in `admin-metrics.util.ts`.
- **Comped (admin-granted) tiers vs real revenue (ABA-433)**: an admin can hand a user Pro/Business from the admin panel with no payment behind it, and every revenue figure used to count those users as paying — on prod all 7 active `business` subscriptions are grants and there are zero Stripe-backed paid subs, so the dashboard reported $139.93/mo of revenue that does not exist. **Detection needs no column and no migration**: `stripeSubscriptionId` is written by exactly ONE place in the codebase (the Stripe webhook, `SubscriptionsService.handleSubscriptionCreated`) while `AdminService.changeSubscriptionTier` sets only `tier` + `status`, so "active paid tier with no Stripe subscription id" identifies the manually-granted set exactly, **retroactively** for grants already made, and self-heals if the user later pays; `handleSubscriptionDeleted` nulls the id **and** resets `tier` to free, so a churned ex-payer can never read as a grant. `modules/admin/admin-comped.util.ts` holds `isComplimentarySub`/`isStripePaidSub` **and** the matching `COMPED_SUB_WHERE`/`PAID_SUB_WHERE` Prisma literals in one file on purpose — a counting query and an in-memory filter that disagree is the failure mode it guards. The predicates are **nullish-tolerant, not `=== null`**: an absent field (a hand-built row, a narrowed select) would otherwise read as *paying*, i.e. fail in the direction that inflates revenue; the `where` literals are deliberately NOT `as const`, since Prisma's generated types reject the readonly array a const assertion produces. Pure `toMrrRows(subs, predicate)` (`admin-metrics.util.ts`, beside `normalizeMrr`) builds both the paying and the comped set through ONE mapper so they stay comparable — do not add a second mapper. **Excluded everywhere**: investor metrics (`payingUsers`/`mrrUsd`/`arppuUsd`/`freeToPaidConversion` + per-language segment MRR + `trialToPaidConversion`, which must not count a post-trial grant as a conversion) plus new `MonetizationBlock.compedUsers`/`compedMrrUsd`; and both legacy calculations, `getAnalyticsOverview` and `getSubscriptionStats`, which switched from `count()` to rows through `normalizeMrr` — fixing the comped inflation, the stale hardcoded $9.99 Pro price (real price since ABA-320 is $4.99) and the ignored yearly interval in one move. `conversionRate` counts only real payers. **Users list**: `GET /admin/users` returns a computed `isComplimentary` per row and takes `billing=paying|comped` as a **separate axis that composes with `tier`** (`tier=business&billing=comped` = "business tiers I gave away"); the raw `stripeSubscriptionId` is collapsed to that one boolean before it leaves the service. Admin UI: `TierBadge` `complimentary` prop (amber "Comped" badge), Billing filter + explanatory panel on the user pages, Comped cards with `InfoHint` on `/metrics` and `/subscriptions`, an amber sub-line under the dashboard MRR card so the corrected number is explained rather than silently lower, and a note in `ChangeTierDialog` that granting a tier there charges nothing. **Known limitation**: someone who paid for Pro via Stripe and was then hand-upgraded to Business keeps a non-null `stripeSubscriptionId` and still counts as paying at the Business price — closing that needs an explicit flag. Tests: `admin-comped.util.spec.ts`, `admin.service.spec.ts` (billing-filter composition + no id leakage), `toMrrRows` + three comped scenarios in the existing metrics specs.
- **Acquisition dashboard (ABA-452)**: closes the read-surface gap left by ABA-436 — the `User.acquisition{Source,Location,Language,Plan}` columns (captured from the `?src=&loc=&lang=&plan=` link tags on the landing/blog/help sites, see the ABA-436 entry under **Mobile** below) had no admin UI. `AdminAnalyticsService.getAcquisitionBreakdown(days)` groups signups in a day window by each of the four columns via `Prisma.groupBy` (on the existing `@@index([acquisitionSource])`) — a `null` in any dimension is explicitly bucketed as `'direct'`, never dropped, since NULL there is a legitimate "unknown" (pre-migration user, native install, no query param). `GET /admin/analytics/acquisition?days=30` (clamped 1–365) on the class-level `JwtAuthGuard + AdminGuard`, no extra caching (a few lightweight indexed `groupBy`s). Admin page `apps/admin/src/app/acquisition/page.tsx` (+ `use-acquisition.ts` hook) — 4 stat cards + 4 breakdown tables (by source/location/language/plan) with a 7/30/90-day toggle, same `Stat`/`InfoHint` pattern as `/metrics`; sidebar nav entry "Acquisition" (Megaphone icon) sits under Investor Metrics. New shared types `AdminAcquisitionBreakdownResponse`/`Row` in `packages/shared-types/src/dto/admin.ts`. No schema change (columns/index already existed).
- **Investor metrics (ABA-340)**: `GET /admin/metrics/investor?months&weeks&activationDays` (`JwtAuthGuard + AdminGuard` inherited from the class; params clamped, defaults 6/12/3; Redis cache `admin:investor-metrics:{months}:{weeks}:{activationDays}` TTL 1h) returns the metrics an investor asks for during due diligence — all from existing tables, **no migration**, admin-only, no mobile changes. Blocks: weekly cohort **retention** triangle + pooled W1/W4/W8 headline; **activation** (logged first transaction within a 3-day window); **DAU/WAU/MAU** + stickiness; MoM **growth**; **honest MRR** (interval inferred from `currentPeriodEnd−currentPeriodStart` >45d ⇒ yearly÷12, USD-normalized with an `mrrApproximate` flag when any paying sub is non-USD); ARPU/ARPPU; free→paid + trial→paid **conversion**; **churn**; AI-COGS **gross margin**; and **PL/other** language segments. Correctness lives in the pure, unit-tested `modules/admin/admin-metrics.util.ts` (`now` always injected; `MRR_MONTHLY_USD` is a **4th copy** of the pricing table — keep in sync — and since ABA-433 it is the pricing source for the legacy dashboard/subscription MRR too); `AdminInvestorMetricsService` does the Prisma IO + assembly. **Definitions**: `active` = created ≥1 expense/income that **UTC** day (deduped by `userId`); trials **excluded** from MRR/`payingUsers`; `totalUsers` and the `freeToPaidConversion` denominator = **all registered users** (matches the signup universe feeding retention/growth/segments). **Churn**: detected by `status='canceled' && canceledAt >= monthStart` (count-based, tier-independent) because `SubscriptionsService.handleSubscriptionDeleted` resets `tier→free` + `stripePriceId→null` on cancel, discarding the pre-cancel tier — so **logo churn is exact but revenue churn is `null` in v1** (Phase-2 needs a pre-cancel-tier snapshot). `normalizeMrr` returns the **exact** monthly-equivalent sum; cents rounding happens at the service assembly boundary (no double-rounding; ARPU/ARPPU derive from the exact value first). Mobile-facing: none. Admin `/metrics` page (`apps/admin/src/app/metrics/page.tsx` + `use-investor-metrics.ts` hook + sidebar nav): headline cards, cohort-retention heatmap, growth bar chart, monetization cards, PL/Other segment toggle (the admin `formatPercent` prefixes `+` for deltas — the page uses a local plain formatter for non-delta stats). Every metric card + section header + the segment toggle carry a hover **tooltip** (`InfoHint` → shadcn `Tooltip`, aliased `UITooltip` to avoid the recharts `Tooltip` name clash; descriptions in a `HINTS` map, English to match the admin UI) explaining what each metric means (ABA-341). **Follow-ups**: revenue churn via pre-cancel tier snapshot; CAC/LTV:CAC (need marketing-spend, not in DB); referral K-factor; the legacy dashboard `getAnalyticsOverview()` MRR was fixed in ABA-433 (was hardcoded $9.99 and ignored yearly).
- **Caching layer** (ABA-151): `common/cache/cache.service.ts` — global `@Global()` ioredis wrapper; `delByPrefix` uses cursor-based `SCAN` (not blocking `KEYS`). `common/cache/redis-throttler-storage.ts` — `RedisThrottlerStorage` (`ThrottlerStorage` v5 interface, INCR+PEXPIRE NX+PTTL pipeline, `keyPrefix: 'throttle:'`); registered via `ThrottlerModule.forRootAsync` in `app.module.ts` so rate limits survive restarts. **`CacheService.incrementWindow(key, windowMs)`** (ABA-416) is the general-purpose version of the same INCR+PEXPIRE-NX pattern for any security-sensitive rate limit that needs to live outside `ThrottlerModule` — unlike every other `CacheService` method it does NOT swallow Redis errors (callers must fail closed, not silently allow unlimited attempts on a Redis outage). First consumer: `EncryptionService.checkRecoveryRateLimit` (`enc:recovery:{email}`, 5/15min) — replaced a plain in-memory `Map` that reset on every deploy, the same defect class as the still-open `auth.service.ts` rate-limit Maps (tech-debt `auth-rate-limit-in-memory-maps`, not yet fixed).
- **Fire-and-forget logging convention (ABA-461)**: any `void someCall().catch(...)` guarding a non-critical side effect (Family Feed events, anomaly checks, gamification, renewal/reminder pushes, wallet-currency backfills, community-price contributions, etc.) MUST log the rejection, never swallow it with `.catch(() => {})` — a silent failure there means the secondary effect never happened and there's no way to diagnose it. Use `logFireAndForget(this.logger, 'ClassName.whatItDoes')` from `common/utils/fire-and-forget.ts`, which returns a `.catch()` handler that calls `Logger.warn` (never `.error` — mirrors the mobile ABA-157 convention that a fire-and-forget failure is expected/transient, not exceptional). Every call site across the API (`subscriptions`, `expenses`, `incomes`, `wallet`, `budgets`, `purchase-requests`, `import-bank`, `import-wise`, `sync`, `referrals`, `telegram`, `community-prices`, `insights`, `shopping-list`, `trip-settle-up`, `debts`, `gamification`, `user-subscriptions`) was migrated to this helper; a new fire-and-forget call site should use it too, not a bare empty `.catch()`.
- **Daily cron pagination** (ABA-457): `common/utils/paginate.ts` — `paginateById(fetchPage, batchSize=500)`, a generic async generator that streams a Prisma `findMany`-shaped query in `id`-ordered batches (`orderBy:{id:'asc'}` + `cursor`/`skip`) instead of loading the whole eligible-row set in one call. Consumed by all 5 daily notification crons that used to `findMany` an entire table (tech-debt `daily-crons-full-table-scan`): `tracking-gap-reminder.cron.ts`, `debt-reminder.cron.ts` (both methods — the per-batch repayment lookup is scoped to that batch's debt ids, not the whole day's set), `expense-recurring.cron.ts` (see below — its recurringId-dedup switched from "first-seen wins" under a global `orderBy:date desc` to "latest-date wins" compared per row, since pagination is now by `id`), `subscription-renewal.cron.ts` (both crons), `trial-reminder.cron.ts`. New cron/batch-job code that scans an unbounded table should use this util rather than a bare `findMany`. Deliberately NOT changed: `NotificationsService.sendToUser`'s per-user `findUnique` re-fetch (N+1) — that method has many other callers and its signature was out of scope for this fix.
- **Debt reminders**: `modules/debts/debt-reminder.cron.ts` — `@Cron('0 9 * * *')` daily cron that finds active lent/borrowed debts due in 3 days (upcoming) or overdue yesterday, batches repayment queries to compute remaining balance, and calls `NotificationsService.sendToUser(..., 'debt_reminder')`. Respects `user.notifyDebtReminders` preference (default `true`). User preference toggle in `app/settings/notifications.tsx`. `NotificationType` includes `'debt_reminder'`; `NotificationPreferencesResponse` includes `debtReminders: boolean`.
- **Recurring expenses**: `modules/expenses/expense-recurring.cron.ts` — `@Cron('0 8 * * *')` daily cron that groups all active recurring expenses by `recurringId`, takes the most recent per series, computes `nextDue = lastDate + period` (weekly/monthly/yearly), and if `nextDue <= today` clones the expense with today's date (same `recurringId`) and sends a push notification. Respects `user.notifyRecurringExpenses` preference (default `true`). Mobile new-expense form has a "Repeat" toggle + period chip picker that sets `isRecurring: true`, assigns a `recurringId` UUID, and sets `recurringPeriod`. Expense detail screen shows a "Part of a recurring series" banner with period label and an inline "Stop Recurring" button (`PATCH /expenses/:id/stop-recurring` sets `isRecurring: false`; history preserved). User preference toggle in `app/settings/notifications.tsx`.
- **Tracking gap reminder** (ABA-292): `modules/gamification/tracking-gap-reminder.cron.ts` — `@Cron('0 10 * * *')` daily cron that queries `UserStreak` (type `daily_tracking`) grouped by userId, finds the most recent `lastActivityDate` across all accounts, and fires a `tracking_gap_reminder` push when `diffDays >= 3 && diffDays % 3 === 0` (sends on day 3, 6, 9…; skips days 4, 5, 7, 8). New users with no `UserStreak` row are skipped. Column `user.notifyTrackingGap Boolean @default(true)` (migration `20260627000000_add_tracking_gap_notification`); preference gate in `NotificationsService.sendToUser`; `GET/PATCH /users/me/notification-preferences` includes `trackingGap: boolean`. Mobile toggle "Tracking Reminder" in `app/settings/notifications.tsx`; `trackingReminder`/`trackingReminderDesc` i18n keys in all 9 locales.
- **Gamification tests (ABA-426)**: `gamification.service.spec.ts` (28 tests total with `streak.service.spec.ts`) covers every `checkAchievements()` switch branch via an in-memory `Map`-backed `userAchievement` double (mirrors real upsert/update/findUnique semantics) rather than per-call arg mocking — one scenario call exercises all 16 `ACHIEVEMENT_DEFINITIONS` at once. Pins the `update`-vs-`upsert` split (existing row → `update`; no row → `upsert`, including the no-write case when already complete at ≥ new progress) and the `referrals_*` `achievementAccountId` redirect to `user.defaultAccountId`. `budget_3months_no_exceed`'s "Simplified" 33%-cap-forever behavior (see the `checkAchievements` source comment) is asserted explicitly as a documented known limitation, not left as a silent surprise. `streak.service.spec.ts` covers `updateStreak`'s same-day/consecutive-day/streak-break day math and a timezone case showing one wall-clock instant yields opposite verdicts (continue vs. break) depending on `user.timezone`. `gamification.controller.ts` (pure delegation) and `tracking-gap-reminder.cron.ts` remain untested — no branching logic to pin yet.
- **Purchase requests** (ABA-298): `modules/purchase-requests/` — group purchase approval for shared accounts. Any member (including viewers) can propose a purchase; other members vote via app or bots; approval creates a planned expense. **Data model**: `purchase_requests` + `purchase_request_votes` tables (migrations `20260630000000_add_purchase_requests`); `Account.purchaseApprovalRule ApprovalRule @default(MAJORITY)` (migration `20260630000001_add_account_approval_rule`); `Expense.isPlanned Boolean @default(false)` + `@@index([accountId, isPlanned])` (migration `20260630000002_add_expense_is_planned`). **Approval rules**: `MAJORITY` (approve/reject count > 50% of total members); `UNANIMOUS` (all must approve; one reject kills it); `OWNER_ONLY` (only the account owner's vote counts). ABSTAIN excluded from denominator so non-participating members don't block decisions. Voting is upsert on `[requestId, userId]` so members can change their vote. **9 endpoints** (all `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`): `GET/POST /purchase-requests`, `GET/POST /purchase-requests/:id`, `POST /purchase-requests/:id/vote` (any member incl. viewers — intentionally NOT behind `ViewerBlockGuard`), `POST /purchase-requests/:id/convert` (ViewerBlockGuard — creates planned `Expense`, sets `plannedExpenseId`), `POST /purchase-requests/:id/mark-purchased` (ViewerBlockGuard — sets `status = PURCHASED`), `PATCH /purchase-requests/:id` (ABA-302 — edit PENDING request, ViewerBlockGuard, creator or owner only), `DELETE /purchase-requests/:id` (creator or owner only), `PATCH /purchase-requests/settings/approval-rule` (owner only, declared first to avoid `:id` shadow). Route order matters: `settings/approval-rule` → `pending-count` → `/` → `/:id` → `/:id/vote` → `/:id/convert` → `/:id/mark-purchased` → `PATCH /:id` → `DELETE /:id`. **Edit/Delete (ABA-302)**: creator or account owner can edit (title/amount/currency/merchant/description) or delete (= set status REJECTED + fire family-feed rejected event) a PENDING request. `[id].tsx` shows Edit + Delete buttons only for PENDING requests; Edit navigates to `new.tsx?editId=` which prefills fields and calls `updateRequest` on save; viewers cannot edit. **Notifications**: 4 push types — `purchase_request_created` (all members except creator), `purchase_request_voted` (creator), `purchase_request_approved` (all members), `purchase_request_rejected` (creator). User preference `notifyPurchaseRequests Boolean @default(true)` gated in `NotificationsService.sendToUser`. **Telegram bot**: `PurchaseRequestHandler` in `modules/telegram/handlers/purchase-request.handler.ts`, callbacks `pr_approve:{id}` / `pr_reject:{id}` (`:` separator). **WhatsApp bot**: same via `sendButtons`, callbacks `pr_approve--{id}` / `pr_reject--{id}` (`--` separator). **Planned expense**: `loadAllExpenses` in `expenseRepository.ts` filters `AND (is_planned IS NULL OR is_planned = 0)` so planned expenses never inflate spend totals. `expense/[id].tsx` shows a "Planned · Mark as purchased" banner when `isPlanned: true`; tap: optimistic `updateExpense({ isPlanned: false })` FIRST, then `markAsPurchased(linkedPR.id)`, catch → rollback + alert. Mobile: `purchaseRequestStore.ts` (server-only, not offline-first — voting requires cross-member consistency); 3 screens `app/purchase-requests/index.tsx` (3 tabs Active/Approved/History), `new.tsx`, `[id].tsx`; entry via Settings hub row with pending-count badge. 34 `purchaseRequests.*` i18n keys in all 9 locales. Tests: 27/27 (service 15, controller 12). **Phase 2 deferred**: expiry cron, comments thread, Family Feed integration, push image to bots.
- **Family Feed** (ABA-299): `modules/family-feed/` — Instagram-style activity feed for shared accounts. Events written fire-and-forget from `ExpensesService.create()`, `IncomesService.create()`, and `PurchaseRequestsService` (4 call sites: created/approved/purchased/cancelled→rejected); all use `@Optional() private readonly familyFeed?: FamilyFeedService` + `void this.familyFeed?.recordEvent(...).catch(() => {})` so existing tests pass without modification. `recordEvent()` no-ops when `account.type === 'personal'`. **Data model**: `family_feed_events` (`id`, `accountId`, `userId`, `type FeedEventType`, `entityId`, `metadata Json`, `createdAt`; `@@index([accountId, createdAt DESC])`); `feed_reactions` (`@@unique([eventId, userId])`); both `ON DELETE CASCADE`; migration `20260629214117_add_family_feed`. **Grouping**: `groupEvents(events, callerUserId)` groups `EXPENSE_ADDED`/`INCOME_ADDED` by `(userId, calendarDay)` into one `FeedGroup`; `PURCHASE_REQUEST_*` events always individual cards. `FeedGroup.eventIds` carries `event.entityId` (the expense/income PK) for deep-links — NOT `event.id` (the feed-event PK). **PR deduplication**: `groupEvents()` uses a `seenPrIds` Set (events are DESC) to keep only the most recent event per PR, so approve/reject after CREATED doesn't show two cards. **Live PR status**: `getFeed()` batch-queries `purchase_requests.status` from DB and passes a `prStatusMap` to `groupEvents()` — live status overrides the inferred type, so a PR cancelled years ago shows REJECTED even though its feed event was PURCHASE_REQUEST_CREATED. `FeedEventType` enum includes `PURCHASE_REQUEST_REJECTED` (migration `20260630100000_add_feed_event_rejected`). **3 endpoints** (class-level `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`, no `ViewerBlockGuard` — viewers CAN react): `GET /family-feed` (default limit 100, NaN-safe clamp), `POST /family-feed/:eventId/react` (upsert on `[eventId, userId]`, 6 emoji allowlist), `DELETE /family-feed/:eventId/react` (204). Mobile: `familyFeedStore.ts` (in-memory, server-only, optimistic react/removeReaction with `prev` rollback, `console.warn` on failure); `family-feed.api.ts`; `EmojiReactionBar.tsx` + `FeedGroupCard.tsx` in `src/components/feed/`; full screen `app/family-feed/index.tsx` (FlatList, `useFocusEffect` reload, pull-to-refresh); `FamilyFeedWidget` (last 3 groups, reloads on `currentAccountId` change — prevents stale cross-account UI). `'familyFeed'` added to `WIDGET_KEYS` after `'safeToSpend'`; widget hidden when `currentAccountType === 'personal'`. 15 `familyFeed.*` i18n keys in all 9 locales. Tests: 17/17 (service 14, controller 3). **Feed retention (ABA-303)**: configurable `familyFeedRetentionDays` stored in `system_config` (key-value table via `SystemConfigService.get/set`); `FamilyFeedService` daily `@Cron('0 3 * * *')` `cleanupOldEvents()` reads the setting and deletes `family_feed_events` older than that many days (default 5). Exposed in the admin panel **Settings → System Health card** via `GET/PATCH /admin/config`. **Vote rejection cleanup (ABA-303)**: when `PurchaseRequestsService.vote()` resolves to `REJECTED`, it fire-and-forgets `prisma.familyFeedEvent.deleteMany({ where: { entityId: id, accountId } })` so the rejected PR card disappears from the feed immediately. **Phase 2 deferred**: budget/goal/anomaly events in feed, reaction push notifications, comments thread, Slavic plural i18n variants.
- **Group Trip Wallet** (ABA-305): new `AccountType.trip` — a temporary shared account for travel groups (friends/couples on a trip), with multi-way expense splitting and a settle-up/debt-payment flow. **Data model**: `Account.tripStartDate/tripEndDate/tripStatus` (`TripStatus`: `active|settling|archived`); `AccountMember.paymentMethod` (`SettleMethod`: `blik|revolut|paypal|cash|other`) + `paymentHandle`; `Expense.paidByUserId` (defaults to creator, editable — "who actually paid"); new `TripExpenseShare` (`@@unique([expenseId, userId])`, `shareType ShareType` `equal|exact|percentage|shares`, `shareAmount`) and `SettleUpTransaction` (`fromUserId`, `toUserId`, `amount`, `status SettleStatus` `pending|confirmed`) tables (migrations `20260701183505_add_trip_wallet`, `20260702082444_add_trip_settle_up_notification_pref`). **Splitting**: `resolveShares(totalAmount, splitType, shares)` (`modules/expenses/trip-share-calculator.ts`) is a pure function handling all 4 split types with deterministic rounding-remainder assignment (last participant absorbs the residual cent); wired into `ExpensesService.create()`/`update()` — writes are strictly additive/no-op for non-trip expenses, and shares are always fully delete+recreated on edit, never partially patched. `paidByUserId` is set on BOTH the upsert's `create:` and `update:` branches (a real bug found in review: the `update:` branch initially omitted it, silently dropping payer edits on the offline-retry-upsert path). **Settle-up module**: `modules/trip-settle-up/` — `computeBalances()`/`simplifyDebts()` (`settle-up-calculator.ts`) are pure functions: the former nets each expense's `paidByUserId` (+amount) against each `TripExpenseShare.userId` (−amount) into per-person `netAmount` (positive = owed money, negative = owes money); the latter greedily matches largest-creditor↔largest-debtor to produce the *minimum* set of suggested transfers, not every pairwise debt. `TripSettleUpService.getBalances(accountId)` additionally nets `confirmed` `SettleUpTransaction` rows into the SAME adjusted balances array used for both the `balances` and `suggestedTransfers` response fields (a payment, once confirmed, must reduce the displayed debt — both fields derive from one adjusted array or they visibly disagree) and returns `pendingTransactions` (all `pending` rows) so the **receiver** of a payment — who never calls `pay` — can discover a real transaction id to confirm from their own device (the original design tracked ids only in local mobile state, which made the receiver's confirm flow unreachable cross-device; fixed by exposing this server-side). **3 endpoints** (`JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`, guard-derived `req.accountId`/`req.user.id` used throughout, never a raw `:id` param or client-supplied identity field — an IDOR in an early draft used the untrusted URL param): `GET /accounts/:id/settle-up`, `POST /accounts/:id/settle-up/pay` (`SettleUpPayDto` — local `class-validator` class, not the bare shared-types interface; validates `dto.fromUserId === req.user.id` and that the `{fromUserId,toUserId,amount}` triple matches a real `suggestedTransfers` entry within 0.01 tolerance — both checks run BEFORE any DB write, closing a forged-debt-record vulnerability found in review; generates a `revolut.me`/`paypal.me` deep-link with `encodeURIComponent`'d handle+amount, or BLIK "pay manually" instructions since BLIK has no cross-bank deep-link API; `AccountMemberPaymentInfoDto.paymentHandle` carries a `@Matches(/^[A-Za-z0-9+ ._-]{1,50}$/)` allowlist — `+`/space included specifically for BLIK phone-number handles), `PATCH /accounts/:id/settle-up/:txnId/confirm` (receiver-only, transaction looked up by `{id, accountId}` so a foreign-account id 404s before the receiver check ever runs — no info-disclosure gap between "not found" and "not yours"; **not** `TripArchivedGuard`-blocked, since an owner can force-archive with unconfirmed debts still outstanding and blocking confirm would strand them). **Lifecycle**: `TripSettleUpReminderCron` (`0 9 * * *`) flips `active→settling` when `tripEndDate` has passed and pushes a `trip_settle_up` notification (gated by `user.notifyTripSettleUp`, default `true`, via the same central `NotificationsService.sendToUser` per-type switch every other notification type uses — not a per-cron manual check); `settling→archived` is owner-only (`PATCH /accounts/:id/archive-trip`, requires zero `pending` `SettleUpTransaction` rows unless `force: true`) and makes the account permanently read-only via `TripArchivedGuard` (DI-resolved class ref, stacked alongside `ViewerBlockGuard`) on expense create/update/remove/bulk **and** `settle-up/pay` (paying is still allowed while merely `settling`, blocked once `archived`). **Invites**: reuses the existing `AccountInvitation` model/`acceptInvitation()` unchanged — only the mobile UX differs: a `budget://trip-invite/<8-char-hex>` deep link is captured pre-auth and auto-accepted post-registration via the SAME cold-start gate pattern (`!isInitializing && isAuthenticated && fontsLoaded`) already used for the `chat_mention` push deep-link (`app/_layout.tsx`) — the generic `Linking` handler has an explicit `trip-invite/` exclusion so it doesn't also fire a duplicate, wrong navigation. No native iOS/Android Universal/App Links config exists yet, so this is reachable today only via the custom scheme, not a real tapped `https://` link. **Mobile**: offline-first `tripExpenseShareRepository.ts` (mirrors `expense_category_splits`' soft-delete/timestamp conventions; `bulkInsertShares` wrapped in `withTransaction` for atomicity); `tripStore.ts` (server-only, mirrors `purchaseRequestStore`/`familyFeedStore` — settle-up needs cross-member consistency); `TripExpenseSplitPicker` component wired into `expense/new.tsx`/`ExpenseDetailsCard.tsx` (gated on `tripMembers.length > 0` to avoid mounting with an empty member list; edit-cancel reverts to a `persistedTripSplitType/Shares` mirror, not a blank/discarded-edit state; `validateTripSplit()` blocks submit on an invalid manual exact/percentage split rather than letting it fail silently server-side); `AccountSwitcher` shows a tappable trip-status badge (→ `settle-up.tsx`, active or settling) + collapsible "Past trips" section; `accountStore.canEdit()` returns `false` for `tripStatus === 'archived'` (client-side mirror of the server guard, avoids a doomed-optimistic-write/revert-to-pending loop). Entry points: `account/create.tsx` (trip as a 5th type → `/trip/new`), `account/[id].tsx` (Settle Up / Payment Settings / owner-only Archive Trip with unconfirmed-debts force-retry). 47 `trip.*` i18n keys across all 9 locales (grew organically past the original 26-key estimate — treat `en.ts`'s live `trip` object as the source of truth, not any historical plan doc; the 4 `archiveTrip*` keys briefly shipped English-only, backfilled to all 8 other locales in ABA-375). **Known gaps**: `syncPendingExpenses()`'s offline-retry payload still drops `shares`/`splitType`/`paidByUserId` on resend (pre-existing pattern shared with `items`/`splits`/`isRecurring`); `archiveTrip`/`updatePaymentInfo` mobile actions patch in-memory state only, relying on the next full server reload to persist locally.
- **Anomaly alerts** (ABA-242): `modules/anomaly/` — rule-based proactive alerts (no LLM cost), triggered **on expense write**, not by cron. `AnomalyService.checkExpense(accountId, userId, expenseId)` is fired-and-forgotten from `ExpensesService.create` (covers app, voice, OCR, all three bots, mobile sync); `checkExpenseBatch` fires after both import commits (wise + bank; duplicate detector skipped — preview already content-dedups). Subscription auto-charge cron NOT hooked. 4 detectors: `duplicate_charge` (ABA-245: same **payee** + amount + currency within ±1 day, same-import-batch pairs excluded — payee = `merchant`, or `description` when no merchant is set, so a duplicated expense without a merchant is still caught; candidates queried by amount/currency/date, payee label matched in JS), `price_increase` (active `UserSubscription` matched by normalized name OR `recurringId` series, strictly >10% up, same currency), `recurring_suggestion` (3+ same-amount charges, gaps 25–35d monthly / 6–8d weekly, untracked merchant, fires once EVER per merchant), `category_spike` (current month ≥30% above avg of ≥2 prev months, **per currency**, no budget required — replaced the deleted `BudgetAlertService.checkSpendingAnomalies` and its `thresholdPercentage: -1` hack). Feed table `anomaly_alerts` (migration `20260610000000_add_anomaly_alerts`); `@@unique([accountId, dedupKey])` IS the dedup — insert + catch P2002 = already alerted. Dedup keys: `dup:{expenseId}`, `price:{merchantNorm|recurringId|subId}:{YYYY-MM}`, `spike:{categoryId}:{YYYY-MM}`, `recur:{merchantNorm}`, `merge:{sortedId1}:{sortedId2}` (ABA-296 `possible_merge`). No text in DB — only `params` Json; mobile renders from `alerts.*` i18n, push localized via `notification-i18n.ts` (9 langs; category_spike reuses `anomalyTitle/Body`). Push: existing `spending_anomaly` type, now gated by new `user.notifyAnomalyAlerts` (default true, in notification-preferences API + settings toggle), capped 3/day/account (`pushSent` count, known benign read-then-act race); push `data` uses `anomalyType` key (`type` would be overwritten by NotificationsService). Endpoints: `GET /alerts?unread=`, `PATCH /alerts/read-all` (declared BEFORE `:id` routes — ABA-166), `PATCH /alerts/:id/read`, `DELETE /alerts/:id` (dismiss); writes carry `ViewerBlockGuard` (read state is account-wide). Mobile: bell + unread badge in home hero header → `app/alerts/index.tsx` (tap → `expense/[id]` or prefilled `/subscriptions/new`; write affordances `canEdit`-gated). **ABA-247**: an alert's `expenseId` is the **server PK**, so `expense/[id].tsx` resolves it by `id` **OR `serverId` OR `clientId` OR `localId`** — on a device the local row id is the clientId and the server PK lives in `serverId`, so matching only `id` showed "Expense not found". `alertStore` (in-memory only, no SQLite); the badge refreshes via `useFocusEffect` on the home tab (+ one delayed re-load ~2.5s later, since the server creates the alert asynchronously after an expense is saved) so it updates without a manual pull-to-refresh (ABA-246). Push deep-link `spending_anomaly` → `/alerts`. **Alert deep-link resolution + resolve-on-delete cleanup (ABA-339)**: an anomaly alert deep-links to an expense by its **server PK**, but a locally-created row is keyed by its clientId (`id === localId`) and learns its server PK only in `serverId`. **Root cause the user hit** ("расход не открывался с алертов"): `expenseStore.addExpense` and `expenseSync.syncPendingExpenses` **discarded** the `api.createExpense` response, so a freshly-created expense had `serverId === undefined` until the next full pull happened to backfill it (`expenseSync.ts:316` sets `serverId: se.id`) — so the just-created expense that triggered a `duplicate_charge` alert couldn't be resolved from that alert and dead-ended on "Expense not found". Fix: both create paths now capture `created.id` → set `serverId` in-memory + persist via new `setExpenseServerId(localId, serverPk)` (`expenseRepository.ts`, targeted `UPDATE expenses SET server_id` — no `updated_at`/`sync_status` side effects). `app/alerts/index.tsx` `handlePress` → `openAlertTargets` resolves the target(s) against the live store (4-way `id`/`serverId`/`clientId`/`localId` match); **if not resolvable it forces ONE `loadExpenses({force:true})` pull (backfills serverId) and retries** (inline per-card `ActivityIndicator` via `resolvingId`), and only then, if still missing, concludes the pair was already resolved → shows `alerts.alreadyResolved{Title,Body}` (9 locales) + dismisses. This pull-and-retry (NOT the earlier naive `expenses.length>0`-gated instant-dismiss, which would have wrongly hidden alerts whose expense merely lacked serverId) is what rescues existing stuck alerts. **Resolve-on-delete cleanup**: once the duplicate is deleted/merged the alert is stale, so `AnomalyService.dismissForExpense(accountId, expenseId)` sets `dismissedAt` on any active alert matching the top-level `expenseId` column **OR** `params.otherExpenseId` (deleting EITHER side of the pair clears it), fired-and-forgotten (`void`, never throws) from `ExpensesService.remove`/`mergeExpenses`/`reconcileNotificationStub`.
- **Safe-to-Spend engine (ABA-293)**: `modules/insights/safe-to-spend.service.ts` — single source of truth for the home hero number (#1) and `check_affordability` AI function (#3). `GET /insights/safe-to-spend` (JwtAuth + AccountContext, no tier guard — free). Inputs: wallet summary (`WalletService.getSummary`), upcoming subscription charges (`userSubscription` where `isActive`, projected forward within horizon), upcoming recurring expenses (mirrors `expense-recurring.cron.ts` grouping: latest per `recurringId`, next due date), on-track goal contributions (linear pace to deadline), inferred monthly income (income history 90d, 25–35d cadence heuristic — same detector as anomaly `recurring_suggestion`). FX via `getRatesSafe`/`convertAmount` with `fxApproximate` flag (same pattern as `ai-tools.service.ts`). Formula: `safeToSpendToday = max(0, (walletBalance + expectedIncome − obligations − buffer) / daysRemaining)`; `buffer = 0` (v1). Horizon = min(end-of-month, nextExpectedIncomeDate); clamps to 1 day minimum. Redis cache `sts:{accountId}:{baseCurrency}` TTL 300s. Pure `computeSafeToSpend(inputs)` formula helper is a **duplicated pair, same convention as `financial-month.ts`/`receipt-category-split.ts`**: the service imports its own canonical copy at `apps/api/src/modules/insights/safe-to-spend.util.ts`, and a mirror in `packages/shared-utils` is what the mobile offline fallback (`useSafeToSpend.ts`) actually imports — it is **not** a single shared function, and the API never imports `@budget/shared-utils` at runtime (no build step there — see the `check-no-shared-utils-runtime-import.sh` deploy guard under Production). `safe-to-spend.service.spec.ts` tests the shared-utils copy, not the file the service calls at runtime — harmless only because the two are kept identical by hand; this wording ("lives in shared-utils") was previously stale here and nearly caused a repeat of that same outage. **NO migration** — computable entirely from existing tables. `SafeToSpendResponse` + `SafeToSpendBreakdown` + `AffordabilityVerdict` DTOs in `packages/shared-types/src/dto/insights.ts`. Mobile: `useSafeToSpend` hook (`src/features/insights/useSafeToSpend.ts`) + `insightsStore.loadSafeToSpend()` (MMKV-cached) + home hero number (tap → breakdown bottom-sheet) + `'safeToSpend'` `WidgetKey`/widget + `check_affordability` `ActionResultCard` chip (yes/no + `reasonCode`).
- **Financial Wrapped (ABA-336)**: `modules/insights/wrapped.service.ts` (thin IO wrapper) + pure `wrapped.util.ts` `assembleWrapped` (unit-tested, mirrors `safe-to-spend.util.ts`) — a Spotify-Wrapped-style year-in-review assembled ENTIRELY from existing data (**NO migration**). `GET /insights/wrapped?year=YYYY` (JwtAuth + AccountContext, **no tier guard — FREE** for shareability, same precedent as safe-to-spend; clamps year to `[2000, currentYear]`). `WrappedResponse` = ordered discriminated-union `WrappedCard[]` (`packages/shared-types/src/dto/insights.ts`) — only cards WITH data are included; `hasEnoughData:false` (+ empty `cards`) below 5 tracked rows or on tier-2 full-encryption. Cards: `intro`, `total_tracked`, `top_merchant` (visits, tie-break spend), `biggest_month`, `top_category`, `category_mix`, `receipts_scanned` (`source in ('ocr','notification')`), `savings` (net + rate + savedVsLastYear — pulls prior year in the same query), `personal_inflation` (reuses `PriceHistoryService.getPriceHistory('12m').inflationIndex` — rolling-12m proxy, only for current/prev year; **`PriceHistoryService` is now exported from `PriceHistoryModule`**), `streak` (`StreakService.getStreak`). All amounts FX-converted to `user.currencyCode` via `getRatesSafe`/`convertAmount` (unknown rate → amount excluded from sum, `fxApproximate` flag). Redis cache `wrapped:{accountId}:{baseCurrency}:{year}` TTL 3600s. `InsightsModule` imports `PriceHistoryModule` + `GamificationModule`. **Mobile**: `api.getWrapped(year)` (`analytics.api.ts`), `useWrapped` hook (`src/features/insights/`), full-screen swipeable gradient card-deck `app/wrapped/index.tsx` (**zero new native modules** — paging `ScrollView` + `expo-linear-gradient`; progress dots; text share via `Share.share({message})` composed from `wrapped.share*` i18n + amount-hiding toggle), route `fullScreenModal` in `_layout.tsx`, entry banner in the Analytics tab. 41 `wrapped.*` i18n keys × 9 locales. **Shareable IMAGE (M3)**: `src/components/wrapped/WrappedShareCard.tsx` is a thin wrapper (gradient + `wrapped-<year>.png` file prefix + payload mapping) over the generic `src/components/share/ShareImageCard.tsx` (ABA-353, extracted from the earlier byte-faithful duplicate with `InflationShieldShareCard` — see **Share image mechanism** below). `buildSharePayload` mirrors `buildShareMessage` + reuses `money()` so `hideAmounts` masking is identical; no new i18n keys. **Deferred**: M4 seasonal home widget.
- **Inflation Shield (ABA-346)**: `modules/insights/inflation-shield.util.ts` (pure, unit-tested) + `inflation-shield.service.ts` — forecasts each tracked product's price from receipt history and recommends what to **stock up on now** before it rises (product, quantity, store, estimated saving), plus how much the shield has **saved so far**. `GET /insights/inflation-shield` (JwtAuth + AccountContext, **no tier guard — FREE**, precedent safe-to-spend/wrapped); Redis cache `shield:{accountId}:{baseCurrency}` TTL 1h; **no LLM cost** (deterministic). **Engine**: `forecastProductTrend` = least-squares regression over a `SHIELD_FORECAST_LOOKBACK_WEEKS` (12) window with a `minSpanDays` (14) guard + a `hasSignal` flag (replaced an initial two-fixed-window momentum that killed recall on infrequent stockpileable goods); `estimateCadenceDays`; `isStockpileable` (conservative — short-cadence perishables like milk excluded, **silent on unknown cadence**, `SHIELD_MIN_CADENCE_DAYS=14`); `recommendStockUp` (quantity = consumption/wk × min(horizon, `SHIELD_MAX_STOCK_WEEKS=8`) capped at `SHIELD_MAX_UNITS=12`; **`projectedSaving` is HALVED** — linear-ramp avoided-cost model `(projectedPrice−currentPrice)/2 × qty`, not the full end-gap); `assembleShield` (keeps rising ≥ `SHIELD_MIN_MONTHLY_RISE_PCT=5`%/mo + stockpileable + ≥3 points, FX-converts every amount to display currency with `fxApproximate`, basket forecast weights only `hasSignal` products, `hasEnoughData`). All env-tunable via `SHIELD_*`; defaults in `SHIELD_DEFAULTS`. `PriceHistoryService.getProductTrends(accountId)` (public, reuses private `fetchRows`) supplies per-product series; `SafeToSpendService.compute` supplies each item's `affordableToday`. DTOs `ShieldItem`/`InflationShieldResponse` in `packages/shared-types/src/dto/insights.ts`. Data source is **personal-only** in v1 — the util accepts an optional community `store`/`currentBestPrice` but community-boost (region → `CommunityPriceService` → cheapest store) is deferred (community reads kill-switched off pending anti-Sybil validation). **Realized-savings tracking**: migration `inflation_shield_recommendations` (+ `ShieldStatus` enum `active|acted|expired`; authored **DB-free** via `prisma migrate diff` since this repo runs migrations against prod via the deploy `migrator`, no local DB). Prisma-only leaf `InflationShieldTrackingService` in its own tiny module imported by BOTH `InsightsModule` and `ExpensesModule` (leaf keeps it **cycle-free**): `recordRecommendations` (idempotent per `(accountId, canonicalName, periodMonth)` — first-of-month snapshot wins, create + catch-P2002 OUTSIDE any `$transaction`), `reconcilePurchase` (on a new expense, marks a matching `active` rec `acted` + credits proportional `projectedSaving`; matches on `canonicalName`(exact) + quantity(≥½) + **calendar-day** date gate `expense.date >= floor(recommendedAt)` so same-day purchases count — **never** a cross-currency price compare; amounts stored in base currency), `getActedRecommendations`. `getShield` fire-and-forgets `recordRecommendations` (before `cache.set`) and returns a real FX-summed `savedSoFar` (`fxApproximate` if any acted rec needed conversion). `ExpensesService.create`'s post-create block fire-and-forgets `reconcilePurchase` (`@Optional()` inject, mirrors `communityPrices`/`familyFeed`) AND `invalidateChatCache` busts `shield:` + `chat:get_inflation_shield:` so a new expense never serves a stale shield. **AI chat**: `get_inflation_shield` (13th→14th chat function; `ChatActionType`, `AiToolsService` schema/dispatcher/`executeGetInflationShield` injecting `InflationShieldService`, **READ action** — NOT in `isWriteAction`, routed through the cached/narrated read path; prompt: numbers verbatim, savings framed as an **ESTIMATE**, all amounts in `baseCurrency` not per-item `currencyOriginal`); mobile `ShieldResult` `ActionResultCard` + `chat.actionInflationShield`. **Mobile**: `api.getInflationShield`, MMKV-cached server-only `inflationShieldStore` (instant paint, keeps stale on error, no upgrade gate — free), `useInflationShield` hook, `InflationShieldWidget` (new `'inflationShield'` `WidgetKey` after `safeToSpend` in `WIDGET_KEYS`; case in `HomeWidgetSwitch`; hides when no data; the `WidgetKey` also needs an entry in `settings/widgets.tsx`'s `Record<WidgetKey,string>` label map — exhaustiveness), screen `app/inflation-shield/index.tsx` (hero "saved ~X estimated", basket forecast, buy-ahead cards with store + affordable badge, pull-to-refresh, empty state; header via `_layout.tsx` Pattern A). **Share IMAGE**: `src/components/insights/InflationShieldShareCard.tsx` is a thin wrapper (green gradient + `inflation-shield-<fileTag>.png` prefix) over the generic `src/components/share/ShareImageCard.tsx` (ABA-353 — see **Share image mechanism** below; `ShieldSharePayload` already matches the generic `fileTag`/`title`/`lines`/`footer` shape, so no payload mapping needed); pure unit-tested `features/insights/shieldShare.ts` builders (`buildShieldSharePayload`/`buildShieldShareMessage`, **hide-amounts masks money but keeps inflation %% visible** — a % is a rate, not a personal amount); screen Share button + hide-amounts toggle with a `Share.share` text fallback on web/failure. `inflationShield.*` + `inflationShield.share*` i18n × 9 locales. Built as 5 sequenced plans (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-1{5,6}-inflation-shield-*.md`); spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-15-inflation-shield-design.md`. **Proactive push (ABA-371)**: `inflation-shield-notify.cron.ts` — daily `@Cron('0 11 * * *')` (staggered 1h after `shopping-reminder.cron.ts`) per-account job that resolves the account owner's userId/currencyCode (owner-lookup pattern from `user-subscriptions` auto-charge cron), calls the same cached `getShield()`, and pushes the single highest-`projectedSaving` item to every account member with a push token (each gated by their own `notifyInflationShield` preference, default `true`). Dedup via `InsightNotificationLedger` (since ABA-455 a thin subclass of the same generic `NotificationDedupLedger` as `ShoppingNotificationLedger` — see **Push de-duplication** under Smart Shopping List below) + `InsightNotificationLog` table (`insight_notification_log`, structural mirror of `shopping_notification_log`, insert-and-catch-P2002, `@@unique([accountId, dedupKey])`) keyed `shield:{canonicalName}:{YYYY-MM}` — at most one push per account per product per calendar month; daily `0 3 * * *` cleanup of rows >90 days. Push type `'inflation_shield'` gated in `NotificationsService` like every other type; `GET/PATCH /users/me/notification-preferences` carries `inflationShield: boolean`; `notification-i18n.ts` has `inflationShieldTitle`/`inflationShieldBody` × 9 langs (body reports product + rounded forecast `monthlyChangePct` only — never the halved `projectedSaving` money estimate, to avoid overpromising). Mobile: `settings/notifications.tsx` "Stock-up alerts" toggle wired into the master enable-all/disable-all switch; push deep-link → existing `/inflation-shield` screen. **Follow-ups**: community-boost, true realized savings + expiry cron, aliased-product reconciliation, merging the push with `shopping_deal` if user feedback says they compete for attention.
- **Fat Finder audit currency (ABA-386)**: `modules/insights/fat-finder.service.ts` — `generateReport(accountId, language?, forceRegenerate?, userId?, month?, year?, baseCurrency = 'USD')`. The report is labelled AND computed in the **caller's display currency** (`insights.controller.ts` passes `req.user.currencyCode || 'USD'`, same as safe-to-spend/wrapped/inflation-shield); it is **never** inferred from an expense row. The old `const currencyCode = expenses[0].currencyCode` took the currency of whichever row was *newest* (`orderBy date desc`), so a PLN account with one small USD charge produced a whole audit "in dollars" with PLN+EUR+USD summed blind — do not reintroduce that. Every expense is FX-converted (`getRatesSafe`/`convertAmount`, mirroring `SafeToSpendService`) into a single `analysis[]` array **before** any aggregation, so subscriptions/large-one-offs/category-trends/totals all read one currency; rates are only fetched when the account actually mixes currencies. A row with an unknown rate (or every non-base row when the provider is down) is **excluded, not mislabelled**, setting `fxApproximate` (wrapped/safe-to-spend convention); `fxConverted` marks that some amount was converted. Both flags ride on the response (optional fields on `FatFinderResponse`) — the *user-facing* "converted figures are approximate" wording is produced by the LLM from a prompt instruction, so there are **no new i18n keys**. The prompt states every amount is already in the report currency and must be written in it. **Cache**: the DB row is keyed `(accountId, periodStart, periodEnd)` only, so a cached report whose `currencyCode !== baseCurrency` is **regenerated instead of served** — that is what lets already-stored wrong-currency rows self-heal instead of persisting for their 30-day TTL. 
- **Spending Story audit currency + shared FX helpers (ABA-387)**: `story.service.ts` had the same defect as ABA-386 — `currentExpenses[0]?.currencyCode || budgets[0]?.currencyCode || 'USD'`, and since that query is `orderBy: { amount: 'desc' }` the **largest single charge** decided the currency of the whole narrative. Now `getSpendingStory(..., baseCurrency = 'USD')` takes the caller's display currency from the controller (`req.user.currencyCode`) and every amount is converted before aggregation: current + previous expenses, incomes, and **both budget figures** (`BudgetsService.getProgress` reports in the *budget's* currency because it filters expenses by it — `percentUsed` is a ratio and must NOT be converted). The top-5 expense list is re-sorted by the **converted** amount (native `amount desc` is meaningless across currencies). Unknown-rate amounts are excluded + `fxApproximate`, mirroring fat-finder. New nullable `SpendingStory.currencyCode` (migration `20260803180000_add_spending_story_currency`) is part of the cache-validity check alongside the existing `periodLabel` match — a story cached in another currency (or a pre-migration `null`) is regenerated, at the cost of one extra 3-request generation per period. **Shared helpers**: `common/utils/fx.ts` (`getRatesSafe(provider, base)` + pure `convertAmount(amount, from, base, rates)`) is the canonical home of the `amount_in_base = amount / rates[from]` formula. Originally only fat-finder and story imported it while `ai-tools.service.ts`, `safe-to-spend.service.ts`, `wrapped.service.ts`, `inflation-shield.service.ts` and `trip-settle-up.service.ts` each carried their own private copy (tech-debt `fx-rate-conversion-helper-quintupled`); all five have since been migrated onto the shared util and their private copies deleted — `ai-tools.service.ts` keeps a thin `private getRatesSafe(base?: string)` wrapper only to preserve its own optional-base short-circuit, but it delegates to the shared implementation. **New FX-consuming code must import the util, not add a private copy.**
- **Auto-capture spend gate (ABA-387)**: the Android capture allow-list is per **app**, not per **notification**, so any push from an allow-listed bank app containing a decimal number + a currency token became an expense. That is how a Revolut-style crypto price alert ("…up 5.32% in the past 2 hours. It's now $59,123.45") was booked as **5.32 USD** with merchant "The Past 2 Hours. It's Now" (the percentage read as the amount, `$` as the currency, and the German/Dutch `in <merchant>` connector as the payee) — 8 such rows reached prod in one account. `notificationParser/generic.ts` now exports `maskPercentages` (a percentage is never an amount; masking applies to the **amount** lookup only — currency/merchant still read the original text), `hasSpendIntent`/`isDeclined`/`looksLikeSpendNotification` (a push must carry spend wording in one of the 9 supported languages — deliberately including the same keywords the per-bank templates anchor on, so gating never rejects a push a template was written for — **or** an explicit debit sign on the amount; declined/rejected/insufficient-funds is always rejected), and `extractAmountSigned`. `notificationParser/index.ts` applies the gate to **BOTH** parse paths and masks percentages before the template amount match: six of the eleven templates (Pekao, Santander, Alior, BNP, Credit Agricole, Nest) use the bare `AMOUNT_PATTERN_PL`, which matches any decimal in the text, so a loan ad's "RRSO 8,99%" was equally capturable. Two recall bugs fixed in the same pass: `SYMBOL_TO_ISO` was missing the ISO code **`EUR`** (only `€`), so every "12,34 EUR" push from a non-PL bank was dropped as "no currency detected"; and `CURRENCY_RE` now word-bounds ASCII codes so `Br` no longer matches the "br" inside "brutto" → BYN (the boundary-less regex is still why NOK/SEK/RON stay out of `DETECTABLE_UNSUPPORTED`). Tests: `notificationParser/__tests__/{generic,parseNotification}.test.ts` carry the real production strings.
- **Share image mechanism (ABA-353)**: `src/components/share/ShareImageCard.tsx` — the shared hidden-`react-native-webview` → HTML/canvas (1080×1920 story card) → `canvas.toDataURL('image/png')` → `expo-file-system` write → `expo-sharing` `shareAsync` mechanism, extracted from what was a ~540-line byte-faithful duplicate between `WrappedShareCard` and `InflationShieldShareCard` (each previously re-implemented the same webview bridge, 8s timeout, single-in-flight guard, and never-throws error handling). `ShareImageCard` takes `renderFnName` (the `window.<name>` bridge the injected JS calls — must be unique per mounted card), `gradientFrom`/`gradientTo`, and `fileNamePrefix` as props, plus a `ShareCardPayload { fileTag, title, lines, footer }` at `share()`-time; `forwardRef` `share(payload): Promise<boolean>`, never throws. `ShareImageCard.web.tsx` is the shared no-op (keeps `react-native-webview`/`expo-file-system` out of the web bundle). `WrappedShareCard`/`InflationShieldShareCard` are now thin wrappers owning only their gradient/file-prefix/payload-shape; their own `.web.tsx` no-ops are unchanged (kept standalone — each is a required, separately-typed Metro platform-extension file, not part of the duplication). A future third shareable-card feature should add a new thin wrapper over `ShareImageCard`, not a fourth copy of the mechanism. No component-level test was added (this codebase's WebView-bridge components, e.g. `ExpenseMapView`, only unit-test their pure helper functions — there's no `react-test-renderer`/`@testing-library/react-native` dependency to render `ShareImageCard` itself).
- **Bank-notification auto-capture (ABA-294)**: Android-only native `NotificationListenerService` (`apps/mobile/android/…/notifications/BankNotificationListenerService.kt`) extending `android.service.notification.NotificationListenerService`. Implemented as a **legacy Old-Arch `ReactContextBaseJavaModule`** (no TurboModule/codegen — avoids Windows MAX_PATH issue identical to keyboard-controller). `NotificationCaptureModule.kt` + `NotificationCapturePackage.kt` registered manually in `MainApplication.kt:getPackages()` (documented seam). `MainActivity.kt` untouched. AndroidManifest: service block with `BIND_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SERVICE` permission + intent-filter. JS bridge: `notificationCapture.ts` subscribes to `onBankNotification` DeviceEventEmitter event (payload: `packageName`, `title`, `text`, `postedAt`). Allow-list-first filter; on-device parsing only (text never leaves the device). **New-Arch event emission (ABA-297)**: MUST use `reactContext.emitDeviceEvent(EVENT_NAME, params)` — `getJSModule(DeviceEventManagerModule.RCTDeviceEventEmitter::class.java).emit()` is silently swallowed in RN 0.81 New Architecture (caught by `catch(_: Exception){}` with no log, so events never reach JS). `BankNotificationListenerService.emitEvent` accepts `ReactApplicationContext?`; when null/not-ready, stores the event in `pendingEvent: WritableMap?` (one-slot volatile queue) and `NotificationCaptureModule.init` flushes it via `runOnJSQueueThread`. **Subscription timing**: `subscribeToCapture()` in `_layout.tsx` uses an empty-deps `useEffect` (runs on mount) — NOT gated on `isAuthenticated`/`fontsLoaded`/`isInitializing`; gating causes a startup race where Kotlin emits before JS registers its listener. The Kotlin gate (SharedPreferences flag) is the source of truth. **PKO BP template**: `amountRegex` must include `Obci[ąa][żz]enie(?:\s+karty?)?` — PKO BP card debits use "Obciążenie kartą", not "Płatność" (ABA-297 fix, `templates.pl.ts`). **externalRef**: `expenseStore.addExpense` passes `externalRef` to `api.createExpense` so the server `@@unique([accountId, externalRef])` can deduplicate across devices. **Multi-country (ABA-295)**: allow-list expanded to ~43 verified bank packages across 8 European markets (PL/DE/AT/ES/FR/NL/UA/RU/BY — kept in sync between the Kotlin `BANK_PACKAGES` set and JS `BANK_PACKAGES_DISPLAY`, which carries a `country` field; opt-in screen groups banks by country). JS pipeline: `notificationParser/index.ts` tries the per-bank PL template (`templates.pl.ts`) first, then falls back to a **generic country-agnostic parser** (`notificationParser/generic.ts`: amount in EU comma-decimal + Anglo dot-decimal; currency by symbol/ISO mapped to the supported `Currency` union — unsupported (CHF/CZK/SEK…) returns null + `console.warn`, never a wrong-currency expense; merchant via language-aware connectors `bei`/`chez`/`à`/`en`/`bij`/`at`/`в`/`у`/`w`). `normalizeMerchantWithPLOverride` applies the PL canonical brand map on top of the generic `normalizeMerchant`, so a PL brand in a non-PL bank's push still canonicalizes; category suggestion stays PL-only (`suggestCategoryFromMerchantPL`), non-PL merchants land uncategorized and `merchantRulesStore` learns over time. **CHF not yet in `Currency`** — Swiss captures are skipped until `'CHF'` is added to `primitives.ts`/`SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES`. Then: `merchantRulesStore` → dedup check (`externalRef = 'notif:<sha256(pkg|amt|merchant|date).slice(0,16)>'` against `@@unique([accountId, externalRef])`) → `expenseStore.addExpense({ source: 'notification', externalRef, … })` offline-first. Failures logged `console.warn`, never `console.error`. UX feedback: local toast "Captured X · Merchant — tap to review" deep-linking to `expense/[id]`. Opt-in screen `app/settings/auto-capture.tsx` (Android-only; linked from import hub). iOS/web: `NotificationCapture.ios.ts` no-op stub (`isPermissionGranted()=false`). `ExpenseSource` += `'notification'` (additive plain-string column, no migration). Play Store note: `BIND_NOTIFICATION_LISTENER_SERVICE` is a sensitive permission requiring justification; may delay review.
- **Notification-capture dedup (ABA-296)**: two-tier cross-source reconciliation for `source:'notification'` stubs. Shared predicate: `expensePayee()` (merchant || description, lowercased/trimmed) and `DUP_DAY_MS` exported from `anomaly.service.ts` — single source of truth shared with `duplicate_charge` detector. **Tier 1 auto-dedup (same currency, `P`):** Case A = `ExpensesService.reconcileNotificationStub` — when a non-`notification` expense is created (`isNew`, `source !== 'notification'`), soft-deletes a matching `source:'notification'` stub (same amount/currency/date ±1d/payee); runs BEFORE the fire-and-forget `checkExpense` so the suppressed stub is never a `duplicate_charge` candidate; only notification stubs are deletion candidates — two genuine non-notification expenses are never deleted. Case B = `captureService` `contentMatch.ts` — before `expenseStore.addExpense`, content-matches the parsed push against local `expenseStore.expenses` via predicate `P`; skips the create entirely if a match exists (placing the skip on the client avoids client/server row divergence). `@@index([accountId, source])` (migration `add_expense_source_index`) keeps the stub lookup cheap. **Tier 2 suggest-merge (cross-currency, `Q`):** `P` and `Q` are mutually exclusive — `P` requires same currency, `Q` requires different currency, so a pair is auto-deduped XOR suggested, never both. `AnomalyService.detectPossibleMerge` fires after `detectDuplicateCharge` from `checkExpense`: finds a `Q`-matching expense (same payee + date ±1d, currency differs), inserts a `possible_merge` alert (dedupKey `merge:{sortedId1}:{sortedId2}` is order-independent → once per pair ever). `ImportBankService.flagPossibleMerges` adds a pass after `flagContentDuplicates` in `buildPreviewResponse`, marking `possibleMerge: true` / `mergeCandidateIds` on eligible rows without changing import/skip counts — a `possibleMerge` row stays importable by default. `POST /expenses/merge` (ViewerBlockGuard, clientId resolution via `OR:[{id},{clientId}]`) merges two expenses: gap-fill carried fields, tag union, project carry via `project_expenses` join, soft-delete secondary + `syncVersion` bump on both rows (propagates via standard sync pull). Mobile: `app/expense/merge.tsx` — survivor picker (default: import/settlement-currency row) + field choices; entry via `possible_merge` alert tap (`aId`/`bId` params) or import-preview row; `expenseStore.mergeExpenses` (optimistic + offline-first, fire-and-forget server call with `console.warn` on failure); `api.mergeExpenses` in `expenses.api.ts`. `possible_merge` is a plain string in `anomaly_alerts.type` (no migration); `AnomalyAlertType` += `'possible_merge'`; new `MergeExpensesDto`. i18n: `alerts.mergeTitle/mergeBody`, `expenses.merge.*`, `bankImport.possibleMerge/mergeWithExisting/importAsNew` (all 9 locales). Tier 2 never auto-acts — only `POST /expenses/merge` mutates.
