- **Personal Inflation Index (ABA-307)**: `modules/price-history/` — tracks price history per product per store from OCR receipt line items and computes a personal Laspeyres inflation index. **Data model**: `expense_items.canonical_name TEXT` (populated by OCR, NULL for manual items; migration `20260702160001_add_expense_item_canonical_name`); `product_aliases` table (`@@unique([accountId, rawName])`, stores user overrides; migration `20260702160002_add_product_aliases`). **OCR extension**: `ReceiptItem.canonicalName` added to prompt — per `ocr.service.ts`'s `canonicalName rules` block, the model KEEPS per-unit weight/volume, fat/alcohol percentage, and flavour/variant descriptors, and STRIPS only pack-quantity multipliers, product codes, and PLU numbers: "MLEKO 3,2% ŁACIATE 1L 6SZT" → "Mleko Łaciate 3,2% 1L" (size is never stripped — do not assume canonicalName is size-agnostic) + fallback `buildCanonicalNameFallback(description)` (up to 3 meaningful tokens ≥3 chars, not purely numeric/unit — e.g. "JOGURT ACTIVIA TRUSKAWKOWY" instead of just "JOGURT"; backfill migrations `20260702160003` v1 + `20260702160004` v2 re-backfill existing rows; single-word entries from v1 are overwritten by v2, multi-word LLM-generated names preserved); write path: `ExpensesService.create()` + `SyncService` both persist `canonicalName`. **Discount line folding (ABA-343)**: some receipts (Polish Biedronka/Lidl/Żabka) print each discount on its own line and the OCR model sometimes emits those as negative line items ("OPUST PIWO HEINEKEN -8,00", "Lidl Plus kupon -6,87"). `OcrService.validateAndNormalizeReceipt` calls pure `extractReceiptDiscounts` (`modules/ai/utils/receipt-discount.ts`, unit-tested): a line is a discount when its amount is **negative AND** carries a discount label (`isDiscountLine`, regex across pl/en/de/es/it/ua/ru — the negative gate is the safety net so a positive product with a label-like brand name is never pulled). Discount lines are removed from `items` and their value folded into `parsed.discount` (`max(existingDiscount, Σ pulled lines)` — no double count). It only cleans the item list + the informational discount; the paid total (`amount` = OCR `total`) is **never** touched. Forward-only (new scans); existing rows unaffected. **Inflation formula**: Laspeyres weighted index — `inflationIndex = Σ(weight_i × priceChangePct_i) / Σ(weight_i)` where `weight_i = baseAvgPrice × purchaseCount_in_base`; `null` when `productCount < 3`; base period = `[now - period*2 .. now - period]`, current = `[now - period .. now]` using calendar-month boundaries. Multi-currency: majority currency by row count. **5 endpoints** (class-level `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`, no `@RequireTier` — free): `GET /price-history?period=3m|6m|12m` (Redis cache `ph:{accountId}:{period}` TTL 300s), `GET /price-history/products`, `PATCH /price-history/products/alias` (ViewerBlockGuard), `DELETE /price-history/products/alias/:rawName` (ViewerBlockGuard), `POST /price-history/products/merge` (ViewerBlockGuard), `POST /price-history/products/backfill-ai` (ABA-308, ViewerBlockGuard — re-generates canonical names for NULL/single-word entries using GPT-4o-mini; deduplicates descriptions, batches 50 at a time, max 500 unique per call; user aliases never overwritten; ~$0.001/1000 descriptions; mobile "Re-analyze with AI" button in products screen). Route order: static paths before `/:rawName`. **Name resolution**: `COALESCE(alias.canonicalName, item.canonicalName)` — alias overrides LLM value; merging = two rawNames share the same alias canonicalName. `PriceHistoryProduct.rawName` carries the original `expense_items.canonical_name` key (pre-alias) so mobile rename calls always update the correct alias row. **Mobile**: `priceHistoryStore.ts` (server-only, same pattern as `familyFeedStore`); `priceHistory.api.ts`; `InflationIndexSection.tsx` in Analytics tab (period chips, product list top-3 + expand, per-product bottom sheet: `InteractiveLineChart` + cheapest-first store comparison + rename — `canEdit`-gated); `app/settings/products.tsx` management screen (multi-select merge, rename alias, delete alias, `canEdit`-gated); linked from Settings → Reference Data hub. SQLite: `ALTER TABLE expense_items ADD COLUMN canonical_name TEXT` in `client.native.ts`. 29 `priceHistory.*` i18n keys (19 original + 6 products-screen UX keys + 4 AI-backfill keys) + `settingsNav.products/productsDesc` in all 9 locales. `priceHistoryStore.isLoadingProducts` flag drives ActivityIndicator in products screen while initial load runs.
- **Community Price Map (ABA-335)**: `modules/community-prices/` — crowdsourced, **k-anonymized** "where's cheapest" grocery DB built from ALL accounts' OCR'd `expense_items.canonical_name` (extends the Personal Inflation corpus; a data moat with a network effect). **Consent**: opt-in `User.contributeCommunityPrices Boolean @default(false)` (migration `20260711000001_add_community_contribution_pref`), settable via `PATCH /users/me` + returned by `GET/PATCH /me`; mobile toggle in `settings/data.tsx` (server-backed via `authStore` + `api.updateProfile`, optimistic w/ revert); `communityPrices.*` (3 keys) × 9 locales. **Write pipeline (M1)**: `CommunityPriceService.recordContribution` — fire-and-forget + **fail-silent** (NEVER throws into caller; `Logger.warn` only), hooked from the same post-create block in `ExpensesService.create` as anomaly/familyFeed (via `@Optional()` injection) + parity call in `SyncService.processExpenseItemChange` (also takes `userId` now). Gates on salt env + consent flag + POS coords + non-E2EE account + non-empty merchant; resolves product via alias-COALESCE, store via API-local `normalizeMerchantPL`, region via NEW `GeocodingService.reverseGeocode` (Nominatim `/reverse`, cached in `geocode_cache`, fail-silent) with grid-cell fallback (`community-price.util.ts` `regionBucket`), week via `mondayOfWeek`. **Data model**: `community_price_observations` (migration `20260711000000_add_community_price_observations`) — `@@unique([canonicalName, merchantNormalized, region, weekStart, currencyCode, contributorKey])` (map `community_obs_dedup`; IS the one-vote-per-account-per-week dedup + anti-poisoning); holds **NO** accountId/userId/expenseId/user-coords — only POS-derived `region`. `contributorKey = sha256(salt:accountId)` (one-way; the ONLY contributor link; contribution SKIPPED entirely if `COMMUNITY_PRICE_SALT` unset). **Read API (M2)**: pure `aggregateCommunityPrices` (`community-price-calculator.ts`, unit-tested — mirrors `computeBasket`) — exposes a store ONLY when `≥ K` distinct `contributorKey` back it (**k-anonymity**, `COMMUNITY_PRICE_K` default 5, min 2, never a single user), majority currency, outlier drop outside `[median/2, median×2]`, reports median/min/receiptCount/contributorCount, cheapest-first. `CommunityPriceService.getCommunityPrices(product, region, period)` + `searchProducts(q)` (nested-map distinct-count; returns only products that clear K in ≥1 region). Endpoints `GET /price-history/community?product=&region=&period=1w|4w` + `.../community/products?q=` on `CommunityPriceController` — **both Pro-gated** (`JwtAuth + AccountContext + SubscriptionTierGuard + @RequireTier('pro')`), global Redis cache `cph:{sha1(product)}:{region}:{period}` (anonymous data), NEVER returns `contributorKey`. `CommunityPriceModule` imports the standalone `GeocodingModule` (`modules/ai/geocoding.module.ts` — a leaf module with no imports, extracted so `AiModule` and `CommunityPriceModule` can share ONE `GeocodingService` singleton without an `AiModule → ExpensesModule → CommunityPriceModule → AiModule` cycle; a prior version of this module provided its own second `GeocodingService` instance, silently splitting the instance-level Nominatim throttle — fixed, see `docs/tech-debt/geocoding-service-duplicate-instance-breaks-throttle.md`) + imports `SubscriptionsModule` (for the tier guard). DTOs in `packages/shared-types/src/dto/community-price.ts`. **Mobile (M3)**: server-only `communityPriceStore` (mirrors `priceHistoryStore`; 403 → `useUpgradeStore.show(..., 'pro')`, same detection as `shoppingListStore`), `community-prices.api.ts`, screen `app/price-history/community.tsx` (debounced product search → cheapest-first per-store compare with median/k-anon counts + 1w/4w toggle), entry banner in Analytics `InflationIndexSection`; `communityPrices.*` = 15 keys × 9 locales. **Security (audited, ABA-335)**: the egress audit confirmed clean (contributorKey never leaves the server, both routes Pro-gated, write fails closed, no raw SQL, region town-level only) but flagged a Sybil weakness (K-gate counts distinct accounts; an attacker can mint accounts + `POST /expenses` with client-supplied merchant/canonicalName/location). Hardening: **read kill-switch** `COMMUNITY_PRICE_READ_ENABLED` (default OFF → read API returns empty, keeps the surface dark); **receipt-source gate** (only `ocr`/bot-photo scans contribute); **contributor eligibility** `isEligibleContributor` (account ≥7d old AND ≥15 real expenses, env-tunable via `COMMUNITY_MIN_ACCOUNT_AGE_DAYS`/`COMMUNITY_MIN_CONTRIBUTOR_EXPENSES`); label-length caps (`MAX_LABEL_LEN=64` + item `@MaxLength(120)`); read `weekStart` upper bound; case-exact cache key; **multi-week persistence** (`aggregateCommunity*` gain `minWeeks`/`displayFromWeek` — a store is exposed only if backed across ≥`COMMUNITY_MIN_PERSISTENCE_WEEKS` distinct weeks over an 8-week lookback, price still from the requested period; blocks single-week bursts); **behavioral correlation clustering** (`community-price-correlation.ts` — near-identical broad-footprint contributors are union-find clustered and the K-gate counts distinct CLUSTERS via `distinctClusterCount`, so a scripted ring collapses to one; conservative `minFootprint`/`jaccard`, flag `COMMUNITY_CORRELATION_ENABLED` **default OFF** until validated; signup-fingerprint velocity — IP/device/timing — is intentionally NOT done, impossible without new data collection since `contributorKey` is one-way). These raise the Sybil cost but do NOT fully solve it — remaining go-live checklist (validate+enable correlation, decide signup-velocity data collection, optional DP noise) in `docs/superpowers/community-price-antisybil.md`. **Store map (M4)**: the observation table deliberately holds NO coords, so a separate `community_store_geo` lookup (migration `20260711000002`; `@@unique([merchantNormalized, region])`, holds ONLY the STORE's public coordinate — no accountId/userId/contributorKey) is populated fire-and-forget from the same write path (the POS coord is already there). Pure `aggregateCommunityMap` (per store+region, same K-gate/outlier/median, unit-tested) → `getCommunityMap` joins K-cleared cells to store-geo, returns `CommunityPriceMapPoint[]` ONLY for cells with a known coord (re-marks cheapest among survivors); `GET /price-history/community/map` (Pro + read kill-switch, cache `cphmap:*`). Privacy: a pin shows ONLY for a store that already cleared K (the geo table is never returned raw — a single-user/home-mislabeled coord can't surface). Mobile: `communityPriceStore.loadMap` + `buildCommunityMapPoints` (mirrors `buildStoreMapPoints`) + a List/Map toggle on `app/price-history/community.tsx` reusing the zero-native `ExpenseMapView`, empty-state for the dark-read case; `communityPrices.*` = 18 keys × 9. **Env**: `COMMUNITY_PRICE_SALT` (required for contributions), `COMMUNITY_PRICE_K` / `COMMUNITY_PRICE_READ_ENABLED` / `COMMUNITY_MIN_ACCOUNT_AGE_DAYS` / `COMMUNITY_MIN_CONTRIBUTOR_EXPENSES` (optional). **Deferred**: full anti-Sybil (blocks flipping read on for real users); per-litre/kg normalization (canonical_name strips size → exact-match only); PL-biased merchant normalization fragments non-PL stores.
- **Receipt price check / "чек-контроль" (ABA-373, API = plan 1 of 2)**: after a receipt is scanned, every line is compared against the **median** of what this user previously paid for that product **at that same store** within a 12-week window; lines costing measurably more are reported so the user can act while still at the register. Deterministic — **no LLM in the hot path, no migration**. **Positioning constraint (binding on all copy, all languages)**: the data cannot prove a promo failed to apply (no discount line ⇒ no evidence a promo existed), so nothing may say "overcharged"/"cheated"/"promo not applied" — only "more expensive than usual — check the receipt". `overpaidAmount` is an internal field name; keep it internal. **Engine**: pure `modules/price-history/receipt-check.util.ts` (`checkReceiptPrices` → `{ findings, stats }`, `groupReceiptLines`, `median`, `perUnitPrice`, `resolveReceiptCheckConfig`, `RECEIPT_CHECK_DEFAULTS`), modelled on `inflation-shield.util.ts`. Median (not mean) so one promo-priced purchase in history can't manufacture a finding. Gates, all env-tunable `RECEIPT_CHECK_*`: `LOOKBACK_WEEKS` 12, `MIN_POINTS` 2, `MIN_RISE_PCT` 15, `MAX_RISE_PCT` 100 (above ⇒ assume a different pack size, drop + count in `stats.droppedByCap`), `MIN_AMOUNT` 1.00, `MAX_FINDINGS` 5 by amount; plus same-merchant, same-currency (**never any FX**). **No pack-size gate, by design**: the original spec assumed `canonicalName` strips pack size and planned a `size` field + equality gate to stop a 1L/500ml false-positive match — that premise was wrong (see the ABA-307 entry above: `canonicalName` already KEEPS per-unit size), so different pack sizes are already different products and never match in the name lookup; the field/gate were built anyway, were unreachable (nothing ever populated `size`), and were removed as dead code. Duplicate receipt lines are grouped by name alone with a quantity-weighted unit price, so one product yields at most one finding. Community prices are a **fallback baseline** when personal history is too thin (`source:'community'`, never `confidence:'high'`) — implemented + unit-tested but **not yet service-wired** (prod reads are off behind `COMMUNITY_PRICE_READ_ENABLED`). **Two call sites, one engine**: (1) inline at scan time via new private `OcrService.finalizeReceipt(parsed, categories, accountId)` = `buildReceiptExpense` + `runPriceCheck`, which **replaced all four `buildReceiptExpense` call sites** (image, text-PDF, 2 branches of the PDF-as-file fallback) — keep it that way so a 5th scan path inherits the check; `ReceiptExpense.priceFindings` is **always present** (empty array, never `undefined`) and so reaches the mobile scan screen and all 3 bots for free; `runPriceCheck` is **fail-silent** (any throw ⇒ `[]` + `logger.warn`, precedent `GeocodingService` in the same service). (2) post-create `AnomalyService.detectPriceOvercharge` (fired last from `checkExpense`, **not** `checkExpenseBatch` — imports have no line items) persists one row, type `'price_overcharge'` (plain string, **no migration**, precedent `possible_merge`), dedupKey `overcharge:{expenseId}`, all findings in `params`. **No push ever** — new `CreateAlertInput.skipPush` (a discriminated union, so a future detector can't silently forget its push fns) returns **before** the daily-cap query so a feed-only row can't consume the push budget. **`RECEIPT_CHECK_ALERTS_ENABLED` (default OFF)** gates only the alert *write*: the mobile `alerts/index.tsx` `default:` branch renders `String(alert.type)`, so un-gated this would show already-installed apps a card titled `price_overcharge` with an empty body. The detector still runs and logs what it would have written. The plan-2 mobile build — which renders the real card instead of the raw type string — has landed on this branch (not yet released to the stores); flipping the flag in production remains blocked until an actual store rollout of that build satisfies the precondition in `docs/ops/receipt-price-check-rollout.md` (precondition, log-based readiness check, exact flip command, rollback). **Query**: `PriceHistoryService.getProductTrendsFor(accountId, canonicalNames, merchantNormalized, since, currencyCode, excludeExpenseId?)` — narrow sibling of `getProductTrends` (which reads the account's ENTIRE item history and must never touch the scan hot path); merchant **and** currency are matched in JS beside each other (stored merchant is a display name), honours `IGNORED_SENTINEL`, skips non-positive price points. **Four bugs the review caught — do not reintroduce**: (a) `ExpensesService.create` commits before firing `checkExpense`, so without `excludeExpenseId` the detector counts **the receipt under examination** as its own history (1 prior at 20 + this at 30 ⇒ inline finds nothing, detector invents baseline 25 and upgrades `confidence` to `high`) — the "both passes agree" claim is false without it, and `receipt-check.util.cross-path.spec.ts` is the test guarding it; (b) history keyed by alias-**resolved** name while the engine looks up the **raw** line name ⇒ any renamed/merged product yielded zero findings forever, silently — entries are now emitted under the requested raw name; (c) grouping keyed by name alone mixed two currencies into one series; (d) `NaN` unit price passed every gate (all `NaN` comparisons are false) ⇒ "about NaN PLN more" in the bot line. **Bots**: `buildPriceCheckLine` + `priceCheckSummary` in all 3 `photo.handler.ts` + `helpers/i18n.ts` (9 langs each); empty findings ⇒ empty string ⇒ reply byte-identical to before. **Shipped in plan 2** (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-25-receipt-price-check-mobile.md`): `PriceFindingsCard` on `expense/receipt.tsx`; the `price_overcharge` alert card (which is what makes the release gate flippable — before it, `alerts/index.tsx`'s `default:` branch rendered the raw type string); the "found overpayments" total on the Analytics inflation section, fed by `GET /alerts/price-check-summary` (**per-currency map, never a blended total** — summing would require FX, which this feature forbids; the client shows the user's own currency if anything was found in it, else the largest single one) and cleared on account switch via `alertStore.clearPriceCheckSummary()`; `receiptCheck.*` × 9 locales; and a plural-safe rewrite of the bot line (the bot `t()` has **no** plural support, so the count sits after a label as `товаров: {{count}}` rather than before an agreeing noun — do not "fix" it back). **Still deferred**: community wiring + "cheaper nearby", per-litre/kg normalization (needs *parsed* size — value + unit + conversion — not a raw token), an index on `expense_items.canonical_name`, and a real *saved* counter (needs a dispute/refund flow to prove the user acted). Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-25-receipt-price-check-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-25-receipt-price-check-api.md`.
- **Receipt category auto-split (ABA-398)**: splits a scanned supermarket receipt's own line items across expense categories — a 240 zł Biedronka trip becomes 180 groceries + 35 household + 25 alcohol instead of one "Groceries" blob — computed once at scan time inside the OCR funnel, no new endpoint, no LLM in any write path. **Classification chain, cheapest first**: `ReceiptCategorySplitService.classify()` (`modules/ai/services/receipt-category-split.service.ts`) answers from per-account learned rules first — `ProductRulesService` (`modules/merchant-rules/product-rules.service.ts`, a `MerchantRulesService` sibling registered in the same module), keyed by `normalizeProductName` over table `product_category_rules`. **The key is the receipt's own printed line (`ExpenseItem.description`), NOT the model's `canonicalName` (ABA-441)** — and both sides must use the same one: `ClassifyLine.ruleKey` reads it, `LearnableExpenseItem.ruleKey` writes it. `canonicalName` is invented per scan and is not stable, so keying on it meant the cache never hit: one Biedronka receipt scanned on consecutive days taught 22 rules then 33 more **with no key in common**, leaving contradictory pairs (`piwo carlsberg 0,5l`->Piwo beside `carlsberg 0,5l`->Groceries) whose winner is an accident of spelling, and every scan paying the model again. `normalizeProductName` now strips everything that is not a letter or a digit (spacing, punctuation, case, diacritics - `l-stroke` explicitly, since NFD does not decompose it, and it is the one the OCR actually dropped); the class is Unicode-wide, not `a-z`, so a Cyrillic line does not normalize to the empty string. Migration `20260828120000_reset_product_category_rules` empties the table - old rows are keyed by the dead scheme and can never match again (migration `20260812120001_add_product_category_rules`, `@@unique([accountId, canonicalNameNormalized])`, cascade-deleted with its category like merchant rules) — and sends only the lines the rules didn't cover to GPT. **The model never emits money**: it receives each line's index + label and the account's category **names**, and returns `[{itemIndex, categoryName}]` only — no amount, percentage, or total anywhere in the exchange. The response is validated against a `Set` of the account's real category names (case-insensitive, not an object map — the AI-import validator's own rule against `Object.prototype` false positives) and the real `[0, items.length)` index range; anything invented or out of range is silently dropped, never trusted. Ceiling: **`AI_SPLIT_MAX_INFERENCES_PER_DAY`** (default 20, NaN-guarded), a per-account daily Redis counter (`aisplit:{accountId}:{YYYY-MM-DD}`) deliberately outside the monthly AI usage quota (precedent: AI-import inference, ABA-390) — a rule hit never counts against it, and the counter increments only after a model call actually returns (a thrown/rejected call leaves it untouched, so a failed attempt doesn't burn the day's budget). Every model answer is written back into `ProductRulesService.upsertRules` so a repeat product costs nothing next time — the reason this is free and outside the monthly quota is affordable at all. **Arithmetic**: pure `buildCategorySplits()` is a **deliberately duplicated pair** — canonical `apps/api/src/common/utils/receipt-category-split.ts`, mirror `packages/shared-utils/src/formatting/receipt-category-split.ts` (same reason as `financial-month.ts`: the API has no build step and can't import `@budget/shared-utils` at runtime) — groups lines by resolved category so that **the group cent-values sum to the receipt total's cent-value exactly** (each amount is a correctly-rounded 2dp value by integer-cents construction; re-summing the *returned floats* afterwards still carries the usual IEEE-754 epsilon, which every money formatter here already rounds away). **The tolerance gate accounts for two known, opposite adjustments (ABA-440)**: a basket `discount` (money off after the lines were priced — lines are gross, total is net) and a returnable-packaging `deposit` (Polish `kaucja`: bottle/can charges printed in their own `OPAKOWANIA ZWROTNE` block below the goods total, never as line items, yet included in `DO ZAPŁATY`). The discount is spread proportionally across the item groups and the residual is assigned to the largest one; the deposit is deliberately never spread and never absorbs the residual — both adjustments run only on the item groups, before the deposit is appended, since it is an exact printed figure and guessing which lines the bottles were on is worse than leaving it alone. On the Biedronka receipt that prompted this, 4.50 of deposits was 1.9% of the total before a single line had been misread. **The deposit is its own split group, not only a gate adjustment**: `buildCategorySplits`' optional `depositGroup` parameter appends a `{categoryId, categoryName}` group holding exactly `depositCents`, appended after the discount-spread and residual steps above so it shares in neither, and it counts toward the function's own two-group minimum — a receipt that is otherwise entirely one category (typically groceries) now splits into that category plus deposit, on both the automatic scan path and the manual reassignment path (`buildManualSplits`' `willAppendGroup` parameter keeps a lone assigned category alive instead of refusing when a deposit is about to be appended, and `withDepositGroup` re-attaches the deposit **unscaled** after the user's own assignment is rebuilt, so reassigning a line never rescales the deposit itself — it stays exactly what was printed). The deposit's category name comes from an API-local table, `DEPOSIT_CATEGORY_NAMES` (`receipt-category-split.service.ts`, one word per locale — `Kaucja`/`Pfand`/`Depósito`/`Statiegeld`/… — exported as `depositCategoryName()`), resolved from the **account owner's** language via `ReceiptFinalizerService.resolveOwnerLanguage` (`findFirst` filtered on `role: 'owner'`, never sorted — the same alphabetical-`editor`-before-`owner` trap `WalletCurrencyService.resolveOwnerId` documents) so a shared account converges on one deposit category regardless of who scanned; if the account already has a category with that exact name (case-insensitive), the deposit reuses its id instead of minting a `proposed:` one. **Nothing may ever file a receipt LINE into it either (ABA-451)**: the deposit category is a real row in `categories`, so from the scan after the first `kaucja` it sat in the list handed to the classifier like any other and the model filed cured ham, bacon and peanuts under `Kaucja` on a Lidl receipt — then the save-time learner wrote those three as `product_category_rules`, making the mistake deterministic, model-free and self-re-teaching on every later save. `classify()` now splits its input into `assignable` and deposit categories via `isDepositCategoryName` (`common/utils/deposit-category.ts`, which also owns the name table and `depositCategoryName`; it lives in `common/` because `modules/merchant-rules` is a leaf the AI module depends on): the deposit ones are kept out of the prompt AND out of `validCategoryIds`, so an existing poisoned rule is ignored rather than honoured, and `validateProposals` rejects a proposal named after one. Recognition matches **all nine** locale names, never just the owner's current language — an owner can switch language and a shared account can change owner, but a category keeps the name it was born with. Migration `20260829080000_delete_deposit_product_rules` deletes the rows already written (`DELETE 3` on prod). Still open: a user can hand-assign a line to it in `ItemCategorySheet`, which would produce a second group with the same `categoryId`. **It is deliberately never routed through `proposals`**, so it never has to clear `MIN_PROPOSAL_SHARE_PCT` (10%) — a deposit is typically 1-2% of a receipt and would be dropped by that floor every time, which would have made this whole feature silently never appear. Independent of whether a split is produced at all, `Expense.depositAmount` (nullable `Decimal(12,2)`, written at all three of `ExpensesService`'s create / create-upsert's update-branch / `update()` write sites — the same three-site pattern `location` needed) persists the printed figure, so it is visible even on a receipt whose arithmetic doesn't reconcile closely enough within the 5% tolerance to produce a split at all. **It must also be listed in `findAll`'s explicit `select`** — that method selects fields one by one (unlike `findOne`, which uses `include`), so a field omitted there is simply absent from every list response; native papers over it from the local SQLite row, but web (no SQLite) and any second device or reinstall would show the value missing while it sits in Postgres. **Encryption — known gap, not an oversight**: `discountAmount` is in `ENCRYPTION_FIELDS.expense.tier2`, but `depositAmount` is in **neither tier** and is not fed into `maybeEncrypt`'s input at `expenseStore.ts`/`expenseSync.ts`, so a tier-2 (fully encrypted) account stores its deposit **in clear**. That partially leaks the encrypted `amount`: a deposit is a fixed per-unit charge, so its plaintext bounds the basket's bottle/can count and, on a small receipt, a meaningful share of the total. Closing it means adding the field to `tier2` **and** to both `maybeEncrypt` call sites — the two must land together, since the tier list alone would zero the plaintext without anything encrypting it. Until then the two client write paths pass `expense.depositAmount` straight through with no `encPayload` read; do not re-add an `encPayload.depositAmount ?? …` fallback, which reads as though encryption participates when it never can. **Refuses to emit any split** when the usable lines are further than `RECEIPT_SPLIT_DEFAULTS`' 5% tolerance from the receipt total, or when fewer than 2 categories would result — refusing is the honest answer, not smearing an unexplained gap across categories; within tolerance, the residual (unassigned/unlabeled lines, discounts, rounding) goes to the largest group instead of inventing a category for it, so an unlabeled line's money is folded in, never dropped from the sum. **Storage — two new things, not a new split table**: the split rows themselves ride on the pre-existing `ExpenseCategorySplit`/`expense_category_splits` table and `SplitEditor` component that `analytics.service.ts` already preferred over an expense's own category; what's new is `ExpenseItem.categoryId String? @map("category_id")` (nullable, `onDelete: SetNull`; migration `20260812120000_add_expense_item_category`) for per-line explainability, written by both `ExpensesService.create()` and `SyncService.processExpenseItemChange` through the same `resolveCategoryId`/`resolveExpenseCategoryId` helper the expense-level category already uses — so a rule is only ever taught the resolved server id, never a raw client-local one. **Transport**: `ReceiptExpense.categorySplits: ReceiptCategorySplit[]` is **always present** (empty array, never `undefined` — the `priceFindings` convention), computed in `OcrService.finalizeReceipt` right after `runPriceCheck`, so all four scan call sites and all three bots get it for free; a fully encrypted (tier-2) account skips classification before touching any line item. **Mobile**: the receipt scan screen (`app/expense/receipt.tsx`) renders the split as `CategorySplitChips` and lets the user reassign a line via `ItemCategorySheet`, recomputing `currentSplits` from the shared-utils `buildCategorySplits` on every edit (never patched incrementally); every reassignment that reaches save teaches a rule (`items[].categoryId` now survives `expenseStore.ts`'s `sanitizedItems`, which used to silently drop it). If any incoming split fails to resolve to a local category (by id or by name), **the whole set is dropped, not partially kept** — an all-or-nothing seed gated by `splitDropped` + a `receiptCategorySplit.dropped` note. **The mobile i18n namespace is `receiptCategorySplit`, not `receiptSplit`** — the latter already belongs to the unrelated guest-link bill-splitting feature below; do not collide the two. **Analytics**: `groupExpensesByCategory()` (`src/features/analytics/categoryGrouping.ts`) contributes each split's own converted amount to its own category — for the current period **and** the 3-month trailing average behind `vsAverage`, so the delta doesn't compare a split month against unsplit history — while an expense with no splits still contributes its whole amount to `categoryId`, unchanged; totals hooks (`useSummaryAnalytics`, `useFilteredTransactions`) are untouched, since they sum `expense.amount`, never a split. Splits reach memory only via the pull-and-merge cycle (`expenseSync.ts`'s `attachSplits`, a bulk `getSplitsForExpenses` read chunked at 900 ids), so a just-scanned receipt's analytics catch up on the next pull, not instantly. **Bots**: all three (Telegram/WhatsApp/Slack `handlers/photo.handler.ts`) pass `categorySplits` into the created expense's `dto.splits` and append one reply line via `buildCategorySplitLine` (`helpers/i18n.ts`, 9 langs; empty splits ⇒ empty string ⇒ byte-identical reply); Telegram's line is `escapeHtml()`-wrapped like every other free-text field in that file, since an unescaped `&` in a category name (e.g. "Health & Beauty") breaks its `parse_mode: 'HTML'` reply and silently downgrades a successful scan to the generic failure message. **Budgets are deliberately untouched** — splits change category analytics only; `budgets.service.ts`, `budget-alert.service.ts`, `SafeToSpendService`, and the `get_budget_status` AI tool all keep reading the expense's own single `categoryId`, the same way manual splits already behave. **Deleted as dead code**: `SplitSuggestionService`, `POST /ai/suggest-splits`, and mobile's `api.suggestSplits` — all three had zero call sites, and the old contract asked the model to return `"amount": number` per category, the exact anti-pattern this feature exists to replace. **Category proposals**: when none of the account's existing categories fit a group of lines, `ReceiptCategorySplitService.classifyWithModel`'s prompt also asks for `newCategories: [{name, lines}]` alongside `assignments`, and `validateProposals` (same file) holds every entry to the same "never trust, only drop" posture as `validateAssignments` — the name must normalize (control chars stripped, whitespace collapsed) to 2–30 characters containing at least one letter, must not case-insensitively match an existing category or an already-accepted proposal in the same response, and each of its line numbers must be in range and not already claimed — an **assignment always wins a contested line over a proposal**, because `validateAssignments` runs first and seeds the `claimed` set the proposal validator reads, so the outcome never depends on response order. At most **`MAX_PROPOSED_CATEGORIES`** (3) proposals survive a single scan: the length check sits at the top of the loop, so a 4th distinct, individually valid proposal is dropped outright, not merged or truncated. Proposal names are requested **in the account owner's language** (`user.language`, mapped through a private `LANGUAGE_NAMES` table covering all 9 app locales, defaulting to English) — not generated in English and translated after the fact. **Transport convention**: an unresolved proposal reaches the client as an ordinary `ReceiptCategorySplitPayload` with `categoryId: null` (`OcrService.runCategorySplit`); the pure arithmetic — `buildCategorySplits` and its `ReceiptCategorySplit` type in `common/utils/receipt-category-split.ts` — is deliberately left untouched and knows nothing about proposals, because the mobile edit path's `rebuildCategorySplits` feeds that identical shape into a non-nullable column, and teaching it a nullable id would leak the possibility everywhere that type flows. Two sentinel string prefixes carry a proposal internally and must never be confused: the server groups a proposal's lines under an opaque `proposed:<name>` key inside `runCategorySplit` alone (`proposedKey`/`isProposedKey`/`proposedNameFromKey` in `receipt-category-split.service.ts`), rewriting it back to `categoryId: null` before anything leaves the function; the mobile client separately holds the same idea as `new:<name>` (`src/features/receipt/proposedCategory.ts`) in local screen state only. Neither prefix is ever a DTO field or a stored value — both stay strictly on their own side of the API boundary. **Nothing creates a category at scan time**: `classify()` only proposes a name and never calls `CategoriesService.create`. The sole creators are the mobile save handler (`app/expense/receipt.tsx`) and each bot's confirm branch (`resolveProposedSplits` in `modules/ai/utils/receipt-split-items.ts`, called from all three `handleReceiptAddCallback`s) — both via the idempotent `CategoriesService.create` / `categoryStore.createCategory`, icon `🏷️`, no colour, and both only once the user has explicitly confirmed (Save in the app, "Add expense" in a bot) — a scan the user abandons must not plant a category. **The list of names to create must be read from the SPLITS, not from the line→category map** (`proposedNamesForSave` in `src/features/receipt/resolveProposedCategories.ts`): `seedItemCategories` writes a key only for the indexes in a split's `itemIndexes`, and the **deposit group's is empty by construction** — it is the one group with no receipt line behind it — so a list built from `Object.values(itemCategories)` skipped the deposit's proposal, and the save path sent the raw `new:Kaucja` sentinel to the API, where `resolveExpenseCategoryId` (which auto-creates any non-UUID, non-`default-` string it can't match by name) minted a category **literally named `new:Kaucja`** that every later scan then matched by name and reused. The bots were always correct here — `resolveProposedSplits` iterates all splits regardless of `itemIndexes` — so left unfixed the app and the bots would have minted two different categories for the same thing. The screen keeps a separate `proposedNamesInPlay` for the line-category picker (a category with no lines has no place in a picker over lines); creation reads `proposedNamesToCreate`. **Rule learning moved to save time only**: the scan-time learner that used to live inside `ReceiptCategorySplitService.classify()` was deleted, because it taught `product_category_rules` from a scan regardless of whether the user ever saved it — this is exactly how the production account in the ABA-398 incident ended up pinned to one category shape. `ExpensesService.create()` (`expenses.service.ts:517`, `void this.productRules?.upsertRules(...)`) is now the single writer, keyed off the RESOLVED per-item `categoryId` the expense actually saved with; all three bots now also pass `items[].categoryId` (via `buildItemCategoryMap`) into `expensesService.create()`, without which a bot-confirmed receipt would resolve its proposal into a real category but never teach the rule that makes the next identical receipt free to classify. **The model is re-read once when a receipt does not add up (ABA-442)**: extraction is not reproducible - the same Biedronka PDF and the same prompt gave line totals of 345.16, 294.28 and 311.97 against a true 299.82, and read the printed `OPUSTY LACZNIE: -70,34` as 70.34 twice and 18.97 once, so two of three scans refused to split for two unrelated reasons. `OcrService.readReceipt` (the single choke point all FOUR scan paths now go through - image, text-PDF, and both PDF-as-file branches) checks the pure `reconciliationGapPct` from `modules/ai/utils/receipt-reconcile.ts` (`Sum(lines) - discount + deposit` vs total, tolerance matching the split gate's 5%) and, when it fails, re-issues the IDENTICAL request once and keeps `betterRead`. A tie or an unmeasurable second reading keeps the first, so a re-read can only rescue a scan, never degrade one; the extra call is only ever spent on a scan already headed for no split. **The re-read's real cost is tracked as its own AI-COGS line (ABA-459, tech-debt `ocr-reread-cost-not-tracked`)**: `AiUsageGuard`'s `@TrackAiUsage('ocr', 2.0)` on `scan-receipt` runs as a `canActivate` guard *before* `readReceipt` ever executes, so it can only ever see the fixed first-call cost — a re-read is a second, full, billed vision call the decorator is structurally blind to. `readReceipt` calls `SubscriptionsService.recordAdditionalUsage(userId, 'ocr_reread', 2.0, accountId)` (new best-effort method, writes a `usage_logs` row via the existing private `logUsage`, deliberately does **not** touch the monthly quota — that quota-generosity gap is separate and still open) right when the second call is dispatched, on both its success and failure path, since OpenAI bills either way. `admin-analytics.service.ts`'s `ESTIMATED_COST_PER_REQUEST` carries a matching `ocr_reread: 0.012` entry; because every admin AI-COGS/usage consumer already `groupBy`s on the raw `featureType` string with no allow-list, the new type shows up automatically as its own line in AI-COGS and every per-feature breakdown, with no admin frontend change. **Deliberately NOT** deriving the discount from `Sum(lines) - total`: that makes every reading reconcile by construction, including the one that over-read the basket by 45, destroying the only signal that a reading is bad. **A discount the lines already reflect is dropped, and the re-read is aimed by the SIGN of the gap (ABA-449)**: pure `isDiscountAlreadyInLines` (same file) clears a `discount` only when the lines reconcile WITHOUT it and fail WITH it — the narrow inverse of the derivation above, since it can only ever DROP a figure the lines contradict, never invent one, and stays silent when neither form reconciles. It exists because the earlier checks in `validateAndNormalizeReceipt` could not catch that case and actively **blessed** it: the tax-exclusive form `subtotal - discount + tax = total` is satisfied identically whenever the model reports the VAT line as a discount, because the two cancel — a Sinsay receipt whose `Podatek PTU 3,01` came back as `discount: 3.01` reconciled perfectly and then produced no split, `Sum(lines) - 3.01` missing the total by 19%. Four of one account's eleven scans on 2026-08-27 stored such a discount (also Rossmann's already-applied `Uwzgl. opust` rows, and two Biedronka reads that returned NET lines), and all four stored **zero** category splits as a direct result. The check runs LAST in `validateAndNormalizeReceipt`, after the discount-line fold, so it sees the final items/discount/deposit. Separately, `buildCorrectionNote` no longer always blames the quantity column: an over-read gets the pack-notation guidance it was written for, an **under-read gets price guidance instead**, because a pack multiplier can only ever inflate a sum. The two Biedronka receipts scanned 2026-08-27 failed the other way — every quantity correct, 7 of 15 unit prices misread (`14,69`->`4,69` and `16,99`->`6,99`, a dropped leading digit; `2 x 10,49` -> `2 x 5,27`, the value of the `OPUST` row printed BENEATH that line) — so the old wording sent the second pass to re-check the one column already right. Discount-row keywords in that note span `OPUST`/`RABAT`/`СКИДКА`/`ЗНИЖКА`/`DISCOUNT`; these receipts are not only Polish. **Still undetectable**: invented item NAMES on a reading whose arithmetic happens to reconcile — a Pepco receipt stored two wholly fabricated line names whose prices summed to exactly the total, so `reconciliationGapPct` was 0 and nothing fired. **Diagnosability**: `runCategorySplit` logs exactly one `[CategorySplit]` line per outcome — `few_lines`, `no_categories`, `no_assignments`, `one_category`, `refused_by_arithmetic`, or `ok groups=N proposed=M` — precisely because the original production failure (a 30-line Biedronka receipt, three legitimate categories, zero split, zero error) had to be diagnosed by reading database tables with no log line to point at the cause. `one_category` names the specific, actionable cause (fewer than 2 distinct categories/proposals survived); `refused_by_arithmetic` is the honest catch-all for the other three ways `buildCategorySplits` can return `[]` — the gap-over-tolerance check, a residual that zeroes out the largest group, or no line having a usable amount — none of which is "the gap", so a single specific-sounding label for all three would be confidently wrong two times out of three. **DI trap, fixed (ABA-453)**: all 8 Telegram handlers (`CommandHandler`/`ExpenseHandler`/`IncomeHandler`/`ChatHandler`/`VoiceHandler`/`PhotoHandler`/`CategoryHandler`/`PurchaseRequestHandler`) used to be constructed by hand in `TelegramBotService.onModuleInit()` (`new PhotoHandler(this.ocrService, this.expensesService, this.subscriptionsService, this.categoriesService)`) — Nest's container never saw them, so any future constructor dependency added to one needed a matching manual edit at that call site or Telegram silently received `undefined` for it at runtime (this bit `PhotoHandler` once already, for `categoriesService`). All 8 are now `@Injectable()`, registered as `providers` in `telegram.module.ts`, and injected into `TelegramBotService`'s constructor — the same pattern WhatsApp's and Slack's handlers already used. `onModuleInit` now only constructs the `Telegraf` instance itself (needs the runtime bot token, so it can't be a static provider). **Deferred**: category budgets counting splits fractionally, `AnomalyService.detectCategorySpike` honouring splits (it keeps grouping by `categoryId`, so a spike confined to a split-only category won't fire), backfill of already-scanned receipts, a hand-written product dictionary (the per-account rule cache was assumed to reach the same steady state in one or two shopping trips without one - ABA-441 disproved that for the old key and fixed it; whether the printed-line key actually converges is still unmeasured), splits for bank/Wise imports or manual entry (neither has line items), and re-deriving splits for historical receipts after rules accumulate.
- **Receipt splitting / guest links (ABA)**: `modules/receipt-split/` (44th module) — the payer of a shared bill splits it among friends who don't have the app; each gets a public, unauthenticated web link showing only their own share plus a payment deep-link, and the payer sees a per-person status list (`sent → opened → claimed → settled`). **Two-call-site guest surface**: `GuestController` (`@Controller('s')`) serves `GET /s/:token` (renders the guest page, throttled 20/60s) and `POST /s/:token/paid` (guest's "I paid" action, throttled 10/60s) — both excluded from the `/api/v1` prefix in `main.ts` (`exclude: [..., 's/:token', 's/:token/paid']`, same list as the Stripe/Telegram/WhatsApp/Slack webhooks) — the **only unauthenticated surface in the app**. An unknown, expired, and cancelled token render byte-identical responses (same status/body/length) so a probing guest can't distinguish "never existed" from "used to exist" — enforced by a deliberately two-query `findUsableParticipant` (see the doc comment on that method for why the split matters for timing, not just tidiness). Payer-facing CRUD is a separate `ReceiptSplitController` (`/expenses/:id/receipt-split*` — distinct route segment from the unrelated category-splits `/expenses/:id/splits`): `POST` create, `GET` status, `PATCH /:participantId/confirm`, `DELETE` cancel, all `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard` (class) + `ViewerBlockGuard` + `TripArchivedGuard` (route, including the `GET` — a viewer can't see the split any more than create one). Rejects (`400`) on a fully E2EE (tier-2) account — the server can't read encrypted line items to build the guest page. **Accounting — `isSplitReceivable`, never `isDebt`**: creating a split writes one `isDebt: true, isSplitReceivable: true` Expense per participant (the receivable) alongside the original receipt Expense (the outflow); splitting a 200 bill 3 ways would otherwise double-count as 350 of spend. `common/utils/expense-filters.ts`'s `EXCLUDE_SPLIT_RECEIVABLE = { isSplitReceivable: false }` is the single shared predicate spread into every user-facing total — `analytics.service.ts`, `budget-alert.service.ts`, `safe-to-spend.service.ts`, `wallet.service.ts` — and it filters on `isSplitReceivable`, **never `isDebt`**: for a standalone cash loan ("I handed Kolya 500") the debt row IS the outflow, so filtering on `isDebt` would silently rewrite the numbers of every user who already tracks debts. Mobile mirrors this with `filterConsumption()` (`apps/mobile/src/utils/consumption.ts`, same `isSplitReceivable`-only rule, absent-means-false since the column is nullable client-side), consumed by **seven** client surfaces: `NetProfitWidget.tsx`, `useFilteredTransactions.ts`, `useSafeToSpend.ts`, `useScenarioProjection.ts`, `useCalendarData.ts`, `widgetData.ts`, `budgetStore.ts`. **Guest link base**: `APP_PUBLIC_URL` env var (`.env.example`) — unset in prod today, falls back to `https://api.ai-budget.pl` (already serves the route); the pretty apex form needs a `location /s/` nginx block that does not exist yet (`docs/ops/receipt-split-rollout.md` has the exact block + rollback). **Notifications**: the guest's `POST /s/:token/paid` fires `split_payment_claimed` to the payer (`notification-i18n.ts`, no per-type preference toggle — a one-off action request like `account_invitation`, not a recurring alert); the payer's own confirm (`PATCH .../confirm`) runs `DebtsService.recordRepayment` — the exact same path a manual repayment takes — under an atomic `settledAt IS NULL` claim (`updateMany` guard) so a double-tap/retry can't mint two repayment incomes. **Concurrency invariant**: partial unique index `receipt_split_live_slot` on `(expense_id, seq) WHERE cancelled_at IS NULL` (migration `20260727120000_add_receipt_split_participant_seq`) enforces at most one LIVE split per expense — a concurrent double-create collides (P2002 caught outside the `$transaction`, ABA-313 pattern) and a re-split after `cancelSplit` freely reuses `seq 0` because the cancelled rows have dropped out of the index. **`getSplit` 404s when no split exists for the expense — the normal state of every unsplit receipt, not a failure** (mobile `receiptSplitStore.load` treats a 404 as "no split" and only warns on any other error). Four migrations: `20260726120000_add_receipt_split` (table + `Expense.isSplitReceivable` + `User.paymentMethod`/`paymentHandle` — now a legacy single-pair fallback, see below), `20260726130000_add_receipt_split_cancelled_at`, `20260727120000_add_receipt_split_participant_seq`, `20260727130000_add_user_payment_methods`. **Payer-set payment info now lives on the payer's own profile, and supports several methods**: `app/settings/profile.tsx` grew a "Payment Settings" card (`Settings → Profile → Payment Settings`, a personal preference — not `canEdit`-gated, same precedent as currency/accent color); before this, `User.paymentMethod`/`paymentHandle` was read by the guest page but never written by anything in the app except the trip-wallet Payment Settings screen, which writes `AccountMember`, not `User` — so a normal (non-trip) account's guest link could never show a pay button at all. New table `user_payment_methods` (`userId`, `method SettleMethod`, `handle`, `sortOrder`, `@@unique([userId, method])`, cascade FK to `User`) holds up to 5 rows, one per method. `PUT /users/me/payment-methods` (`ReplaceUserPaymentMethodsDto` — `@ArrayMaxSize(5)` + `ArrayUnique` on `method` + handle `@Matches(PAYMENT_HANDLE_REGEX)`, now a **third** copy of that regex that must stay byte-for-byte identical to the legacy pair's and `AccountMemberPaymentInfoDto`'s) replaces the whole list atomically: one `$transaction` that deletes+recreates the list **and** nulls out the legacy `User.paymentMethod`/`paymentHandle` pair, so a user who saves through this endpoint (even to an empty list) can never have the guest page silently resurrect a stale legacy value later. `GET /users/me` returns `paymentMethods: Array<{method, handle}>` ordered by `sortOrder`. **`GuestController.resolvePayer` resolves at request time (not write time — so setting/changing this after links were already sent still fixes them), three levels deep**: the `user_payment_methods` list first (any rows at all ⇒ that's the whole answer, full stop) → else the legacy `User.paymentMethod`/`paymentHandle` pair → else, only when EITHER legacy field is unset, the payer's `AccountMember`-level pair for that account (the pre-existing trip-wallet per-account override, deliberately still a single pair, not a list — it's a one-off override, not a full method menu). The guest page now renders **one block per resolved method** (`GuestPaymentMethodBlock[]`, in resolution order) — a tappable button for `revolut`/`paypal`, an instructions box for `blik`/`other`/`cash` (each with its own copy, ABA-378 fixed `cash`/`other` rendering nothing despite the payer having typed a handle for them — an IBAN, a card number, an in-person note). `buildGuestPayLink` (helpers/guest-page.ts) returns `{ paymentLink, instructions: GuestPayInstructionKind | null }` (`'blik' | 'other' | 'cash' | null`, replacing an earlier `manualInstructions: boolean` that could only say "show *a* box," never *which* copy) and is still a pure per-method builder; the multi-method fan-out (calling it once per resolved method) lives in `GuestController.buildModel`, not the builder. Its revolut/paypal branches still mirror `trip-settle-up.service.ts`'s `createPayment` exactly, but that function has no cash/other branches of its own — the two are no longer in full lockstep, only on revolut/paypal/blik. Mobile: `app/expense/split.tsx` (one route — the creation form becomes the status view once a split exists), `receiptSplitStore.ts` (server-only, no SQLite mirror, mirrors `purchaseRequestStore`/`tripStore`). **Screen decomposition (ABA-422)**: `split.tsx` was a 972-line screen mixing both states; it's now a ~350-line thin composition that resolves the expense/items, loads the split, and renders one of two components under `src/components/receipt-split/` — `AssignmentEditor.tsx` (owns `participants`/`assignments`/`selectedItemId` state, validation, and builds the `CreateSplitDto` it hands to the root's `onSubmit`) and `ParticipantStatusList.tsx` (the post-creation status rows — send/copy/confirm/cancel — purely props-driven, no state of its own beyond what the root passes as `confirmingId`/`isCancelling`). The add-person sub-flow (name input + "people you've split with before" chips) is `useAddParticipant` (`src/hooks/`) + a presentational `AddPersonRow.tsx`, both consumed only by `AssignmentEditor`. Mirrors the same split the shopping-list and account-detail screens already went through — any future edit to the assignment UI or the status rows should extend the matching component, not `split.tsx` itself. **QR-code bill split (ABA)**: `ReceiptSplitParticipant.groupToken String? @unique` — set ONLY on the `seq:0` ("anchor") row at creation (128-bit random, same strength as the per-participant `token`); `null` for a split created before this column existed, no backfill. `SplitStateResponse.groupUrl: string | null` (`${GUEST_LINK_BASE}/s/g/{groupToken}?lang=`) is built by `toStateResponse` from the anchor row on every call site (create/idempotent-return/race-return/getSplit) — mobile treats `null` as "no QR available", never an error. Two new `GuestController` routes, same unauthenticated/throttled/indistinguishable-404 posture as `:token`: `GET /s/g/:groupToken` renders a **names-only** picker page (never an amount, a currency, or a status — verified by `guest.controller.spec.ts`) with one link per participant to `GET /s/g/:groupToken/:seq` (`:seq` is a small position index, not a bearer credential); that second route is the "You are «Name» — is that you?" confirm step (answers the wrong-tap risk WITHOUT touching the already-hardened `:token` handler at all) whose "Yes" link is the only place this whole code path reveals a real per-participant `/s/:token`. Not a new privacy boundary: the payer's pre-existing "Copy all links" button already bundles every name+token into one blob today — the QR is a nicer transport for data the app already lets the payer export in one shot. Mobile: `GroupQrModal.tsx` (bottom-sheet, `react-native-qrcode-svg` — already a dependency, same import shape as `app/settings/bots.tsx`'s WhatsApp QR) opened from a "Show QR" button on `ParticipantStatusList` (hidden when `groupUrl` is null); "Copy all links" stays for the async/remote case. `receiptSplit.showQr/qrTitle/qrHint/qrShare/qrShareMessage` × 9 locales. Zero new tables, zero change to the settle/claim security model. See `docs/contracts/qr-code-bill-split-{api,mobile}.md`.
- **Smart Shopping List (ABA-330)**: `modules/shopping-list/` — shared, offline-first shopping lists + a Pro-gated "where's cheapest" basket comparison built on the receipt price-history corpus (M1–M6; specs/plans in `docs/superpowers/`). **Data model**: `shopping_lists` + `shopping_list_items` (`@@unique([accountId, clientId])`, soft-delete + `sync_version`, cascade FKs; migration `20260707173751_add_shopping_lists`). REST CRUD (`shopping-list.controller.ts`, `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`) resolves list/item by `OR:[{id},{clientId}]` so offline-first clients address rows by their local `clientId`; direct-POST create is idempotent on `clientId` (ABA-316). `getLists` lazily materializes a default list ONLY when the account has **zero lists total** (brand-new onboarding) — **NOT** when it merely has zero *non-archived* lists — and **returns archived lists** so a cross-device archive is distinguishable from a delete (omitting them once caused an account-wide silent delete — the merge tombstoned then `deleteList`-pushed the still-present list). **Archive-to-empty-state (ABA-348)**: the old "materialize when zero non-archived" condition **un-archived the same `default-{accountId}` list** whenever you archived your last list, so an archived list popped back with all its items on the next pull ("archive it, it disappears then comes back"). Now archiving every list leaves an empty "create a list" state (mobile `shoppingList.noLists`/`createFirstList`; the restock/deals strips + bottom add-bar hide when `lists.length === 0`). Deleting (not archiving) the last list still materializes a fresh default (zero-total case, unchanged). Item writes are NOT `ViewerBlockGuard`-gated (collaborative); `deleteList` is. **Basket compare (Pro)**: pure `computeBasket` (`price-history/basket-calculator.ts`) — per-store latest price, coverage-gated "cheapest" badge (full-coverage else best ≥80%), stale-price flag, majority-currency; `POST /price-history/basket` (`SubscriptionTierGuard` + `@RequireTier('pro')`) accepts an optional `{lat,lng}` origin → per-store `distanceKm`/`nearby` via haversine (M4; `(0,0)` = null-island skipped). Store coordinates derived from the most-recent geo-tagged expense per merchant (widened `fetchRows` select). **Restock (M3)**: pure `predictRestock` (median gap between purchases, ≥3 points) → free `GET /shopping-list/suggestions` (excludes already-listed). **Deals (M6)**: pure `detectDeals` (a store's recent price ≥15% below the product's 90-day avg, within a 14-day window) → free `GET /shopping-list/deals` (excludes already-listed). All three pure functions mirror each other and are unit-tested. **Cron**: `shopping-reminder.cron.ts` (`0 10 * * *`) sends `shopping_reminder` (top due restock) and `shopping_deal` (top price drop) pushes per account; the `accountMember` query filters only `pushToken`/`isActive` and relies on `NotificationsService.sendToUser`'s per-type gate (`notifyShoppingReminders`/`notifyShoppingDeals`, both `Boolean @default(true)` columns + `GET/PATCH /users/me/notification-preferences` + 9-lang `notification-i18n`), so deal-only opt-ins still get deal pushes. `NotificationType += 'shopping_reminder' | 'shopping_deal'`. **Push de-duplication (ABA-350)**: the cron previously re-sent the top restock + top deal **every day** (level-triggered `predictRestock` keeps a frequently-bought staple perpetually overdue → "buy bread" every morning). Now every send is gated through `ShoppingNotificationLedger` (Prisma-only leaf, `shopping-notification-ledger.service.ts` — since ABA-455 a one-constructor-line subclass of the generic `NotificationDedupLedger`, `common/utils/notification-dedup-ledger.ts`, shared with `InsightNotificationLedger` below) + the pure `shopping-notification-dedup.util.ts` (`restockDedupKey`/`dealDedupKey`/`weekBucket`): **restock fires once per purchase cycle** (dedupKey `restock:{name}:{lastPurchase}` — re-fires only after a repurchase changes `lastPurchase`), **deals once per product+merchant+ISO-week** (`deal:{name}:{merchant}:{mondayISO}`), both first gated by a soft per-account per-type global floor `SHOPPING_REMINDER_MIN_GAP_DAYS` (default 2, `0` disables). Send-state lives in new table `shopping_notification_log` (`@@unique([accountId, dedupKey])`, `@@index([accountId, type, sentAt])`, cascade FK; migration `20260717120000_add_shopping_notification_log`) — dedup is **insert-and-catch-`P2002`** (anomaly-alerts convention, NO `$transaction`); `ledger.tryRecord` → `false` on P2002 → skip send. Daily `cleanupOldLogs()` `@Cron('0 3 * * *')` deletes rows >90 days. The whole per-account loop body is try/catch-wrapped so one account's DB error can't abort the rest of the run. Preference gates + notification types unchanged; **no mobile/i18n change** (user-facing docs in `user_docs/*/38-shopping-list.md` updated to drop the stale "daily" wording). **Mobile**: offline-first `shoppingListStore` + `shoppingListSync.ts` — SQLite `shopping_lists`/`shopping_list_items` mirror (`shoppingListRepository`/`shoppingListItemRepository`, per-row `sync_status`; the app does NOT use the generic `/sync/push|pull` machinery). Pull-merge = load-local → push-pending via REST CRUD → pull-full via `GET /shopping-list` → merge upsert + tombstone-by-absence; **local row `id` = `clientId` permanently** (never adopts the server PK); **mark synced ONLY on the server ack** (marking before the ack caused a hydrate-during-create silent delete); the pending-sweep pushes `createList` THEN `updateList` so an offline rename/archive isn't reverted; `mergeServerLists` skips upserting still-pending clientIds (no stale-server clobber). **Archived rows in the merge (ABA-348)**: `getAllShoppingLists(accountId, includeArchived=false)` — the pull-merge passes `includeArchived=true` so a locally-archived, still-pending list is present in the merge's local snapshot and thus protected by the pending-guard from a stale server copy that still reports it un-archived (`_doPullAndMerge` loads the FULL set for the merge context, displays only the non-archived filter). Screens `app/shopping-list/{index,compare,map}.tsx` (list + switcher + "time to restock"/"deals" strips with dismiss-on-tap; Pro-gated compare via `useUpgradeStore`; store map on the generic `ExpenseMapView` with a cheapest/nearby toggle, live GPS via `captureCurrentLocation({force:true})`); entry via home quick action `shopping` + Settings hub + Analytics "Plan a shop" + push deep-links. `shoppingList.*` (39 keys) + `notifications.shopping*` toggles across all 9 locales. Pure fns + services/controller/cron have Jest suites; `computeBasket`/`predictRestock`/`detectDeals` are the tested cores. **Screen decomposition (ABA-352)**: `app/shopping-list/index.tsx` was a 994-line god file mixing the list view with three inline modals; decomposed into `src/hooks/useProductSearch.ts` (query/products/loadingProducts + derived filtered/frequent/exact-match) and three presentational components under `src/components/shopping-list/` — `AddItemModal.tsx` (product search sheet, owns `useProductSearch`), `ListSwitcherModal.tsx` (list picker + rename/archive/delete), `ListNameModal.tsx` (create/rename name entry, exports `NameModalState`) — each with its own `createStyles`, mirroring the `src/components/home/*` pattern. `index.tsx` is now 598 lines, a thin composition; no behavior change. **Regrowth fix (ABA-429)**: the file grew back to 774 lines once Shopping Mode (ABA-408, see below) was bolted on inline — its own state/handlers extracted the same way into `src/hooks/useShoppingModeButton.ts` (`active`/`toggle`, busy-ref guard, focus + `AppState` refresh listeners, the four not-ready/no-shops/permission/notification-permission `Alert` branches) and `src/components/shopping-list/ShoppingModeButton.tsx` (owns its own `createStyles`); `index.tsx` is back down to 615 lines. Any future addition to this screen should extend one of these hooks/components, not grow `index.tsx` inline again.
- **Store Arrival card + Shopping Mode live on `feature/shopping-mode`, NOT on `development` (ABA-438)**: the pair (ABA-404 home card, ABA-408 foreground-service location session, ABA-437 branch clustering) was lifted off the mainline because `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION` in `AndroidManifest.xml` makes Google Play refuse the **track update** for every submit until the Foreground service permissions declaration is filled in Play Console — so one unreleased feature froze the release pipeline for the whole app. Removed from `development` by a single forward commit (no history rewrite): the feature-only files, the `storeArrival` `WidgetKey` and its `HomeWidgetSwitch` case, the location-task registration in `index.js`, the sweep/list-sync hooks in `app/_layout.tsx`, the sign-out stop in `authStore`, the button on `app/shopping-list/index.tsx`, both manifest permissions, the `shoppingMode`/`storeArrival` i18n objects in all 9 locales, and the Shopping Mode section of `user_docs/*/38-shopping-list.md`. `expo-location`/`expo-task-manager` stay in `package.json` — `locationCapture` still needs the former and the branch needs both back. To re-land: fill the Play declaration first, then merge the branch and revert that removal commit; do NOT cherry-pick the 40 original commits, they are interleaved with unrelated ABA work.
- **Expense geo-location & map (ABA-310)**: expenses carry `location: {lat, lng, name?}` (server columns `locationLat/locationLng` existed dormant since early schema + new `locationName`, migration `20260703161013_add_expense_location_name_and_geocode_cache`). **Geocoding**: `modules/ai/services/geocoding.service.ts` — Nominatim + `geocode_cache` table (`@@unique(queryNormalized)`; lat/lng NULL = **negative cache** for "no match"; transient errors NOT cached), identifying User-Agent, in-process ≥1.1s serialization (single API container), 5s timeout, **fail-silent** (returns null on any failure — receipt scanning must never break). Registered in the standalone leaf `GeocodingModule` (`modules/ai/geocoding.module.ts`, no imports of its own), imported by both `AiModule` and `CommunityPriceModule` so there is exactly ONE DI-managed `GeocodingService` instance app-wide — required because the throttle is instance-level state; a second instance would pace its own gap independently and could together exceed Nominatim's 1 req/s policy (see `docs/tech-debt/geocoding-service-duplicate-instance-breaks-throttle.md`). Consumed by `OcrService.buildReceiptExpense` (async) and `CommunityPriceService` (reverse-geocode). **Structured geocoding (ABA-311)**: `geocodeStructured({street, city, postalCode, country})` uses Nominatim's **structured** endpoint (separate `street`/`city`/`postalcode`/`country` params, not free-text `q=`) — far more robust on Polish receipts that print the store address AND the company's registered seat (Biedronka → Jeronimo Martins HQ in Kostrzyn); the noisy blended `q=` string returns no match and negative-caches. `normalizeStreetForGeocode` **strips the leading street-type prefix** (`ul.`/`al.`/`pl.`/`os.`/Cyrillic `вул.`/`ул.`/`пр.`…) because Nominatim's structured `street` wants "`<number> <name>`" — `street=ul. Wojska Polskiego 1` returns nothing, `street=Wojska Polskiego 1` hits the exact building. **Two-tier**: tier-1 full (street+city/postcode) → exact building; on no-match, tier-2 drops the street → town/postcode centroid (always a pin when the city resolves). Structured cache keys are `struct|street=…|city=…|postalcode=…|country=…` (disjoint from free-text keys). `geocode(address)` (free-text) kept as the fallback when the OCR yields no structured parts. `buildReceiptExpense` prefers structured (`merchantStreet/City/PostalCode/Country` from the OCR prompt) and falls back to free-text `merchantAddress`; the OCR prompt instructs the model to extract the STORE (point-of-sale) address only and ignore the registered seat/legal name. `POST /ai/scan-receipt` returns `location: {lat, lng, name} | null` where `name` = clean composed store address (`composeAddressName`: "street, postcode city"), falling back to raw `merchantAddress`. **Write path**: `buildLocationColumns(location)` (`modules/expenses/expense-location.util.ts`, unit-tested) spread into ALL THREE Prisma write sites — create branch, create-upsert **update branch** (previously dropped location on offline retry — same bug class as trip-wallet `paidByUserId`), and `update()`; semantics: `undefined` untouched / `null` clears all three / object-without-name clears stale name (manual pin move invalidates the geocoded label). `UpdateExpenseDto.location?: LocationDto | null` — explicit `null` = "remove location". `SyncExpensePayload.location` (nested, flows through sync.service's `...payload` spread). **E2EE**: `locationLat/locationLng` in `ENCRYPTION_FIELDS.expense.tier2` (numeric tier — `decryptFromSync` restores numbers ONLY for tier2 fields; tier1 would return strings), `locationName` was already tier1; create-path push rebuilds the outgoing `location` from the maybeEncrypt payload with NO plaintext fallback for the name (encrypted string → null → omitted). Known inherited gap: the mobile update path doesn't re-encrypt (pin edits push plaintext for E2EE accounts and a stale `encryptedPayload` can revert them on next pull) — pre-existing pattern shared with all field edits. **Mobile capture**: `src/services/locationCapture.ts` (`captureCurrentLocation` — Platform web→null, opt-in toggle gate unless `{force:true}`, permission check, 4s `Promise.race` timeout, `console.warn` never error) + MMKV `locationSettingsStore` (id `location-settings`, default OFF) + toggle in `settings/data.tsx` (permission requested at enable-time, denial keeps it OFF; hidden on web). Source priority: **manual pin > OCR address > silent GPS**; GPS wired via mount-refs (never delays save) into `expense/new.tsx`, `voice.tsx`, `receipt.tsx` (fallback when scan has no address) and awaited in `notificationCapture/captureService.ts` (phone is at the store when the bank push arrives); imports NEVER get location. Pull merge: `parseServerLocation` (`src/utils/location.ts`) — server sends flat Prisma-Decimal STRINGS, mobile rebuilds the nested object (Phase C); SQLite repository already handled nested location end-to-end. **Map — zero native modules** (`react-native-maps` deliberately rejected: native + API key + Windows MAX_PATH codegen risk): `src/components/map/ExpenseMapView.tsx` = WebView hosting inlined Leaflet+markercluster (`npm run generate:map-html` from apps/mobile regenerates `mapHtml.generated.ts` — NEVER edit by hand; script reads the monorepo ROOT node_modules where leaflet hoists; OSM raster tiles + attribution; popups built via DOM `textContent`, XSS-safe; **default marker icon PNGs embedded as base64 data URIs** (`L.Icon.Default.mergeOptions` — Leaflet's CSS references the marker images by relative URL which don't resolve in the single self-contained document, so without this the pin renders at the right spot but with NO icon image); `.web.tsx` variant renders the same document in an `<iframe srcDoc>`. Bridge: `window.__configure/__setPoints/__setView/__setPicker/__setPickerPin`, messages `ready/open/mapPress`; **readyTick counter + payload-diff guard** — unchanged payloads are NOT re-injected (unrelated parent re-renders would reset pan/zoom via clearLayers+fitBounds) but a recreated WebView process (new `ready`) always re-syncs. `buildExpenseMapPoints(expenses)` → `{points, missingCount}`; **(0,0) = "null island"** convention: the zeroed plaintext of an undecryptable E2EE tier-2 row, treated as no-location everywhere. **Surfaces**: Expenses tab List/Map icon toggle (map inherits ALL period/category/merchant filters — `ExpenseFilterBar` stays visible; `view=map&mapKey=<nonce>` params — the nonce is REQUIRED because an unchanged param value doesn't re-fire the effect); expense detail `LocationSection` (`app/expense/components/`, mini-map `interactive={false}`, one-tap remove via `updateExpense(id, {location: null})`, add/edit → picker, canEdit-gated); manual pin picker `app/expense/location.tsx` (modal + header, 4-way id resolution like `expense/[id]`, tap-to-place + draggable pin + My-location `{force:true}`, save sends coords WITHOUT name; **address search (ABA-312)**: debounced search bar → `GET /ai/geocode/search?q=` → `GeocodingService.search` (Nominatim `limit=5`, shared throttle/User-Agent, Redis-cached via optional `CacheService` key `geo:search:{q}` TTL 1h, fail-silent `[]`, no `AiUsageGuard` — free) → results dropdown; picking a result sets+recenters the pin AND saves the result's address as `location.name` (hand-placed/tap/GPS pins stay name-less). `location.searchPlaceholder/searchNoResults` in 9 locales); trip account "Trip map" row (`account/[id].tsx` — **`switchAccount(id)` first** when the viewed trip isn't active, else the map would show the wrong account). i18n: `location.*` (11 keys) + `map.*` (5, Slavic `_one/_few/_many` plurals) + `trip.tripMap` × 9 locales. `Expense.location` entity type widened to `| null` (matches `paidByUserId` precedent). Follow-ups tracked in ABA-310: offline location-CLEAR lost on retry path, cache upsert race, `require.resolve` in build script, in-screen canEdit guard. **Bot receipt geocode (ABA-329)**: Telegram/WhatsApp/Slack `handlers/photo.handler.ts` now carry `receipt.location` through the `PendingReceiptData` payload (image + PDF paths) into `expensesService.create()` (`location: data.location ?? undefined`), so bot-scanned receipts land on the map — previously the geocoded location was silently dropped (the "bot photo geocode flag" gap). Bots have no device GPS, so the receipt store address is the only source; the date-edit read-modify-write preserves `location` for free. Tests: `handlers/photo.handler.spec.ts` per bot. **Proximity search + near-me picker + recents (ABA-333)**: `GET /ai/geocode/search` now takes optional `lat`/`lng` (`GeocodingService.search(query, origin?)`) → biases Nominatim toward a ~150km `viewbox` (`bounded=0`, far matches still return) and re-sorts candidates by proximity (`rankDistSq`, cos-lat-scaled planar distance); the Redis cache key includes the rounded origin. Backward compatible (no origin = old behavior). `expense/location.tsx` silently captures current GPS on mount via `captureCurrentLocation({force:true})` (used to center the map near the user when *adding* a location — an existing-location edit still centers on its pin — and to bias the search); picking a search result saves it to `recentPlacesStore` (MMKV `id:'recent-places'`, last 8, dedupe by name, pure `loadRecentPlaces`/`addToRecentPlaces` helpers) — an empty search box shows a "Recent" list, and while searching a matching recent is surfaced above fresh results (`location.recentSearches` i18n × 9). **Web geolocation (ABA-333)**: `captureCurrentLocation` gained a web branch (`captureWebLocation` → `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition`, never throws, null on error/denial/unsupported/4s-timeout) so near-me centering + proximity search work in the web app; the toggle-gate check moved BEFORE the platform split so non-`force` callers (silent expense-add capture) still no-op on web while explicit `force:true` map contexts get GPS; `requestLocationPermission` returns `true` on web when the API exists (the browser shows its own one-time prompt on capture).
- **App version gate**: `modules/app-versions/` — public `GET /app-versions/check?platform=ios|android&version=x.y.z` (no auth, mobile calls before login) returns `{latestVersion, minSupportedVersion, isUpdateAvailable, isUpdateRequired, releaseNotes, storeUrl}`. Admin CRUD under `/admin/app-versions` (behind `JwtAuthGuard + AdminGuard`). Latest row per platform = `ORDER BY publishedAt DESC LIMIT 1`. Service enforces `latestVersion >= minSupportedVersion` via `utils/semver.ts`. Default Play Store URL hardcoded; iOS storeUrl is a placeholder until App Store ID is assigned (admin overrides per row).
- **Subscription pricing (ABA-320, consolidated in tech-debt `pricing-table-triple-copy`)**: current Pro = **$4.99/mo, $29.99/yr** (cut from $9.99/$95.88 to improve free→paid conversion); Business unchanged at $19.99/mo, $191.88/yr. **Single source of truth**: `apps/api/src/modules/subscriptions/pricing-data.json` (amounts in minor units + display symbol, per currency). Three consumers import/read it directly — no more hand-synced copies: `subscriptions.service.ts` (`import * as pricingData from './pricing-data.json'` — needs `resolveJsonModule: true` in `apps/api/tsconfig.json`; use a **namespace import**, not a default import, since the API has no `esModuleInterop` and a default import compiles to `.default` which is `undefined` for a plain `require()`'d JSON module) drives `getPlans()`, i.e. what the app displays; `apps/api/src/modules/subscriptions/scripts/setup-stripe-products.ts` derives its lowercase-currency-keyed `CURRENCIES` from the same file (used only when actually re-provisioning Stripe); `docs/marketing/landing/build_landing.py` `json.load()`s the same file directly (converting minor→major units) to build `CURRENCY_PRICING`, which drives the static `/pricing/` marketing page — regenerate with `LANDING_BASE= ROBOTS="index,follow,max-image-preview:large" python docs/marketing/landing/build_landing.py` (the **default env-less invocation builds a `noindex` preview variant** that will silently overwrite the committed production site with wrong robots/base-path if you forget the env vars). A price-amount change now touches exactly one file. `subscriptions.service.spec.ts` has a `pricing-data.json` describe-block asserting the JSON's shape and that `getPlans()` reads through it (fails fast on a malformed edit instead of shipping quietly broken pricing). **The displayed price and the price Stripe actually charges remain two independent systems** — `getPlans()`'s amounts are cosmetic; the real charge comes from a Stripe Price object whose ID is read from an env var (`STRIPE_{PRO,BUSINESS}_{MONTHLY,YEARLY}_PRICE_ID_{CURRENCY}`) in `resolvePriceId()`/`createCheckoutSession` — this was deliberately NOT folded into `pricing-data.json` (env-driven, per-environment test/live IDs, not a build-time constant). Stripe Prices are immutable — changing a real live price requires (1) running `setup-stripe-products.ts` (creates brand-new Products + Prices every run, NOT idempotent — do not re-run casually) with a live `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, ideally executed **inside the prod API container** (`docker cp` the script in, `docker exec -w /app/apps/api budget-api-prod node script.js`) so the live secret never has to leave the server; (2) archiving the old Products/Prices (`active:false` — Stripe has no real delete; archiving only blocks *new* checkouts, existing subscriptions keep renewing at the old price untouched); (3) updating all 24 `STRIPE_*_PRICE_ID_*` lines in `.env.production` (back it up first) with the new IDs; (4) `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env.production up -d --force-recreate api`; (5) editing `pricing-data.json`'s amounts so the displayed price catches up with what's actually being charged — until that deploy lands there's a window where Stripe already charges the new price but the app still displays the old one. A stray un-tagged legacy Stripe product from an earlier pricing iteration (no `tier` metadata, incomplete currency coverage) was found during the ABA-320 migration and deliberately left alone rather than guessed at — when hunting for "the old Pro/Business product" to archive, match on `metadata.tier` AND verify the price amounts against the pricing table you're retiring, since `products.list().find()` can grab the wrong same-tier product if more than one exists. The yearly-discount **badge text must not hardcode a percentage** (landing `toggle_save` key, mobile `subscription.tsx`) — Pro and Business now have different yearly discount ratios (~69% vs 20%), so the landing badge is fixed non-numeric copy ("Save with yearly") and the mobile badge computes `Math.round((1 - plan.yearly.amount/(plan.monthly.amount*12))*100)` live from `getPlans()`'s response instead.
- **Invite existing users by search (ABA-309)**: lets an account owner search for an already-registered user by name/email and invite them directly, instead of only by email/code/link. **Data model**: `AccountInvitation.invitedUserId String?` — plain field mirroring `invitedBy`/`acceptedBy` (no Prisma `@relation`), migration `20260703122200_add_invitation_invited_user_id`.
