"My Invitations" (the invitee's view) matches any pending invitation where `invitedUserId = currentUserId` OR `invitedEmail = currentUser.email` — folds in existing email-based invites too, when the typed email happens to match a registered user. **Endpoints**: `GET /users/search?q=<term>` (`UsersController`) — case-insensitive substring match on name/email, min 2 chars, capped at 20, excludes requester + deactivated users, returns only `{id, name, email}`; guarded by `ThrottlerGuard` + `@Throttle({default:{limit:20,ttl:60000}})` — the first route in this codebase to actually consume the globally-configured-but-previously-unused `ThrottlerModule`. `CreateInvitationDto.invitedUserId` — when set, `AccountsService.createInvitation` validates the user isn't already a member, creates the invitation, and sends a **push** (not an email) via `NotificationsService.sendToUser(..., 'account_invitation')`. `GET /accounts/invitations/mine` — current user's pending invitations, joined with inviter name + account name + role. `PATCH /accounts/invitations/:id/respond` (`{action:'accept'|'decline'}`) — verifies the invitation is addressed to the responder (`invitedUserId` or `invitedEmail` match) **before** any other logic (closes an IDOR where an invitation id could be guessed and hijacked), then enforces `expiresAt` the same way `acceptInvitation` already does (search-based invitations have no cron to expire them otherwise — the check must live in this handler too, or a past-expiry invitation stays acceptable forever). `getMyInvitations` must short-circuit `if (!me) return []` before building the `OR` filter — otherwise a stale/deleted-user JWT causes Prisma to silently drop the `undefined invitedEmail` clause, leaving an `OR` that matches every pending invitation system-wide. New `NotificationType = 'account_invitation'` is **always sent, with no per-type preference toggle** (unlike every other notification type) — a direct one-off action request, not a recurring background alert; `notification-i18n.ts` has `accountInvitationTitle`/`accountInvitationBody` in all 9 languages. **Mobile**: `invitationStore.ts` (server-only, optimistic accept/decline with rollback — mirrors `purchaseRequestStore`'s pattern); `app/alerts/index.tsx` gained a tab control (Alerts / Invitations) + new `InvitationCard` component (Accept/Decline buttons — a new interaction pattern for this screen, previously tap-to-navigate/dismiss-only); the bell badge sums `unreadCount + invitations.length`, and `loadInvitations()` runs alongside `loadAlerts()` in both real call sites of `useHomeScreenData.ts` (focus effect + pull-to-refresh) so the badge is populated even before the user opens the Alerts screen — do NOT reintroduce a badge sum that reads only `useAlertStore`. `account_invitation` push deep-links to `/alerts?tab=invitations`. `account/invite.tsx` gained a third "Find user" mode alongside Link/Email — debounced (~300ms) search, selecting a result reveals the existing role picker, success screen shows no invite code (nothing to share, already delivered via push). 13 i18n keys (7 `alerts.*`, 6 `accounts.*`) across all 9 locales.
- **Budget period util** (ABA-171): `modules/budgets/budget-period.util.ts` — single source of truth for `computeBudgetPeriod(budget, now?)` in the API (Monday-based week). Both `budgets.service.ts` and `budget-alert.service.ts` import from here. `packages/shared-utils/src/formatting/index.ts` exports the same function for mobile/admin bundles; `budgetStore.ts` uses it instead of its former inline switch. Do NOT add a fourth copy — extend this util if the logic ever needs to change.
- **Financial month anchor, Wave 1 = budgets only (ABA-383)**: a per-account, nullable `Account.monthAnchorDay Int?` (1..31; `null` = calendar month; migration `20260801120000_add_account_month_anchor`) lets an account shift what "this month" means for budget periods (salary on the 10th → periods run 10th–9th). It is a pure re-bucketing lens — no expense/income data is touched, only which period a budget computation assigns it to; setting it applies **retroactively** to past periods too. **Util**: `normalizeAnchorDay`/`financialMonth`/`shiftFinancialMonth`/`formatFinancialMonth`, clamping an anchor day above a given month's length to that month's last day (e.g. day 31 in February → Feb 28/29). Duplicated deliberately, same reason as every other API/mobile util pair in this codebase (the API has no build step and can't import `@budget/shared-utils` at runtime): canonical copy `apps/api/src/common/utils/financial-month.ts`, mirror `packages/shared-utils/src/formatting/financial-month.ts` (re-exported for mobile/admin). Same case table on both sides — change one, change the other. **API wiring**: validated via the API-local `UpdateAccountDto` (`modules/accounts/dto/index.ts`, `IsInt`+`Min(1)`+`Max(31)`+`ValidateIf(v!==null)` so an explicit `null` reset still validates) on `PATCH /accounts/:id`, owner-only via the existing `validateAccess(..., 'owner')` in `AccountsService.update`. `AccountContextMiddleware` resolves `req.monthAnchorDay` once per request (mirrors `req.accountId`/`req.accountRole`) for `BudgetsController`'s `getProgress`/`getHistory`; `BudgetAlertService`'s daily cron has no request in scope so it reads `budget.account.monthAnchorDay` off the account relation it already includes. `BudgetsService.getAccountAnchorDay(accountId)` is the third read path — a DB-only lookup for callers with an accountId but no request (the AI chat `get_budget_status` tool in `ai-tools.service.ts`). All three converge on the same `financialMonth()`/`shiftFinancialMonth()` math, so a budget's progress number and its period label agree everywhere it's shown. **Mobile**: `apps/mobile/src/hooks/useFinancialMonth.ts` — the one place screens read the anchor + current period from; wraps `useAccountStore`'s `currentAccount().monthAnchorDay` with a day-bucketed `useMemo` so a screen left open across midnight recomputes on the next render instead of serving a stale window. Budget detail's ← → period navigation (`src/features/budgets/periodNav.ts`) steps whole anchored periods via `shiftFinancialMonth`, not calendar months. `accountRepository.ts`/`client.native.ts`/SQLite `accounts` table carry `monthAnchorDay` (omitted field on update = untouched, explicit `null` = cleared). **Settings UI**: owner-only "Financial month" row on `app/account/[id].tsx` (gated by the same `account.myRole === 'owner'` check the rest of that screen uses) opens a bottom sheet — backdrop/handle-bar/Cancel-Save, mirroring `settings/merchants.tsx`'s modal — listing "Calendar month" (sets `null`) then days 1–31, with the retroactive-change hint always visible and a "short months clamp to the last day" hint when the picked day is above 28; saves via the existing `accountStore.updateAccount()` action. 6 `accounts.financialMonth*` i18n keys × 9 locales. **Explicitly NOT migrated in Wave 1** (still calendar-month, on purpose — the constraint is that server and client sides of one number migrate together): analytics, home screen totals, the calendar widget, safe-to-spend, Financial Wrapped/story, Fat Finder, reports, and the AI chat user-context summary. Planned for waves 2 and 3.
- **Budget category threshold alerts** (ABA-314): `modules/budgets/budget-alert.service.ts` — `checkCategoryThresholds(accountId, budget, periodStart, periodEnd)` private method fires push notifications when a per-category allocation crosses the same `[50, 80, 100]` thresholds as the overall budget check. Triggered at the end of `checkBudgetThresholds` (same call site as the overall pass). Uses a single `expense.groupBy({ by: ['categoryId'] })` query per budget, builds a `spentMap`, then loops allocations × thresholds. Dedup via `findFirst({ budgetId, categoryId, thresholdPercentage, periodStart })` + `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })` — relies on two partial unique indexes in Postgres (`budget_alert_overall_unique` WHERE category_id IS NULL; `budget_alert_category_unique` WHERE category_id IS NOT NULL — migrations `20260702152451` + FK `20260702160000`). `BudgetAlert.categoryId String? @map("category_id")` with `category Category? @relation(..., onDelete: SetNull)`. Existing overall-alert `findFirst` calls scoped to `categoryId: null` to prevent cross-type matches. Mark-before-send + rollback pattern identical to overall alerts. Reuses `'budget_alert'` notification type and `notifyBudgetAlerts` preference — no new type, no new toggle. i18n: `budgetCategoryThresholdTitle/Body` (`threshold < 100`) and `budgetCategoryExceededTitle/Body` (`threshold >= 100`) in all 9 locales. Push data payload includes `categoryId` and `categoryName`. `checkBudgetsForAccount` include updated to `categoryAllocations: { where: { isDeleted: false }, include: { category: true } }` for category name access. Tests: `budget-alert.service.spec.ts` (7 tests).
- **Budget history**: `GET /budgets/:id/history?periods=6` returns chronological `BudgetHistoryEntry[]` ({periodStart, periodEnd, limit, actual, isOverBudget}). Uses `computeBudgetPeriod` to iterate the last N periods (max 12). Returns `[]` for `custom`-period budgets. Category / currency filters mirror `getProgress`. Mobile stores result in `budgetStore.budgetHistory[budgetId]` (in-memory, fetched on budget detail screen mount).
- **Budget category model**: `Budget` has **no** `categoryId` field. Category filtering is done exclusively through `BudgetCategory` allocations (`budget_categories` table). An overall budget (no category filter) has zero `BudgetCategory` rows. A single-category budget has exactly one row with `amount = budget.amount`. Multi-category budgets have multiple rows. The legacy `category_id` column was removed via migration `20260523000000_drop_budget_category_id` (backfill + drop). Mobile `budgets` SQLite table still has a dead `category_id` column on existing devices but it is never read or written.
- **Account transfers (ABA-388)**: `modules/account-transfers/` — moving money between two accounts the user belongs to. **Balance display**: `GET /wallet/summaries` (on `WalletController`; the class-level `AccountContextGuard` stays but the handler **ignores `req.accountId`** and enumerates membership from `req.user.id`) returns `AllWalletSummariesResponse { accounts: [{ accountId, balances }] }` — the transfer form has to show the *other* account's balance, which `GET /wallet/summary` cannot do. `WalletService.getSummariesForAccounts` runs the same six aggregates as `getSummary` with `accountId: { in: [...] }` grouped by `accountId + currencyCode`, so it stays **six queries regardless of account count**; it carries the same two invariants (`EXCLUDE_SPLIT_RECEIVABLE` on expenses; incoming transfers only when `countAsIncome: false`, else the linked `Income` double-counts them). The balance arithmetic lives in pure `wallet/wallet-balance.util.ts` (`buildWalletBalanceRow`) called by **both** methods — do not inline a second copy, a transfer form quoting a different balance than the wallet screen is worse than no balance. No migration. Computing other accounts' balances from the mobile SQLite mirror was **rejected**: an account the user has never opened has no local rows (and web has no SQLite), so it would silently read too low. **Editing accounts**: `UpdateAccountTransferDto` carries `fromAccountId`/`toAccountId`/`fromCurrency`/`toCurrency` — currencies travel *with* the accounts, since re-homing a transfer while keeping the old currency stores a meaningless row. `update()` re-validates via `assertCanTransferBetween` (shared with `create` so permission rules can't drift), requires the **request's account to remain a party** (`findAll` filters on `fromAccountId`/`toAccountId`, so moving both sides away hides the row from the account that just edited it), and **moves the linked `Income` to the new `accountId`/currency** when `countAsIncome` is on. **Mobile**: `walletStore.accountSummaries` + `loadAccountSummaries()` (MMKV-cached for instant paint, cleared in `reset()` so logout doesn't leak the previous user's figures — any test importing `walletStore` now needs the per-file `react-native-mmkv` mock); pure unit-tested `src/features/wallet/transferBalances.ts` (current account resolves from the exact, offline-capable local `walletSummary` first, others from the server map; unknown → `null` → em dash, **never a fabricated zero**) and `frequentTransfers.ts` (history grouped by `from|to|fromCurrency|toCurrency`, ranked count-then-recency, top 3, drops routes touching a deleted account or paying from a viewer account). `wallet/transfer.tsx` shows frequent-route chips, per-chip balances, an `Available:` line + `Max` button keyed to the **selected `fromCurrency`** (not the account's default — that's the currency the amount is typed in), and an over-balance **warning that never blocks submit** (a tracker records transfers after the fact, and an account whose initial balance was never set looks emptier than it is). Chip prefill uses two `useRef` guards so the account-change effects don't immediately overwrite the chip's currencies. Date fields on create + edit go through `src/components/DatePicker.tsx` (never `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` directly — ABA-381). **Known gaps**: `findAll` filters by `userId`, so in a shared account a transfer made by another member is invisible to everyone else; other accounts' balances need network on first use.
- **Wallet currency visibility (ABA-431)**: the wallet shows a card for every currency an account actually holds money in, not only the ones somebody set an initial balance for. It used to render exactly one card per `wallet_balances` row, and that table is written **only** by the "set balance" screen — so a production shared account with **124 484 USD** of income showed just its PLN card, and 36 of the ~43 account+currency pairs holding money prod-wide had no row at all (the bug was the normal state, not an edge case). The same `.map()` over wallet rows existed in **three** places and all three are fixed: `WalletService.getSummary` (wallet screen), `WalletService.getSummariesForAccounts` (the transfer form's `Available:` line, ABA-388) and **`walletStore.computeWalletSummary` — the mobile app computes its own summary locally, so that copy is what the phone actually renders**. Meanwhile `getMonthlyBalanceHistory` had always derived its currency set from the transactions, which is why one screen could show USD in the monthly chart while having no USD balance card. **Rule**: visible = live rows ∪ currencies with movements that have **no row at all**. A currency whose only row is **soft-deleted stays hidden even with movements** — that row is the user having hidden it (`WalletService.remove`), and hiding must survive the next transaction in it; this is exactly why the set is not simply re-derived from movements on every read, and why the existence lookup must NOT filter `isDeleted`. Pure `resolveWalletCurrencies` is a **deliberately duplicated pair** (canonical `apps/api/src/common/utils/wallet-currencies.ts`, mirror `packages/shared-utils/src/formatting/wallet-currencies.ts` consumed by mobile's `features/wallet/walletSummary.ts`) — same convention and same reason as `financial-month.ts`: the API has no build step and must not import `@budget/shared-utils` at runtime. Same rule table on both sides. **Persistence**: `WalletCurrencyService.ensureCurrencies(accountId, userId | undefined, codes)` (`modules/wallet/wallet-currency.service.ts`) creates the missing row at `initialAmount 0`. Prisma-only leaf in its own tiny `WalletCurrencyModule` so the write paths can depend on it without a module cycle (the `InflationShieldTrackingService` shape); idempotent via `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })`; **never throws** (every caller is fire-and-forget); **must be called OUTSIDE any `$transaction`** — a constraint violation aborts the whole Postgres transaction (ABA-313/401). `wallet_balances.user_id` is NOT NULL and the read path does not always know who is asking (`SafeToSpendService` calls `getSummary` with no user), so it falls back to the account owner — **filtered** on `role: 'owner'`, never `orderBy: { role }`, since alphabetically `editor` sorts ahead of `owner`. **Called from both sides on purpose**: on write from `ExpenseCreatedHooksService.onExpenseCreated` (the documented home for post-create hooks, ABA-425) and `IncomesService.create`, **and** fire-and-forget from the read path itself — so a write path nobody instrumented (import commits, the subscription auto-charge cron, debts, anything added later) still displays correctly instead of silently reintroducing this bug. Do NOT drop the read-side derivation on the grounds that the writes now cover it. Migration `20260821120000_backfill_wallet_currency_rows` backfills the pre-existing pairs (data-only, mirrors the six money sources `getSummary` aggregates, `NOT EXISTS` without an `isDeleted` filter so a hidden currency is not resurrected); verified against prod inside a rolled-back transaction as `INSERT 0 36`.
- **Wise CSV import**: `modules/import-wise/` — `POST /import/wise/preview` (multipart CSV ≤5 MB, `papaparse`, strips BOM) classifies rows as `expense` (Amount<0) / `income` (Amount>0) / `fx` (paired conversion rows sharing `Payment Reference + Date + opposite sign`, emitted as `CurrencyExchange` not `AccountTransfer` since Wise FX is in-wallet), folds `Total fees` into amount, suggests categories via `MERCHANT_CATEGORY_HINTS`. `POST /import/wise/commit` writes in one Prisma `$transaction` with `source: 'import'`, `externalRef = 'wise:<TransferWise ID>'`. **Dedup**: `Expense`/`Income`/`CurrencyExchange` each have `externalRef String?` with `@@unique([accountId, externalRef])` (NULLs distinct in Postgres). Mobile `app/settings/wise-import.tsx` (5-state machine, `expo-document-picker`); reached via the `/settings/import` hub.
- **Polish bank import** (ABA-126): `modules/import-bank/` — strategy-registry module for CSV/PDF statement imports. Banks: `mbank`, `pko`, `revolut`, `ing`, `millennium`, `pekao` + `universal` column-mapping fallback. 5 endpoints under `/import/bank/*` (`JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`): `POST preview` (multipart ≤5 MB), `POST commit`, `GET/POST mappings`, `DELETE mappings/:id`. `ImportBankService` flow: `decodeCsvBuffer` (UTF-8/Windows-1250 auto-detect, `iconv-lite`) → parser dispatch (mappingId → bankId → saved-fingerprint → auto-detect) → normalized rows → `pairFxRows` (same date, opposite sign, different currency) → `buildExternalRef` → dedup. Per-bank parsers `parsers/*.parser.ts` implement `BankParser { id, displayName, detect(), parse() }`; `universal.detect()` always returns `false`. Helpers in `utils/`: `polish-amount.ts`, `polish-date.ts`, `encoding.ts`, `header-fingerprint.ts`, `fx-pairing.ts`, `pdf-text.ts`. `merchants/merchants-pl.ts` ~40 brand→category hints (`MERCHANTS_PL` + `suggestCategoryFromMerchantPL`). **Merchant name normalization** (ABA-254): the same file also exports `MERCHANT_CANONICAL_PL` (brand UPPERCASE substring → canonical display name, e.g. `BIEDRONKA → Biedronka`) + `normalizeMerchantPL(name)` (longest-key-first `includes` match, idempotent, passthrough for unknown/undefined). Applied in `buildPreviewResponse` (visible in preview) and re-applied defensively at commit so `BIEDRONKA 1234 WARSZAWA` lands as one `Biedronka` seller. `NETTO` is deliberately NOT in the canonical map (collides with the PL accounting term "kwota netto") — it stays only in the category-hint map. **Dedup key**: `bank:<bankId>:<isoDate>:<signedAmountCents>:<sha256(normalize(desc)).slice(0,8)>`. **Two dedup layers** in `buildPreviewResponse`: (1) exact `externalRef` match (re-import of same file); (2) `flagContentDuplicates` — content match on `(date, signedAmountCents, currency)` against ALL account Expense/Income regardless of source, greedy 1-to-1 multiset, excludes FX. Matched rows → `alreadyImported: true`, auto-unchecked in preview. `Expense.date`/`Income.date` are `@db.Date` so date match is exact. **Commit dedup / crash fix (ABA-313)**: `commit()` calls `dropDuplicateRows(accountId, toImport)` **before** opening the `$transaction` — removes externalRefs that already exist in the DB (expense/income/currencyExchange) AND intra-batch repeats. This is REQUIRED, not just an optimization: Postgres aborts the WHOLE transaction on the first `@@unique([accountId, externalRef])` violation (subsequent statements fail with `25P02`), so the old catch-P2002-and-`continue` pattern crashed the entire import (`PrismaClientUnknownRequestError`). The per-row `catch` now rethrows ALL errors (a poisoned tx can't continue) → clean rollback + client retry. Covers the gaps `alreadyImported` misses (intra-file repeats; rows imported between preview and commit). **Saved mappings**: `csv_import_mappings` table (migration `20260523100000_add_csv_import_mappings`, `@@unique([accountId, headerFingerprint])`); commit `saveMapping` field persists column mapping for auto-application. **PDF statements**: `BankParser.format?: 'csv'|'pdf'`; orchestrator detects `%PDF` header (`isPdfBuffer`), extracts text via `pdf-parse` (`extractPdfText`), dispatches to PDF parsers — CSV header/mapping/fingerprint skipped. `erste.parser.ts` and `alior.parser.ts` are PDF parsers (line state machines over the bank's "Wyciąg" table). **Request-a-bank**: `POST /import/bank/request-bank` (multipart `file?` + `bankName`, `notes?`) → forwards to **ops Telegram chat** (`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`, never the user) via `TelegramService.sendMessage`/`sendDocument`. Mobile: Settings → "Import transactions" → `app/settings/import/` (`index.tsx` hub, `preview.tsx`, `mapper.tsx`, `request-bank.tsx`); shared `useImportStore`; `bankImport.*` i18n (8 locales); help section `27-bank-import`. **Visible banks**: Wise, mBank, PKO, Revolut, Erste (PDF), Alior (PDF), Other — ING/Millennium/Pekao are in the registry but hidden in `BANKS` (`app/settings/import/index.tsx`) until validated against real exports. mBank/PKO parsers were rewritten against real exports (mBank: metadata preamble before `#Data operacji…#Kwota`, amounts without ` PLN`; PKO: comma-delimited quoted, single signed `Kwota`, array-mode parse). **Revolut parser** (ABA-116): `revolut.parser.ts` — comma-delimited UTF-8, columns `Type,Product,Started Date,Completed Date,Description,Amount,Fee,Currency,State,Balance`. Detection: `Started Date` + `Completed Date` + `Balance` header combo. Only `State=COMPLETED` rows imported (DECLINED/REVERTED/PENDING skipped). Amount is signed per-row; Fee already folded in. Currency is per-row (supports multi-currency accounts). EXCHANGE rows paired by existing `pairFxRows()` utility.
- **Competitor app migration (ABA-401)**: `monefy.parser.ts`, `wallet.parser.ts`, `moneymanager.parser.ts` — exports from other budgeting apps, in the SAME registry and interface as the bank parsers, tried before `UniversalParser`. Ids `monefy` / `wallet` / `moneymanager` are permanent: `buildExternalRef` embeds the parser id, so renaming one makes previously-imported rows importable again. The id union is declared TWICE (`parsers/parser.interface.ts` and `packages/shared-types/src/dto/import.ts`) and both must be extended together. **These files already imported before this feature** via the ABA-390 AI inference path; a dedicated parser buys a model call not spent, the exporting app's OWN categories carried across instead of guessed from the merchant, and a named entry point. Per-format gotchas: Monefy emits `currency` TWICE (raw + converted) so it is parsed in **array mode** — a keyed parse silently collapses the pair; Wallet states Expense/Income explicitly, marks `transfer` rows (dropped — not spending, and mapping them needs account mapping this import lacks) and restates amounts in its own base currency via `refAmount`/`refCurrency` (ignored, the transaction currency wins); Money Manager writes every amount **UNSIGNED** so its `Income/Expense` column is the only source of direction, and Category+Subcategory are joined (`"Food / Groceries"`). **Detection is deliberately tight** — a parser that claims a foreign format produces plausible rows with the wrong columns read, whereas an unclaimed file still imports via AI inference; `registry.spec.ts` asserts no competitor parser claims another fixture. It also pins a **pre-existing** overlap it surfaced: the **Pekao detector also matches a PKO export**, so PKO statements parse correctly today only because `pko` sits before `pekao` in `PARSERS` — do not reorder that array or insert a parser between them. **Category creation (ABA-401)**: `ImportBankService.preloadCategories` resolves every distinct `(suggestedCategoryName, kind)` in the batch **before** the commit `$transaction` opens and CREATES the ones the account lacks — same reason `dropDuplicateRows` runs there, a P2002 inside a Postgres transaction poisons it. Matching is **case-insensitive** (the import's own resolver was case-sensitive, so one export could mint both `Food` and `food`); the cache key is `` `${type}:${name.toLowerCase()}` `` because the unique is `(account_id, name, type)` and the same name is plausible on both sides. `resolveCategoryId` inside the transaction reads that cache and never creates. Mobile: `MIGRATION_ENTRIES` in `features/import/importEntries.ts` + a separate "Moving from another app?" card in the import hub; `importSourceLabel` searches BOTH lists or a past migration import renders its raw parser id. 2 i18n keys (`bankImport.migrationHeader`/`migrationHint`) × 9 locales. **Parsers were written WITHOUT real export files** — formats come from documented/observed shapes, and mBank/PKO both needed rewriting against real exports before, so expect corrections on first contact (most likely the date format: a slash date is read day-first).
- **AI universal statement import (ABA-390, API = plan 1 of 2)**: when no parser recognises an upload, an LLM either infers the CSV/XLSX **column mapping** (which the existing deterministic `UniversalParser` then uses) or, for PDFs, **extracts rows directly**. Lives in `modules/import-bank/ai/` with its **own** `new OpenAI(...)` — the convention every AI service here follows; `AiModule` is deliberately NOT imported (it pulls 11 modules, and `@TrackAiUsage`/`AiUsageGuard` live in `subscriptions/` anyway, so the fat dependency buys nothing). Only `SubscriptionsModule` is added, for the PDF gate. **Hook point**: the `if (!parser)` branch of `parsePreview`/`parsePdfPreview` — everything downstream (`buildPreviewResponse` → dedup → commit → `ImportBatch` rollback) is untouched. **Resolution order** gains two links: `mappingId` → `bankId` → per-account saved mapping → `detectParser` → **global signature** → **AI inference**. **The model never emits an amount or a date on the CSV path** — it returns column *names*, and `validateMappingResponse` (`ai/statement-ai.validator.ts`) checks each against the file's real header cells with **exact** matching (a `Set`, not an object map — an object map would make every `Object.prototype` key a false positive), rejecting the whole response if any name was invented, **including optional ones** (silently dropping an invented `currency` would produce a plausible import in the wrong currency). **`parser.id` stays `'universal'` on the CSV path** — `buildExternalRef` embeds the parser id in the dedup key, so an AI-inferred and a hand-mapped import of the same file produce byte-identical `externalRef`s; `ai-preview.service.spec.ts`'s invariant test pins this and must not be weakened. PDF uses `parser.id = 'ai'` (`parsers/ai-statement.parser.ts`, `detect()` always false, `parse()` throws — rows arrive pre-extracted via `toParserResult`; `'ai'` is filtered out of `detectPdfParser` and both `supportedBanks` lists). **Global dictionary** `bank_statement_signatures` (migration `20260809120000`, `@@unique(headerFingerprint)`) holds ONLY column names + delimiter + two format hints — no accountId/userId/rows, which is what makes a global row safe; the second user of any bank costs zero LLM calls. Self-quarantine: `find()` returns `null` once `correctedCount > confirmedCount`, and a commit **either** confirms **or** corrects — never both, or the counters could never diverge and quarantine would be dead code. **The decision is structural, not `dto.saveMapping`-based (fix wave 2026-08-10)**: a first whole-branch review made confirm/correct mutually exclusive by keying correct off `saveMapping`, but the mobile client never actually sends `saveMapping` (no UI ever sets it), so every correction still counted as a confirmation and `correctedCount` was structurally pinned at 0 — the very defect self-quarantine exists to prevent, unfixed one level down. `ImportBankService.isMappingCorrection` (`utils/column-mapping.ts`'s `columnMappingsEqual`, a by-value comparison — not reference or JSON-string order) now compares `dto.mapping` (whatever actually parsed the file) against the dictionary's current mapping for that fingerprint: no stored signature, or an unchanged mapping, confirms; a structural difference corrects — independent of whether the client also ticks "save my own mapping" (a separate decision, still with no mobile UI to trigger it). `record()` resets the counters when it replaces an already-quarantined row, so a corrected signature can re-enter. **Consent**: `Account.aiImportConsentAt`, one-time per account, written ONLY by `POST /import/bank/ai-consent` (`ViewerBlockGuard` — `resolveAiConsent` is read-only, because `POST /preview` is unguarded and a viewer must not enable LLM processing account-wide). **There is no `useAi` field**; the client flow is `preview` → `needs_ai_consent` → `POST /ai-consent` → re-request preview. Tier-2 E2EE accounts are refused before any LLM call and get the picker, not a consent screen. **Tiering**: inference is free and stays OUT of the monthly AI limit — its daily ceiling is a Redis counter via the `@Global()` `CacheService` (`aiimp:{accountId}:{YYYY-MM-DD}`), deliberately not `usage_logs`, whose only writer is `trackAiUsage`; PDF extraction is Pro, **pre-checks** remaining quota before spending money and increments only after success. **Never a 5xx from the AI path** — every failure degrades to `needs_picker`/`needs_mapping`. **Never silent completeness**: PDF responses carry `extractionWarning` unless `ai/balance-check.ts` actually reconciles Σrows against closing−opening; absent balances yield `'no_balance'`, not `undefined`. Env: `AI_IMPORT_MAX_PDF_PAGES` (20), `AI_IMPORT_MAX_INFERENCES_PER_DAY` (20), both NaN-guarded via `parseInferenceQuotaEnv`. **Two pre-existing bugs fixed here**: `peekHeaders`/`peekSampleRows` hardcoded `';'` (so a comma export produced ONE merged header cell — which is why **Revolut auto-detection never fired**, its `detect()` needs `'started date'` as its own cell) → now `utils/delimiter.ts` `sniffDelimiter`; and XLSX uploads are normalised to CSV (`utils/xlsx-to-csv.ts`, `exceljs`) **before** `headerFingerprint`, so fingerprinting, `UniversalParser`, the mapping cache and the manual mapper never learn it was a spreadsheet. **The preview response now echoes its parse context (fix wave 2026-08-10)**: both AI-success branches of `tryAiMapping` (signature-dictionary hit and fresh inference) add `headers`, `sampleRows`, `delimiter`, `amountFormat` and `dateFormat` (new optional fields on `BankImportPreviewResponse`) to what they already return — the non-AI `parsed` response is untouched. Before this, the "Wrong? Tap to fix" chip row opened the mapper with `preview.headers` undefined, so every column picker rendered empty and the mapper fell back to its own hardcoded `;`/`polish`/`auto` defaults regardless of what the file or the AI actually used — for a comma- or tab-delimited statement that silently re-parsed to zero rows, and `isValid` was still `true` (fields were prefilled from `aiMapping`) so Continue was enabled and `router.replace`d over the working preview with no way back except re-picking the file. Mobile's `resolveMapperInitialState` (`src/features/import/`, pure + unit-tested) now seeds the mapper's column AND format state from these echoed fields, falling back to the old positional-header/literal defaults only when they're absent. **Gotchas**: a statement with no currency column takes the *user's* currency (not the hardcoded `'PLN'`) and flags `currencyAssumed`; PDF extraction drops rows whose currency is outside the `Currency` union (allow-list is API-local — runtime imports from `@budget/shared-types` are forbidden here — and must track `packages/shared-types/src/entities/primitives.ts`); `pdf-parse` joins pages with a page-number template unless an explicit joiner is passed, so the AI path re-extracts with `'\f'` rather than splitting the shared text. **Known gap (narrowed by the 2026-08-10 fix wave)**: for a `mappingId`-selected or per-account fingerprint-matched *saved* mapping, `parser.parse()` still receives only a client-supplied delimiter override, never the saved row's own `delimiter` column, so a genuinely comma/tab-delimited saved mapping still parses zero rows on those two paths. The AI-inference path was always unaffected (threads the delimiter explicitly); the manual mapper reopened from an AI preview is now ALSO unaffected — see **the preview response now echoes its parse context** below — so the remaining gap is confined to the two saved-mapping resolution paths, not "every saved mapping" as originally scoped. **Mobile (plan 2, shipped)**: the deployment-order constraint above is discharged **only for clients carrying this build**, not unconditionally — the API's sole gate on the whole AI-import surface is `OPENAI_API_KEY`, which production already needs for every other AI feature, so there is **no kill-switch** and no `AI_IMPORT_ENABLED` flag (deliberately not added — the human manages this by holding the branch, not by feature-gating the server). Every already-installed **pre-plan-2** build still falls through the unhandled `needs_ai_consent` status to its `parsed` branch and renders `preview.rows ?? []` — an empty list with an enabled Import button. That is why this branch stays on local `development`, deliberately unpushed, until the app-store release ships (see the mobile ledger's BRANCH DECISION) — pushing it from this clone would serve `needs_ai_consent` to every current production client. `needs_ai_consent` is a real `preview.tsx` branch, not a fall-through — it `router.replace`s to `app/settings/import/ai-consent.tsx`. **There is no `useAi` request field anywhere**; the flow is always three calls: `preview` → `needs_ai_consent` → `POST /import/bank/ai-consent` (`api.grantAiImportConsent()`, viewer-blocked because `POST /preview` isn't) → re-request `preview`. The consent screen states what leaves the device — header row + up to 10 sample rows for CSV/XLSX, the first 20 extracted lines for a PDF (`isPdf = !preview.headerFingerprint` is how the copy tells the two apart) — and is shown once per account (`importStore.aiConsentGrantedFor`, deliberately survives `reset()`; a same-account repeat is treated as a stuck loop and renders a distinct failure view — one `useState` snapshot taken at mount, NOT a live subscription, because `accept()` writes that same field mid-flight between its two awaits and a live read would flash the loop UI over every successful grant). Accepting re-requests the preview. **Declining is not AI-prefilled, and the CLAUDE.md/user_docs wording that said otherwise was corrected in the 2026-08-10 fix wave**: `needs_ai_consent` never carries `aiMapping` — declining happens BEFORE any inference has run — so `router.replace`ing to the manual mapper there falls back to `resolveMapperInitialState`'s positional `headers[0..2]` guesses, the same as for any bank `detectParser` doesn't recognise at all. A PDF paywall bounce from that same decline flow is the same case (and, on the PDF branch, `headers` there are the statement's own raw extracted text lines, not real column names — pre-existing, unrelated to this fix). It is the **result-shown** mapper entries — the "Wrong? Tap to fix" chip row on a successful AI preview, and the zero-rows-parsed `needs_mapping` fallback — that carry `aiMapping` and land the mapper genuinely prefilled, now together with the echoed parse context described above. A successful inference renders `AiMappingChips` (`src/components/import/AiMappingChips.tsx`, pure `describeMapping()` helper) above the transaction list as an editable chip row — `Date → Data operacji`, `Amount → Kwota` — plus the model's `aiBankGuess` string, shown but never branched on; tapping the card opens the mapper prefilled. **Commit-time signature bookkeeping (fix wave 2026-08-10)**: `preview.tsx`'s commit now always spreads pure `buildCommitMappingContext()` (`src/features/import/`, unit-tested) instead of gating `headerFingerprint` behind "did the user visit the mapper" — a plain AI-accepted import (chips shown, straight to Import) used to send no fingerprint at all, so neither `confirmedCount` nor `correctedCount` ever moved for it. It sends `headerFingerprint` on every commit that has one, plus whichever mapping actually produced the rows (`pendingMapping.mapping` after a mapper trip, else `preview.aiMapping`), so the API's structural comparison above can tell a real correction from a "tapped through unchanged" confirmation. `buildPreviewNotices()` (`src/features/import/previewNotices.ts`) turns `currencyAssumed`/`extractionWarning` into banners: an assumed currency is user-correctable via a bottom-sheet picker, and the choice flows through pure `applyCurrencyOverride()` into BOTH the rendered list and the commit payload, never just the label; every extraction warning (`no_balance`/`balance_mismatch`/`pages_truncated`) is worded and rendered as a review prompt, never an error. `isTierRequiredError()` (`src/services/importErrors.ts`, an `ImportRequestError` carrying `status`/`code`/`requiredTier`) is checked at all 4 `importBankPreview` call sites — `index.tsx`, `preview.tsx`'s bank-picker retry, `ai-consent.tsx`'s accept, and `mapper.tsx`'s continue (the 4th wasn't in the original plan; the server's `isPdfBuffer` check runs before `bankId` is even consulted, so a PDF can reach the Pro gate from the manual-mapper path too) — and opens the existing `useUpgradeStore` paywall instead of surfacing a raw 403. 26 `bankImport.ai*` i18n keys across all 9 locales (`aiRoleDebit`/`aiRoleCredit` use real local banking vocabulary per language — PL `Obciążenie`/`Uznanie`, DE `Soll`/`Haben` — not literal cognates). **`aiConsentThirdParty` (added in the 2026-08-10 fix wave)**: the consent screen's original copy stated precisely what leaves the device but never said *where it goes* — the spec's own contract for this screen. A second sentence, present in all 9 locales (formal *Sie* in German, matching the rest of that object), now states the fragment is sent to a third-party AI provider for analysis and is not stored by them for training, without naming a vendor. XLSX is now offered in the file picker's MIME list (the server already sniffed the zip magic; the picker just didn't advertise it).
- **Import-bank service split**: `import-bank.service.ts` had grown to 1,188 lines mixing request-a-bank forwarding, deterministic preview parsing, AI mapping/extraction, dedup, the transactional commit writer, and category resolution — larger than `ai-tools.service.ts`/`expenses.service.ts`/`sync.service.ts`, none of which stayed unsplit at that size. Split into: `ai-preview.service.ts` (`ImportBankAiPreviewService` — `tryAiMapping`, AI-import consent `resolveAiConsent`/`grantAiConsent`, daily inference quota, `isMappingCorrection` signature-bookkeeping compare, exports `parseInferenceQuotaEnv`), `ai-pdf.service.ts` (`ImportBankAiPdfService` — `tryAiExtraction`, Pro-gate + usage tracking + balance reconciliation), `import-bank-dedup.service.ts` (`ImportBankDedupService` — `buildPreviewResponse`, `flagContentDuplicates`, `flagPossibleMerges`, `dropDuplicateRows`), and `import-bank-category.util.ts` (plain exported functions, no DI — `resolveCategoryId`/`preloadCategories`/`categoryCacheKey`, same P2002-avoidance pattern as `ExpensesService`/`BudgetsService`). `ImportBankService` is now the 408-line thin orchestrator for `requestBank`/`parsePreview`/`parsePdfPreview`/`commit`, injecting the three new services; `SignatureService` stays a direct dependency of both `ImportBankService` (commit-time bookkeeping) and `ImportBankAiPreviewService` (mapping lookup) — a shared leaf, same Nest-DI singleton in both. `ImportBankController`'s `POST /import/bank/ai-consent` now injects `ImportBankAiPreviewService` directly rather than routing through `ImportBankService` (mirrors ABA-368's "controller injects the new service directly" precedent) — no route/guard/DTO change. Mirrors the `expenses.service.ts` (ABA-368) and `chat.service.ts` (ABA-119) splits — a new import-bank concern should extend one of these focused services, not regrow `ImportBankService`.
- **Import batch history** (ABA-130): `modules/import-batches/` — `GET /import/batches` (last 20, each with `canRollback`: `status === 'committed'` and within 30-day window); `DELETE /import/batches/:id` rolls back (sets `isDeleted=true` + `externalRef=null` on linked rows so the file can be re-imported, marks batch `rolled_back`). Both wise + bank commit endpoints create an `ImportBatch` in-transaction and stamp records with `importBatchId`. Table `import_batches` (migration `20260525144711_add_import_batches`); nullable `import_batch_id` FK on `expenses`/`incomes`/`currency_exchanges`. Mobile: "Past imports" section in `app/settings/import/index.tsx`. `ImportBatchesModule` imported by both `ImportWiseModule` and `ImportBankModule`.
- **Backups** (ABA-163): `modules/backups/` — `POST /backups/export` builds a full account snapshot, serializes it **once**, and streams the JSON string straight to the response body via `@Res()` (no Nest re-serialization — returning `data: backup` previously tripled peak memory and OOM-crashed the API). Filename travels in the `X-Backup-Filename` header (+ `Content-Disposition`). The backup object carries `version`/`data` at the **top level** so restore validates directly (no outer envelope). **`Expense.receiptImage` (binary blob) is stripped from the export** (set to `undefined` right after the query) — a Bytes field serializes to a per-byte integer array (~4-6x size) and was the main cause of >50 MB backups; restore never reads it back. (Query-level Prisma `omit` types as `never` in this client setup, so a JS strip is used.) Mobile calls the endpoint **once** (`reports.api.ts` `downloadBackupData()` → `{ blob, fileName }`); `reportStore.exportBackup()` returns a `BackupExportResult` status so the UI only claims success when the file is actually saved/shared (Android SAF folder picker → shows path; else system share sheet). `POST /backups/restore` dedups by `clientId`/name and **never reuses the source row `id`s** (they are global PKs — reusing them threw `Unique constraint failed on (id)` when restoring into any account that still held those rows); created rows get fresh DB-generated ids and `expense`/`income.categoryId` is remapped through an old→new category-id map built during category restore. **Atomic**: the whole restore runs in one interactive `$transaction` (timeout 180s) — any per-row error throws `RestoreAbort` to roll everything back (no partial import), and the caller returns the collected `errors` with zero counts. `GET /backups/history`. 50 MB cap. See also API-memory note under Production.
- **Subscription manager** (ABA-208): `modules/user-subscriptions/` — CRUD tracker for users' recurring charges (Netflix, gym, SaaS). Table `user_subscriptions` (`id`, `accountId`, `name`, `amount`, `currencyCode`, `billingCycle` monthly/yearly/quarterly/weekly, `nextRenewalDate` @db.Date, `categoryId?`, `notes?`, `detectedFrom?`, `isActive`). 4 endpoints behind `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard`: `GET /user-subscriptions` (list, active first), `POST` / `PATCH :id` / `DELETE :id` (guarded by `ViewerBlockGuard`). Service computes `monthlyEquivalent` (yearly÷12, quarterly÷3, weekly×4.33) and `daysUntilRenewal` at query time — not stored. `SubscriptionRenewalCron` has two daily crons: (1) **09:00 UTC reminder** — sends `subscription_renewal` push to all account members 3 days before `nextRenewalDate` (respects `user.notifySubscriptionRenewals` pref, new column default `true`); (2) **08:00 UTC auto-charge (ABA-211)** — `handleDueRenewals` finds active subscriptions whose `nextRenewalDate <= today`, and per subscription **creates one `Expense`** (dated on the renewal date, `source:'manual'`, amount/currency/category/name from the subscription, attributed to the account **owner** via `accountMember role:'owner'`) and **advances `nextRenewalDate` by one billing cycle** (`addCycle`: weekly+7d / monthly+1mo / quarterly+3mo / yearly+1yr) — both in one `$transaction` so a crash can't double-charge. At most one charge per subscription per run (a long-overdue sub catches up one period per day, no burst). Sends a `subscription_renewal` "charged" push after. Fat Finder shows a "Track this" button on `type:'subscription'` findings, pre-filling the `new.tsx` form via router params. Mobile: `app/subscriptions/index.tsx` (list + monthly-total banner + FAB), `app/subscriptions/new.tsx` (modal), `app/subscriptions/[id].tsx` (edit/toggle-active/delete). Store: `userSubscriptionStore.ts`. API client: `userSubscriptions.api.ts`. Migration: `20260606000000_add_user_subscriptions`. i18n: `subscriptionManager.*` keys (9 locales). Notification preference `subscriptionRenewals` added to `GET/PATCH /users/me/notification-preferences`. **Entry points (ABA-209)**: ABA-208 left `app/subscriptions/index.tsx` route-registered in `_layout.tsx` but with **no navigable entry** (only Fat Finder linked to `/subscriptions/new`). Now reachable via a Settings-hub row (`settings/index.tsx` → `/subscriptions`, `subscriptionManager.title` + `settingsSubtitle`) and a configurable `subscriptions` home quick action (added to `QUICK_ACTION_KEYS`, icon `repeat-outline`, default on). **Renewal calendar (ABA-259)**: `app/subscriptions/index.tsx` has a **List / Calendar toggle pill** below the monthly-total card. Calendar mode renders `SubscriptionCalendarView` (`src/components/subscriptions/`) — Mon-first 7-column day grid where each day with a renewal shows colored dots (active=primary, inactive=textDisabled) + first subscription name (9px); tapping a day expands an inline drill-down with name/amount/cycle/inactive badge. Month nav (← →) managed inline. Data from `useSubscriptionCalendar` hook (`src/hooks/`) — projects `nextRenewalDate` forward/back via `addCycle`/`subtractCycle` to find all renewal days for the displayed month; weekly subs may appear 4–5× per month. Inactive subs shown at `opacity 0.55`. `FlatList` replaced by `ScrollView`. 4 new i18n keys (`calendarView`, `listView`, `noRenewalsThisMonth`, `renewalsOnDay`) in all 9 locales. No new API endpoint.
- **AI module features**:
  - **Chat Q&A**: Natural language financial questions powered by GPT-4
  - **Natural Language Commands**: Execute actions via chat (create expenses/budgets, query data) using OpenAI function calling
  - **16 AI functions**: `create_expense`, `create_income`, `create_budget`, `create_category`, `get_expenses`, `get_budget_status`, `get_category_breakdown`, `record_debt_repayment`, `create_debt`, `get_debt_summary`, `update_goal_balance`, `check_affordability`, `add_to_shopping_list`, `remove_from_shopping_list`, `get_shopping_suggestions`, `get_inflation_shield`
  - **Add / remove / query shopping list (ABA-348, extended ABA-360)**: `add_to_shopping_list({ items: string[] })` — the AI chat can put groceries on the user's shopping list. **Executes IMMEDIATELY** (no confirmation card, no read cache): `chat.service.ts` has a dedicated branch BEFORE the `isWriteAction` viewer/write check (item writes aren't `ViewerBlockGuard`-gated, so viewers may add too), routing to `handleShoppingListAdd` → `AiToolsService.executeAddToShoppingList` → `ShoppingListService.addItemsByName(accountId, userId, names)` (resolves the first non-archived list, else revives/creates `default-{accountId}`; one item per name with a fresh server clientId). Deterministic 9-lang confirmation via `PromptBuilder.getShoppingListAddText`; mobile renders a `ShoppingAddResult` `ActionResultCard` (`chat.actionAddShoppingList`). `AiModule` imports `ShoppingListModule`; `AiToolsService` injects `ShoppingListService`. The executor tolerates a lone `item` string as well as `items[]`. Targets the default/active list only (no add-to-named-list yet); the mobile store reflects the new item on its next pull. **`remove_from_shopping_list({ items: string[] })`** (ABA-360) is the mirror-image write — same immediate-execute treatment (not in `isWriteAction`, dedicated `chat.service.ts` branch before the confirmation check, `handleShoppingListRemove` → `AiToolsService.executeRemoveFromShoppingList` → new `ShoppingListService.removeItemsByName(accountId, names)`): case-insensitive match against `rawLabel` (fallback `canonicalName`) among **unchecked**, non-deleted items across ALL non-archived lists; first match per name is soft-deleted (`isDeleted` + `syncVersion` increment, same convention as the existing item-delete path); unmatched names come back in `notFoundLabels` (never an error — one bad name doesn't fail the rest). No "mark as bought" tool exists — removing via chat IS how "I already bought X, take it off" is handled; the one-tap checkbox on the shopping-list screen remains the only way to check an item off without removing it. Narrated via `PromptBuilder.getShoppingListRemoveText` (9 langs); mobile renders `ShoppingRemoveResult` (`chat.actionRemoveShoppingList` + `chat.actionShoppingNotFound`). **`get_shopping_suggestions()`** (ABA-360) is a **read** action — cached 10 min through the existing generic `executeWithCache`/`handleReadAction` path with NO dedicated `chat.service.ts` branch (same as `get_inflation_shield`/`check_affordability`); `AiToolsService.executeGetShoppingSuggestions` calls the existing `getRestockSuggestions`/`getDeals` in parallel, caps each at top 5. System prompt disambiguates it from `get_inflation_shield`: this tool is today's restock/deals, the shield is long-term price-rise forecasting for stocking up ahead. Mobile renders `ShoppingSuggestionsResult` (`chat.actionShoppingSuggestions` + `chat.shoppingSuggestionsRestock`/`chat.shoppingSuggestionsDeals` sub-sections). Deferred: quantity parsing, named-list (`listName`) targeting for any of the three tools.
  - **Category questions are split-aware, budget questions are not (ABA-446)**: `modules/ai/utils/category-attribution.ts` `attributeToCategories(expense)` is the ONE place the rule lives — **when an expense has `categorySplits`, the splits decide and its own `categoryId` is ignored; when it has none, its own category takes the whole amount** (mirrors `analytics.service.ts:218`). Both `executeGetExpenses` and `executeGetCategoryBreakdown` read it, so the two tools that answer "how much did I spend on X" cannot drift apart — they had, and the symptom was the chat answering **"nothing"** for a category that exists only as a split (deposits, alcohol, household) while the Analytics tab showed a number for the same period. **`get_expenses`'s `categoryName` is resolved but deliberately NOT pushed into the SQL filter** (`filters.categoryId`), because `expenses.category_id` can never match a split-only category; matching happens in memory over the attribution, and a filtered row reports **only its matching share** (4.50, not the receipt's 233.98 — answering with the whole total is worse than answering nothing). Two consequences of filtering after the fetch, both handled: the `limit: 500` page cap now bites BEFORE the filter (a full page logs a warning, the precedent being the keyword search's own `SEARCH_UNIT_LIMIT` log a few lines down), and `count` comes from the filtered rows, never `pagination.total`, which counts the whole period. `categoryTotals` is likewise attributed per split while `totalsByCurrency` stays what was actually paid. **Budgets, `get_budget_status` and Safe-to-Spend stay split-blind on purpose** (ABA-398 design spec, locked decision 1) — do not wire this util into them. Known gap: a deposit reaches the chat only when the receipt actually produced a split; below 2 categories the split is refused by design and the deposit survives only in `Expense.depositAmount`, which no chat tool reads.
  - **Semantic product/item expense search (ABA-343)**: `get_expenses` takes an optional `descriptionKeyword` for "how much did I spend on beer" questions. Flow: the tool fetches expenses in range, flattens each receipt into searchable **units** (`buildSearchUnits` in `modules/ai/utils/semantic-filter.ts` — one unit per **line item** for itemized receipts with `amount = item.totalPrice`, else one unit per expense; FX-converted; capped `SEARCH_UNIT_LIMIT = 1500`), and returns them as `matchedExpenses`. `ChatService.semanticFilterExpenses` then asks the cheap model to return the matching **integer line-indices** (NOT UUIDs — small models mangle/truncate 36-char UUIDs, which silently dropped nearly every match; `parseMatchedIndices` clamps to `[1,count]`) and **unions** them with a deterministic substring pass (`deterministicMatchIndices`, same-script only); on model error it degrades to substring-only, **never** "return everything". The matched set is the **single source of truth** — it drives `matchedExpenses`/`recentExpenses`/`count`/`totalsByCurrency`/`categoryTotals` so the mobile card, the narration, and the total can't disagree. **Date window**: for keyword queries `startDate`/`endDate` are optional (schema `required: []`) and default to **full history** (`2000-01-01`→today) — the prompt tells the model to OMIT dates unless the user names a period (fixes the old "use today's date" → narrow-window "found nothing"). **Typo/any-language tolerance**: the filter prompt interprets the term forgivingly (typos, slang, inflections, any language: `пиво`/`пивка`/`beeer`/`cerveza` → Polish `Piwo …`), and the tool schema tells the orchestrator to pass the user's **raw wording**, not translate/correct it. Because a beer line inside a 150 zł Biedronka receipt contributes only its 49.90 zł, the total is the sum of matched line prices; beer-specific discount lines (see OCR discount note) net out. Pure util + `semanticFilterExpenses` are unit-tested. Line-item search deferred earlier for sum-attribution reasons is now the shipped behavior. Follow-up: recall bounded by the 1500-unit cap (logged when exceeded); backfill of pre-existing discount line items not done.
  - **Affordability Oracle (ABA-293)**: `check_affordability(amount, currencyCode?, description?)` — read action (no confirmation), deterministic `AffordabilityVerdict` from `SafeToSpendService`; verdict fields `affordable`/`reasonCode` forwarded verbatim to the LLM which narrates only (prompt rule mirrors the "report `overBy` verbatim" pattern); cached via `executeWithCache` (cache key includes `baseCurrency`); `reasonCode` values: `within_safe`, `within_available_tight`, `over_available`, `delays_goal`, `wait_until_income`.
  - **Debt commands**: `record_debt_repayment(debtId, amount, date?)` creates a linked repayment income/expense; `create_debt(contactName, amount, currencyCode, direction, dueDate?)` creates lent (Expense) or borrowed (Income) record with `isDebt=true`; `get_debt_summary()` returns active debts (read, cached). AI resolves contact names via `activeDebts` context (ids included) — ambiguous names trigger clarifying question.
  - **Goal command**: `update_goal_balance(goalId, newAmount)` calls `GoalPlannerService.updateGoal({ currentAmount }, { userId, note: 'AI update' })`. Resolves goal names via `savingsGoals` context (ids now included). Auto-completes goal if `currentAmount >= targetAmount`. Also inserts a `GoalContribution` row (see **Goal contribution log** below).
  - **Goal contribution log** (ABA-262): `goal_contributions` table (`migration 20260615100000_add_goal_contributions`). `GoalPlannerService.updateGoal()` accepts optional `contributionMeta: { userId, note? }` — when `newCurrentAmount > oldCurrentAmount`, delta amount is recorded in a `goalContribution.create` inside the same `$transaction`. `GET /ai/goals/:id/contributions` returns last 20 newest-first (no ViewerBlockGuard — read-only). Manual `PATCH /ai/goals/:id` passes `{ userId: req.user.id }` automatically. AI `update_goal_balance` passes `{ userId, note: 'AI update' }`. Mobile goal detail screen shows a "Contribution History" card below the progress bar; `goalStore.contributions` is a per-goalId in-memory map loaded via `loadContributions(id)`. No backfill for existing goals.
  - **Confirmation flow**: Write actions (`create_*`, `record_debt_repayment`, `update_goal_balance`) require user confirmation before execution; read actions (`get_*`) execute immediately (10-min cache)
  - **Language detection** (ABA-264): `PromptBuilder.detectUserLanguage(message, history, uiLanguage?)` picks the reply language in three tiers — (1) the language detected from the **current message's** script/unique chars, (2) the user's **app UI locale** (`user.language`, mapped via `localeToLanguageName`) for ambiguous/plain-ASCII text, (3) recent-assistant-message history (legacy fallback). `chat.service.ts` selects `user.language` and threads it through `buildSystemPrompt` as `uiLanguage`. `detectLanguage` decides French vs Spanish on **language-unique characters only** — the shared `é` (very common in FR: *dépense, café, été*) must NOT force Spanish (the original bug: FR UI → ES replies, then history locked it in). Supported: Russian, Ukrainian, Belarusian, German, Spanish, French, Polish, Dutch, English. Cyrillic/German/Polish (unique scripts) always override the UI locale.
  - **Currency mapping**: Supports currency symbol detection (₴→UAH, $→USD, €→EUR, zł→PLN, £→GBP, ₽→RUB)
  - **Currency labeling + display-currency conversion in answers** (ABA-263): chat answers are LLM-narrated free text, so currency labeling is enforced via prompt, not code. `prompt-builder.ts` carries a hard rule — every amount in tool results / context has its own `currencyCode`; the model must label each amount with that currency (ISO code or mapped symbol) and **never default to € for non-EUR amounts**. `chat.service.ts` selects `user.currencyCode` and passes it to `buildSystemPrompt` as `baseCurrency` (shown in the dynamic context) AND down to the read-action tools as the conversion target. **Conversion**: `AiToolsService` injects `ExchangeRateService` (from `CurrencyExchangeModule`; rates from open.er-api.com, `1 base = rates[X] X`, so `amount_in_base = amount / rates[from]`). all three read tools — `get_expenses`, `get_budget_status`, `get_category_breakdown` — convert every monetary field into the display currency and return them already in `baseCurrency` (rows/categories `currencyCode: base`, single base-currency total, plus `fxConverted/fxApproximate` flags); `getRatesSafe`/`convertAmount` helpers; **graceful fallback** to native amounts when the rate provider is unavailable or a currency has no rate. **`get_category_breakdown` computes from the raw expenses** (group + convert each), NOT `analyticsService.getSummary` — getSummary sums across currencies into one currency-blind number that can't be converted afterwards. The tool cache key (`buildToolCacheKey`) **includes `baseCurrency`** — it is per-user, so two members of one account with different display currencies must not share a cached (wrongly-converted) result. The read-action `tool` message tells the model: if `fxConverted`, present amounts as-is and add a short "approximate rate" note. (Not yet converted: the always-present UserContext summary numbers — `totalSpentThisMonth`/`topCategories` in the system prompt — are still native; the prompt tells the model to use tools for accurate amounts.) Mobile `ActionResultCard.tsx` `ExpensesResult` renders deterministically from the `get_expenses` tool output (`recentExpenses` + `totalsByCurrency`, per-currency totals with codes — now the display currency) — NOT `data.expenses`/`data.total` (those keys don't exist; the old card showed 0 items).
  - **Income voice & receipt capture** (ABA-191): `POST /ai/parse-income` — parallel to `POST /ai/parse-expense` but uses **income-type categories** for matching; guarded + tracked at 1.0. Mobile `app/income/voice.tsx` (record → Whisper → `parseIncome` → confirm form, `source:'voice'`) and `app/income/receipt.tsx` (camera/gallery/PDF → `scanReceipt` → confirm, `source:'ocr'`; total+date only, no line-items). Both accessible from home screen quick-action strip and via mic/camera icons in `income/new.tsx` footer. `Income.source` field (`IncomeSource = 'manual'|'voice'|'ocr'|'import'`) added to Prisma schema (migration `20260602000000_add_income_source`) and mobile SQLite (ALTER TABLE migration in `client.native.ts`).
  - **Endpoints** (all behind `JwtAuthGuard + AccountContextGuard` via the class-level decorator on `AiController`): `POST /ai/chat`, `POST /ai/chat/confirm`, `POST /ai/chat/reject`; `GET /ai/chat/conversations` (last 20, **account-scoped**: `accountId = X-Account-Id AND (isShared OR userId = me)`), `GET /ai/chat/conversations/:id/messages` (last 50, user+assistant roles only, same access predicate), `GET /ai/chat/conversations/:id/poll?since=<iso>` (newer messages + refreshes Redis presence), `PATCH /ai/chat/conversations/:id/shared` (owner-only sharing toggle). `confirm`/`reject` verify the conversation is in the caller's account AND that the caller initiated the `pending_action` (scoped by its `senderUserId`).
  - **Chat history**: `ChatConversation` (now `accountId` + `isShared`) + `ChatMessage` (now `senderUserId` + `mentionedUserIds`) stored server-side. Mobile loads history via `chatStore.loadConversations()` / `loadConversation(id)` with SQLite cache (`src/db/chatRepository.ts`, account-scoped local query). Chat tab shows a History bottom-sheet and a "New Conversation" button when browsing past conversations.
  - **Shared conversations**: per-conversation opt-in group chat for shared accounts. `ChatConversation.isShared` (default false; **any account member may share a conversation THEY created (ABA-334)** — `setConversationShared` checks `conversation.userId === caller` (NOT `accountRole`), and `chat()`'s `initialIsShared` is honored for any member; a member still cannot share someone else's conversation, even an account owner's) makes it visible to all members; private stays creator-only. A message that `@mentions` a member (mentions passed as `{userId}[]`, validated, self excluded) **silences the AI** and pushes a `chat_mention` notification (gated by `notifySharedActivity`) to each mentioned member **not currently present**; no mention → AI replies. Presence in Redis via `CacheService` key `chat:presence:{conversationId}:{userId}` (TTL 45s). AI history prefixes each member's message with sanitized `[Name]: `. Mobile polls `…/poll?since=` every 4s (only while a shared conversation is focused), reconciles optimistic id to server `userMessageId`, renders others' messages left-aligned with name label, `@`-mention suggestion bar, **creator-only** Shared/Private header toggle (mobile `chatStore` tracks `currentIsOwner`/`ownedConversationIds` from the API's per-conversation `isOwner`; `canToggleShared = hasOtherMembers && currentIsOwner`). **Sender-side dup fix (ABA-334)**: while `api.chat()` is in flight the 4s poll could return the just-sent message's server copy while the local optimistic copy still had its `tempId` (id mismatch → not deduped → appended twice, sender-only); the poll now dedupes against state read **after** its network await, and the `tempId→serverId` reconciliation drops duplicate ids. **Read-action userId (ABA-334)**: `handleReadAction` now threads the real `userId` into `executeWithCache` (was a hard-coded `''`), so read tools (e.g. `check_affordability`) execute with the actual user; the cache key (`accountId+args+baseCurrency`) is unchanged. **Deep-link**: tapping a `chat_mention` push → `handleNotificationResponse` switches `accountId` → `router.push('/(tabs)/chat', { conversationId })`; chat screen reads the param and `loadConversation` (falls back to `loadConversations()` when uncached). **Cold-start gate (ABA-264)**: `_layout.tsx` must NOT act on `getLastNotificationResponseAsync()` immediately — navigating while `RootNavigator` still returns `null` (init not done, `<Stack>` unmounted) wedges expo-router on a black screen, and `switchAccount` no-ops against the empty accounts list. The cold-start response is stored in state and flushed only once `!isInitializing && isAuthenticated && fontsLoaded` (same gate as the `Linking` deep-link handler). Keep the two deep-link paths gated symmetrically.
  - **AI service split** (ABA-119): `chat.service.ts` is the lean orchestrator (415 lines); three focused helpers live alongside it: `user-context-builder.service.ts` (builds `UserContext` from Prisma/domain services), `ai-tools.service.ts` (all 11 OpenAI function schemas + `executeAction` dispatcher + cache wrapper), `prompt-builder.service.ts` (system prompt construction, language detection, action i18n). All three are registered in `AiModule` as providers but not exported.
  - **UserContext caching** (ABA-151): `UserContextBuilder.build()` caches the result in Redis under key `uc:{accountId}` (TTL 60 s). `ExpensesService.invalidateChatCache()` and `IncomesService` mutation methods (`create`/`update`/`remove`) call `CacheService.del('uc:{accountId}')` after writing so the cache is refreshed promptly. The exported `ucKey(accountId)` helper in `user-context-builder.service.ts` is the canonical key formula. **Budget summary (ABA-344)**: `context.monthlyBudget` sums **ALL** active monthly budgets (a budget's `amount` is its total regardless of category allocations) — the old `categoryAllocations.length === 0` filter made the prompt show "Monthly budget: Not set" for users whose only budget is category-allocated, and the model then wrongly answered "you have no budget" without calling `get_budget_status`. The prompt line is annotated as a rough monthly-only total and instructs the model to ALWAYS call `get_budget_status` for budget questions.

- **Shared bot i18n (ABA-462)**: `common/bot-i18n/shared-messages.ts` holds the ~30-key, 9-language dictionary shared by all three bots (error messages, confirmations, menu labels, category-flow copy) plus a `createBotT(messages, { markup, defaultParams })` factory and the shared `buildCategorySplitLine`/`CategorySplitLineItem` — replaced three independently-copy-pasted `messages`/`t()` implementations (tech-debt `bot-i18n-triplication`, closed). Canonical markup is HTML (`<b>`, `<code>`, matching Telegram's own `parse_mode: HTML`); `createBotT(..., { markup: 'markdown' })` regex-converts those tags to WhatsApp/Slack's `*bold*`/`` `code` `` syntax at read time. `defaultParams` (e.g. `{ platform: 'Telegram' }`) is merged under any caller-supplied params, letting a "same template, only the brand word differs" message (`notLinked`) live once with a `{{platform}}` placeholder — no call-site changes needed. Each bot's own `modules/{telegram,whatsapp,slack}/helpers/i18n.ts` is now `{ ...sharedMessages, ...platformMessages }` → `createBotT(...)`, holding only genuinely platform-specific copy: `linkFirst`/`welcomeNew`/`unlinkSuccess`/`helpText` (all three — telegram uses `/slash` commands + HTML-entity escaping, whatsapp/slack don't), `welcomeBack`/`linkSuccess` (shared between whatsapp/slack only; telegram overrides both since it says "/help" not "help"), and Slack's own `thinking` placeholder key. **A new bot-facing string shared by 2+ platforms belongs in `shared-messages.ts`, not copy-pasted into each `helpers/i18n.ts`.**
- **Telegram bot**: `modules/telegram/` — Telegraf-based bot with 7 handlers: `ChatHandler` (AI chat with usage tracking, 1.0/msg), `VoiceHandler` (Whisper transcription + chat, 2.0), `PhotoHandler` (OCR receipt scan, 2.0), `CommandHandler` (/start, /link, /help, /usage, /account, /newchat, /unlink), `ExpenseHandler`, `IncomeHandler`, `CategoryHandler`. All system messages localized via `helpers/i18n.ts` (9 languages, resolved from `user.language`). AI usage tracked and limits enforced — users get localized warning when limit reached. **Pending mid-flow state is Redis-backed (`CacheService`), not a module-level Map (tech-debt `telegram-handlers-in-memory-state`, closed)**: `ChatHandler`'s AI-confirmation data (`telegram:pa:{shortId}`, TTL 1800s) and `PhotoHandler`'s pending-receipt data + date-edit cursor (`telegram:receipt:{receiptId}` TTL 1800s, `telegram:awaiting_date:{telegramUserId}` TTL 600s) mirror the WhatsApp bot's `wa:pa:*`/`wa:receipt:*`/`wa:awaiting_date:*` keys — an in-memory Map was silently wiped on every deploy restart, orphaning any in-flight confirmation or receipt scan. Redis TTL replaces the old manual size-capped eviction loop. Both handlers inject `CacheService` (`@Global()`, no module change needed). **Gotcha fixed during the port**: `handleDateInput` mutates the pending-receipt object's `date` field in place — with a real Map that mutation persisted for free via object reference; with Redis a fetched value is a fresh JSON-deserialized copy, so the handler now explicitly writes the mutated object back via `cache.set(...)` before continuing, or the edited date would silently revert to the original on the next read.

- **WhatsApp bot**: `modules/whatsapp/` — `@Global()` module on the Meta Business Cloud API. Parallel to Telegram, same features + shared services (`ChatService`, `WhisperService`, `OcrService`, `ExpensesService`, `IncomesService`, `CategoriesService`, `SubscriptionsService`). Differences: (a) **webhook-only** `POST /whatsapp/webhook`, excluded from `/api/v1` prefix in `main.ts`; (b) **HMAC-SHA256** verification via `verifySignature` over `req.rawBody`; (c) **Redis state** not in-memory — `wa:msg:{id}` (idempotency 24h), `wa:pa:{shortId}` (pending actions 1800s), `wa:receipt:{shortId}` + `wa:awaiting_date:{phone}`, `wa:cat:{shortId}`; (d) callback IDs use `--` separator (UUIDs contain single `-`); (e) interactive via `WhatsAppClientService.sendButtons` (max 3×20char) / `sendList` (max 10 rows); (f) WA markdown (`*bold*`, `_italic_`) via `markdownToWhatsApp`. Linking via 6-hex code: mobile QR + `wa.me/{phone}?text=link%20{code}` in the **Chat bots** screen `app/settings/bots.tsx`; `CommandHandler.handleLink` is the only command from unlinked numbers. Endpoints on `UsersController` (`POST/GET/DELETE /users/me/whatsapp-link[-code]`). `helpers/i18n.ts` ports telegram's (33 keys × 9 languages).

- **Slack bot**: `modules/slack/` — Slack DM bot with full feature parity (AI chat, voice via Whisper, photo receipts via OCR, `expense`/`income`/`category` commands, confirm/reject via Block Kit buttons). **Webhook-only**: `POST /slack/events` (Events API, JSON) and `POST /slack/interactivity` (Block Kit button taps, `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` `payload` field) — both excluded from the `/api/v1` prefix in `main.ts`. `main.ts` also registers `express.urlencoded({ verify })` alongside the existing JSON body parser so that `rawBody` is captured on the interactivity endpoint for HMAC verification. **Security**: `v0=` HMAC-SHA256 over `v0:{timestamp}:{rawBody}` keyed by `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, with a 5-minute replay window (`helpers/verify-signature.ts`). File downloads for voice/photo are fetched from `files.slack.com` over https only, with redirects refused (SSRF hardening). **Inbound message guard (ABA-256)**: `SlackBotService.handleEvent` drops bot/self messages and edit/delete/system subtypes, **but must let `subtype === 'file_share'` through** — Slack tags every uploaded/pasted file (image/voice/PDF) message with that subtype, and dropping it (the old `if (event.subtype) return;`) silently broke ALL receipt-photo / voice / PDF handling. Pattern: `if (event.bot_id) return; if (event.subtype && event.subtype !== 'file_share') return;`. Files are then dispatched from `dispatchMessage` via `event.files[0]` (audio→voice, image→`photoHandler.handleImage`, pdf→`handleDocument`). Regression test in `slack-bot.service.spec.ts`. **Redis state**: `slack:msg:{event_id}` (idempotency 24h; guards against Slack retries via `X-Slack-Retry-Num`), `slack:pa:{shortId}` (pending actions 1800s), `slack:receipt:{shortId}`, `slack:awaiting_date:{slackUserId}`, `slack:cat:{shortId}`. **Callback IDs** use a single `:` separator (`ca:`, `ra:`, `account:`, `cat_e:`, `cat_i:`, `cat_d:`, `receipt_*:`), parsed on the first `:`. **Outbound** via `@slack/web-api` `WebClient` (`chat.postMessage` with text + Block Kit buttons; `auth.test` on startup for the bot user id loop-guard). Button pickers replace `sendList` — account lists capped at 23 items, category lists at 20, with overflow text. **Linking**: 6-char hex code via `link <code>` DM (no OAuth). New tables `SlackLink` + `SlackLinkCode` (migration `20260604103259_add_slack_links`). Link endpoints on `UsersController` (`POST/GET/DELETE /users/me/slack-link[-code]`). Bot-created expenses use `source: 'slack'`. **Thinking indicator** (ABA-196): slow paths (AI chat, voice, photo OCR) immediately post a `💭` placeholder (`SlackClientService.postPlaceholder` → `ts`) and replace it in place with the final answer via `chat.update` (`updateText` passes NO `blocks` so the body renders — `blocks: []` would blank the body; `updateButtons` rebuilds the Block Kit via shared `buildButtonBlocks`; `replyText`/`replyButtons` update when a `ts` exists else send fresh). `ChatHandler.handleText(text, userState, placeholderTs?)` reuses a passed `ts`; voice turns its placeholder into the permanent `🎤 "transcript"` echo, then the chat reply gets its own placeholder. `thinking` i18n key in all 9 languages. **Mobile**: "Slack" section added to `app/settings/bots.tsx` (link code + status + unlink), `slackBot.*` i18n keys in all 9 locales, `api.generateSlackLinkCode/getSlackLinkStatus/unlinkSlack`. **Env**: `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, `SLACK_APP_ID`, `SLACK_BOT_USER` (bot is a no-op when unset). Prod webhook URL: `https://api.ai-budget.pl/slack/events`. **Multi-workspace OAuth** (ABA-200): `GET /slack/install` (issues single-use CSRF state in Redis `slack:oauth_state:{state}` TTL 600s → 302 to Slack `oauth/v2/authorize`) and `GET /slack/oauth/callback` (validates+consumes state → `oauth.v2.access` code exchange → upsert `SlackInstallation` with bot token **AES-256-GCM encrypted at rest** keyed by `SLACK_TOKEN_ENC_KEY`) — both excluded from `/api/v1` in `main.ts`; implemented in `SlackOAuthController` + `SlackOAuthService` + `SlackInstallationService`; helpers `token-crypto.ts` (AES-256-GCM) and `oauth-pages.ts` (HTML success page, team name HTML-escaped). `SlackClientService` resolves the bot token **per `teamId`** (looks up `SlackInstallation` first, falls back to `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` env for the original workspace), caches `WebClient` per token; every outbound method takes a leading `teamId` parameter. New table `SlackInstallation` (migration `20260604230924_add_slack_installations`). New env vars: `SLACK_CLIENT_ID`, `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET`, `SLACK_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URL`, `SLACK_TOKEN_ENC_KEY`. Mobile: "Add to Slack" button in `app/settings/bots.tsx` opens `<API_ORIGIN>/slack/install` in-browser; `slackBot.addToSlack`/`addToSlackHint` i18n keys (9 locales).

### Mobile (React Native/Expo)
- **Platforms**: iOS, Android, **Web** (Expo web via `react-native-web` + Metro). Web is supported for quick browser testing — run `npm run dev:web` from project root (or `npm run web` from `apps/mobile/`). On web, native-only modules fall back via platform-specific files: `secureStorage.web.ts` (localStorage), `db/client.web.ts` (in-memory mock, no persistence — server still works), `useBiometric.web.ts` (no-op). **Auth on web (ABA-235)**: because web has no biometric, both `authStore.initialize()` (session restore) AND `authStore.logout()` (token clear) gate the "keep tokens for biometric re-login" path behind `Platform.OS !== 'web'`. Both guards must stay in sync — if `logout()` keeps tokens on web (as the native biometric path does), the next refresh's `initialize()` restores them and the user appears logged back in. MMKV uses its built-in `createMMKV.web.ts` (localStorage-backed). `expo-notifications`, `react-native-android-widget`, `expo-screen-orientation` are guarded by `Platform.OS === 'web'` checks or platform-specific imports. **Caveat**: SQLite-backed offline-first flows are disabled on web — data shows only what the API returns, no local cache; receipts/voice/biometric features are also degraded. Use web for UI testing, not full functional testing. **Static web hosting (ABA-213) — LIVE at https://ai-budget.pl**: `scripts/build-web.sh` runs `expo export --platform web` (SPA, `web.output: "single"`) → `apps/mobile/dist/` (gitignored). Served on the prod VPS by a tiny `ai-budget-web-prod` `nginx:alpine` container (32 MB, files at `/opt/ai-budget-web/html`), proxied by the shared reverse proxy **`shared-nginx`** (configs at `/opt/shared-nginx/conf.d/ai-budget.conf`, apex 443 block → `proxy_pass ai-budget-web-prod`). Reuses the existing `live/ai-budget.pl` cert and prod `CORS_ORIGIN` already lists `https://ai-budget.pl` — so no CORS change. **`www.ai-budget.pl` → apex (ABA-234)**: apex `ai-budget.pl` is canonical. `www` has its own 443 block that `return 301 https://ai-budget.pl$request_uri;`, `www` is added to the HTTP/ACME (port 80) `server_name`, and the cert was `--expand`ed so its SANs now include `www.ai-budget.pl` (the cert originally covered only apex/admin/api). Without these, `www` had no matching server block and fell through to the first-loaded 443 block (eksiegowyai.pl) with a cert mismatch. `shared-nginx` has **no `default_server`**, so any new `*.ai-budget.pl` host needs its own block or it lands on a sibling site. `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` baked in at build time. **Auto-deploy**: `.github/workflows/web-deploy.yml` runs on every push to `development` + `workflow_dispatch` — builds the bundle on the runner, `rsync --delete`s `apps/mobile/dist/` to `/opt/ai-budget-web/html` (same SSH secrets as `deploy.yml`), verifies `https://ai-budget.pl` 200 (no restart/reload needed — bind-mounted). Full runbook + DR in `docs/ops/web-deploy.md`; VPS access in the `prod-vps-ssh-access` memory. **Pricing page**: `docs/marketing/landing/build_landing.py` also builds a static `/pricing/` page (all 9 languages, `pricing_url(lang)`) showing the real Free/Pro/Business tiers from `subscriptions.service.ts`'s `PRICING` table (`CURRENCY_PRICING`/`LANG_CURRENCY` constants in the generator — en/be→USD, pl→PLN, de/es/fr/nl→EUR, ru→RUB, ua→UAH; `be` has no BYN support so it falls back to USD) with a pure-CSS Monthly/Yearly toggle (same `:checked` sibling-selector trick as the feature-card lightbox). Linked from every page's footer (`PRICING_LABELS`) and included in the apex sitemap. Regenerate after editing: `python docs/marketing/landing/build_landing.py`. **The pricing page's JSON-LD is a `SoftwareApplication`, never a `Product` (ABA-397)**: the tiers originally shipped as three bare `Product` nodes (`name` + one `Offer`), which put all 9 pricing pages into Search Console's **merchant-listings** validation — a spec written for shippable goods — and produced 6 permanent errors (critical `image`; plus `description`, `brand`/`gtin`, `offers.availability`, `offers.shippingDetails`, `offers.hasMerchantReturnPolicy`) that grew 3 items per crawled page.
