Enriching the `Product` could never clear it: an app has nothing to ship and no consignment to return, so those two warnings are unanswerable by construction. Now one `SoftwareApplication` (`@id` `{SITE}/#app`, shared with the landing's node) carries the plans as an `offers` array built by `offer_node()` — one `Offer` per tier **per billing period** (Free, Pro m/y, Business m/y), each with `availability: InStock`, `seller`, and a `UnitPriceSpecification` whose `referenceQuantity.unitCode` is the UN/CEFACT period (`MON`/`ANN`) — mirroring the visible Monthly/Yearly toggle, since marked-up prices must match rendered ones. `org_node()` is the shared Organization used by both `pricing_page()` and `jsonld()`. Do NOT reintroduce `Product` here, and do NOT invent `shippingDetails`/`hasMerchantReturnPolicy` to silence a warning. **Static SEO blog (ABA-267)**: `ai-budget.pl/blog` is real crawlable HTML — the SPA itself can't rank (single `<title>`, JS-rendered, no per-page meta/sitemap). `docs/marketing/seo/build_blog.py` renders `docs/marketing/seo/*.md` (pl) + `docs/marketing/seo/en/*.md` (en) → `docs/marketing/seo/site/` (committed). **Multilingual (ABA-268/270/279/283/318/393)**: 189 articles (**21 topics × 9 languages** — 3 pillars (budget/expenses/saving) + 6 Wave-1 cluster topics (ABA-279: pairs `shared-budget`, `envelope`, `rule-503020`, `best-apps`, `emergency-fund`, `subscriptions`) + 6 Wave-2 cluster topics (ABA-283: pairs `groceries`, `categories`, `debt`, `bank-import`, `family`, `ai-budget`; source files numbered `1[0-5]-*.md`) + 2 Wave-3 feature-led topics (ABA-318: pairs `inflation`, `expense-map`; source files `1[67]-*.md`; tied to the Personal Inflation Index + expense-map features, added to `FROM_BLOG_PAIRS` for homepage links) + 4 Wave-4 topics (ABA-393: pairs `split-bill`, `auto-capture`, `receipts`, `school`; source files `1[89]-*.md` / `2[01]-*.md`; the first three are feature-led USP pieces — receipt splitting with guest links, Android bank-notification capture, receipt OCR line items — and `school` is **seasonal**, worth refreshing each August. **Positioning constraint on `receipts`, binding in all 9 languages**: the receipt price check may only be described as "more expensive than usual, worth checking", never as detecting an overcharge or a failed promotion — the app cannot prove a promotion did not apply); editorial plan in `docs/marketing/seo/content-plan.md`; each new topic ties to a concrete app feature for conversion) across **9 languages** (en/pl/de/es/fr/ru/ua/be/nl, sources in `docs/marketing/seo/<lang>/*.md` + `*.md` for pl; the `pair` frontmatter key MUST be identical across all 9 language files of a topic or hreflang breaks; slugs are localized/ascii-transliterated per language); each at `/blog/<lang>/<slug>/` + a per-language `/blog/<lang>/` index; `en` is `x-default`, all langs paired via the `pair` frontmatter key; **the blog shares the landing's header (brand + language dropdown with native names + "Log in" → `app.ai-budget.pl`) and footer (centered, MICODE sp. z o.o. logo at `/assets/mi_code_logo.svg`)**; per-page `<head>` has title/meta/canonical/OG/Twitter, JSON-LD `Article`+`BreadcrumbList`, and `hreflang` en/pl/x-default. `/blog/` is a **`noindex` JS language dispatcher** (`navigator.language`: `pl`→`/blog/pl/`, else `/blog/en/`) and is deliberately NOT in the sitemap so it never blocks crawling. `sitemap.xml` lists every language URL; `robots.txt` + a per-language 1200×630 OG image are generated too. `web-deploy.yml` copies `site/.` into `apps/mobile/dist/` **before** the rsync so it ships with the bundle. No nginx change — inner `location / { try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html }` serves the real files before the SPA fallback. Regenerate after editing any article: `python docs/marketing/seo/build_blog.py`. **Blog index listing (ABA-394)**: modelled on the mi-code blog listing — **newest first** (sorted by the frontmatter `date`, tie-broken on path so same-day topics come out in reverse file-number order), each card showing `date · reading time` (`reading_minutes()`, 200 wpm, floor 1), title, description, category and a read link, above a **pure-CSS category filter** (hidden radios + `:checked` sibling selectors, same trick as the landing lightbox/pricing toggle — no JS, so every card stays in the HTML for crawlers even when filtered; chip counts are static per category, which is why there is no mi-code-style dynamic results count). Categories come from `CLUSTERS` in `build_blog.py` — the **single source of truth** for both the index filter and the generated pillar down-links (`build_pillar_links.py` imports it), so a topic missing from `CLUSTERS` silently gets neither a chip nor a pillar link. **`date` is a REQUIRED frontmatter field** on every article and is the publication date, deliberately not derived from git: `git_date()` is the *last-commit* date, so a later edit would promote an old article to the top of the index and rewrite its `datePublished`. It also fixed a real defect — `datePublished` used to be the frozen `PUBLISH_DATE` constant for **all** 189 articles, so every page claimed the same publication date; the visible byline now leads with the publication date and appends "Updated" only when the file genuinely changed later. Future-dated scheduling (mi-code hides posts with `date > today`) was deliberately **NOT** copied: the build has no wall-clock dependency today, and a hidden-from-index article would still sit in the sitemap. **Only slugs are ASCII — visible text is not (ABA-395)**: `slug` must be ASCII (it is the URL), but `title`, `meta_description` and body copy must use the language's real orthography. Nine German articles had carried ASCII transliterations (`fuer`, `kuendigst`, `groesste`, `behaeltst`, `persoenliche`) in exactly the two fields Google renders — `<title>` and the description — apparently by copying the ASCII slug convention into the copy; `de/14` had also calqued English "lumpy costs" into `lumpige Posten`, which in German means *shabby* items. When checking a rendered `meta name="description"` length, measure the **HTML-entity-decoded** string: `&#x27;` for an apostrophe is 6 raw characters but one real one, so a raw-length check reports false positives on French copy. **Pillar down-links are generated, never hand-written (ABA-393)**: `docs/marketing/seo/build_pillar_links.py` reads every article's frontmatter and appends a localized "Related guides" list of a pillar's cluster children below a `<!-- pillar-downlinks -->` marker in each of the 27 pillar files (3 pillars × 9 languages); it is idempotent (re-running replaces the block, never duplicates it), and its `CLUSTERS` map is what decides which pillar a new `pair` hangs off — **run it after adding any topic**, or the new article gets no inbound link from its pillar. Before this, pillars linked only sideways to each other and nothing down, so the whole cluster got no link equity from the highest-authority pages. **Internal links must be absolute `/blog/<lang>/<slug>/`** — the 3 Polish pillars used to cross-link with relative paths built from the old numbered slugs (`../01-budzet-domowy/`) while the real slug is `jak-prowadzic-budzet-domowy`, so all 6 inter-pillar links 404'd on our primary market; a relative sibling link in the built HTML is the signature of that bug. **Marketing landing + app subdomain (ABA-269)**: the apex `ai-budget.pl/` now serves a **static 9-language marketing landing** (generator `docs/marketing/landing/build_landing.py` → `docs/marketing/landing/site/`, committed; Polish default at `/`, other langs at `/<lang>/`, hreflang + `x-default`=en, JSON-LD `WebSite`+`Organization`+`SoftwareApplication`+`FAQPage`, clickable feature cards → pure-CSS `:checked` lightbox of per-language screenshots from `feature_graphics/by-language/`, footer with the MICODE sp. z o.o. logo). The Expo **SPA moved to `app.ai-budget.pl`** — separate `ai-budget-app-prod` `nginx:alpine` container at `/opt/ai-budget-app/html`, its own shared-nginx 443 block, cert SAN `app.ai-budget.pl` (certbot `--expand`), and `CORS_ORIGIN` += `https://app.ai-budget.pl` (in `/opt/ai-budget/.env.production`, api force-recreated). The landing "Log in" button → `app.ai-budget.pl`. `web-deploy.yml` now has **two rsync targets**: `apps/mobile/dist` (SPA) → `/opt/ai-budget-app/html`, and the assembled apex tree (landing + `/blog` + sitemap/robots) → `/opt/ai-budget-web/html`. Landing apex build env: `LANDING_BASE= ROBOTS="index,follow,max-image-preview:large"` (preview build used `LANDING_BASE=preview ROBOTS=noindex`); apex `sitemap.xml` covers the 9 landing URLs + blog URLs. **SEO hardening (ABA-271)**: the apex container's `default.conf` uses `try_files $uri $uri/ =404` + `error_page 404 /404.html` (real 404s, not soft-404 landing fallbacks — `build_landing.py` emits `404.html`); the `app.ai-budget.pl` shared-nginx block adds `add_header X-Robots-Tag "noindex, nofollow"` so the login-gated SPA is never indexed (the app container keeps `try_files … /index.html` for client routing); **the `admin.ai-budget.pl` block got the same `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow` header (ABA-342)** — the admin dashboard was previously crawlable and its `_next` assets/favicon surfaced in GSC as "Crawled - currently not indexed"; `build_blog.py` parses each article's FAQ section (`extract_faq`) and emits `FAQPage` JSON-LD for rich results. **Cross-site language preservation (ABA-280)**: switching between the landing and the blog must KEEP the language. The landing's "Blog" nav link + blog-cta + footer point to `/blog/<lang>/` for ALL 9 langs (the old `{lang if lang in ('en','pl') else 'en'}` fallback dumped de/es/fr/ru/ua/be/nl users onto the English blog). The blog's header brand + breadcrumb "Home" link back to the language-matched landing home via `home_url(lang)` (`/` for pl, `/<lang>/` otherwise — mirrors the landing's `lp()`); landing sub-page brands (about/cookies/legal) use `lp(lang)`. Do NOT hardcode `/` or an `en/pl`-only blog link in either generator. **In-body article links (ABA-435)**: articles write the product link as the bare `https://ai-budget.pl`, which IS the **Polish** homepage, so all 253 of them across the 9 languages used to point at a PL page — `/en/`, `/fr/`, `/nl/` received zero in-body inbound links from their own 23 articles. `to_html(body, lang)` in `build_blog.py` now rewrites that exact bare apex to `home_url(lang)` at render time (a deep link keeps its path). Keep writing the bare apex in the markdown; do NOT hand-write `/<lang>/` in an article. Anchor text is deliberately untouched: 79 links already read "AI Budget Assistant", and the other 174 sit inside "…free to start in the browser at ai-budget.pl" sentences where a keyword anchor breaks the grammar in 9 languages. **Landing vs `ai-budget` article intent (ABA-435)**: every landing title/H1 is `<expense noun> + AI` while the `ai-budget` cluster article is `AI + budget` — near-duplicate intent, which surfaced in Search Console as cannibalization on `gestion des dépenses … avec ia` (fr) and `kosten besparen met ai` (nl). The rule: the **landing owns the commercial AI intent**, the article is framed as an informational question (en "How AI Can Help You Budget (Honestly)", fr "Comment l'IA peut vous aider à gérer votre budget", nl "Hoe AI je helpt met budgetteren"). A retitle must keep `slug` and `pair` untouched (URLs + hreflang pairing) and stay inside content-plan.md's 60/155 budgets. The EN landing H1 carries the exact `AI budget` phrase, and the `WebSite` + both `SoftwareApplication` nodes carry `alternateName: ["AI Budget", "AI Budget App"]` — never `org_node()`, which is MICODE sp. z o.o., a different entity. **Internal linking + index schema (ABA-281)**: each landing page renders a "From the blog" section — `FROM_BLOG_PAIRS` (curated pairs) → `read_blog_index()` parses article frontmatter into `BLOG_IDX[(lang,pair)] → (slug,title)` → localized links to `/blog/<lang>/<slug>/`, so the high-authority homepage passes link equity to articles. The per-language blog index emits `Blog` + `BlogPosting[]` JSON-LD (was `CollectionPage`). **HowTo schema intentionally NOT added** — Google deprecated HowTo rich results in 2023; the numbered-step articles rely on content structure for featured snippets, not schema. **SEO/GEO fixes (ABA-342)**: (1) **valid language codes** — all three generators emit `bcp47(lang)` (maps `ua`→`uk`, the valid ISO 639-1 tag) for `<html lang>` / `hreflang` / JSON-LD `inLanguage` **only**; the internal dict key + URL segment stay `/ua/`, and `og:locale` stays `uk_UA`. Fixes broken hreflang reciprocity across the whole 9-lang cluster. (2) **Real sitemap dates** — `<lastmod>` is now the `git log -1 --format=%cs` commit date of each source `.md` (deterministic, memoized `git_date()` in `build_blog.py`, reused by `build_help`/`build_landing`), and the apex merge in `build_landing.py` carries the blog/help `<url>` blocks through **verbatim** so their per-URL `changefreq`/`priority` survive (was: one frozen `PUBLISH_DATE` for every URL + flattened priority + dropped changefreq; `PUBLISH_DATE` is now only the `git_date` fallback). (3) **GEO** — `build_landing.py` emits **`llms-full.txt`** (English markdown mirror of all blog + help bodies, built by importing `build_blog`/`build_help` at module load), referenced from `llms.txt` + linked in the footer; `robots.txt` gains explicit `Allow: /` groups for GPTBot/OAI-SearchBot/ChatGPT-User/ClaudeBot/anthropic-ai/Claude-Web/PerplexityBot/Perplexity-User/Google-Extended/CCBot. (4) **Structured data** — shared `@id` (`{SITE}/#organization` via `org_node()`, `{SITE}/#website`) so Organization/WebSite is one referenced node, not ~500 inline dupes; `Article`/`TechArticle` gain `author.url` + real `dateModified` + a visible byline; SVG logo carries `width/height`; the current-page (3rd) breadcrumb crumb is now visible; per-language blog index H1 is descriptive (was generic "Blog"); FAQ `**…?**` lines are promoted to `<h3>` in `to_html()`. (5) **Per-locale pricing** — `tier_price_display()` formats via `CURRENCY_FORMAT` (USD prefix+dot; EUR/PLN/RUB/UAH suffix+comma → `14,99 zł`, `4,49 €`, `99,00 ₴`); the JSON-LD Offer price stays machine-readable dot-decimal. (6) the `best-apps` article gained an "app type" **comparison table** in all 9 languages (liftable by AI answer engines); FR/NL landing typos fixed (`dépenses`, `gezinsfinanciën`). **Deliberately NOT done**: outbound citations (editorial), `sameAs` additions (no LinkedIn/Crunchbase/Wikidata yet), HTML `Cache-Control: no-store` (nginx on the VPS, not repo), PNG raster logo, per-language `llms-full.txt`. **Internal-linking + admin-noindex follow-up (ABA-342, remediating GSC "Crawled - currently not indexed")**: `PAIR_TO_HELP` (`build_blog.py`) links each blog article to its matching help section, and `HELP_TO_PAIR` (`build_help.py`, via `bb.blog_index()`) links each help page back to the matching blog article — both prepended into the existing "related" section — so deep help pages gain inbound internal links (they previously had almost none beyond their language index). Plus `admin.ai-budget.pl` got `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` in shared-nginx (see the SEO-hardening note above). **GA4 event tracking, landing only (ABA-434)**: the site had `gtag('config', …)` and **zero** `gtag('event', …)` calls, so GA4 could only ever report Enhanced Measurement's automatic events and the **Key events** report stayed permanently empty (38 sessions / 162 events over 25 days ≈ 3.2 automatic events per pageview, no business signal). `build_landing.py`'s `_TRACK_TPL`/`track_html()` is appended to **`consent_html()`**, which every landing page already ends with — so all 33 generated pages inherit it with no per-page edit; `404.html` and `/oauth/callback/` are the two exceptions (neither carries the consent block, and the OAuth relay must stay minimal so nothing delays its `budget://` redirect). Events: `cta_click` (any link to `app.ai-budget.pl`), `plan_select`, `billing_toggle`, `language_change`, `store_click`, `blog_click` — each carrying `language` + `page_type`, link events also `link_location` (nav/hero/band/pricing_card/blog_cta/from_blog/footer/body); `data-tier` on `.pcard` and `data-lang` on the switcher links are the only markup added. **One delegated capture-phase listener, never an `onclick` per anchor** — the markup comes out of a dozen separate f-strings, so a per-link attribute would have to be threaded through all of them and re-added by whoever writes the next section; intent is read off `href` + nearest section instead, which covers new sections for free. **Consent is deliberately NOT re-checked in the tracker**: `window.gtag` exists only once `_CONSENT_TPL`'s loader has run, and that runs only on `granted`, so a declining visitor is a structural no-op with no second copy of the consent rule to drift out of sync. `data-lang` emits **`bcp47(l)`, not the internal key** — `ua`→`uk`, so `language_from`/`language_to` can't report two names for Ukrainian inside one GA dimension. The cookie policy's "what is collected" section (pl+en) now states that interactions are recorded, not only page views. Both client-side faults fixed in `micode-ai/home` (MI-82 `dataLayer.push(args)` as a real array, which gtag.js silently discards because it tells its own commands apart by type; MI-79 measurement id pointing at another project's property) were checked here and are **absent** — the loader pushes `arguments` and `G-WMEFHYETVX` matches the property; do NOT "modernise" `function g(){dataLayer.push(arguments);}` into rest parameters. **Blog/help still send `page_view` only** (`build_blog.py` carries its own `_CONSENT_TPL` copy). GA-side and not code: **seven** event-scoped **custom dimensions** are now registered on property `ai-budget-assistant-c33a2` (`sc`-side ids: stream `G-WMEFHYETVX`) — `link_location`, `plan`, `billing_period`, `page_type`, `language` (display name **Page language**, so it does not collide with GA4's built-in browser Language), plus `language_from`/`language_to`, which the original five-item list missed even though `language_change` sends them and they are equally unreportable without registration. **`cta_click` is still NOT a Key event, and cannot be marked one yet**: this GA4 version only lets you star an event that has already been collected, and in 28 days the property has recorded ONLY the six Enhanced Measurement automatics (`click`, `first_visit`, `page_view`, `scroll`, `session_start`, `user_engagement`) — not one custom event. The tracker itself was verified against the deployed HTML and is correct (`var APP="https://app.ai-budget.pl"` matches the rendered `href` exactly, `.langlist` and `data-lang` are present), and gtag loads only on `cc-consent === 'granted'`, so the likeliest reading is that no consented visitor has clicked a CTA at this traffic level rather than a bug — but it is unconfirmed, and the Key event stays blocked until one real or test click lands. An **Internal traffic** filter is still missing (needs the office IP). **The product itself remains unmeasured** — `app.ai-budget.pl` serves no analytics and the mobile app has no analytics SDK (`google-services.json` is FCM-only), so the funnel goes dark at the click-through. **Signup attribution (ABA-436)**: that last sentence overstated it — the product IS measured, server-side and from our own DB (`AdminInvestorMetricsService` has retention/activation/DAU-WAU-MAU/growth/MRR/ARPPU/free-to-paid/trial-to-paid/churn; `AdminAnalyticsService` has registration trends). What was missing was the **join** between a landing click and the signup it caused, which is now carried in the URL rather than by an SDK. `app_url(loc, lang, plan=None)` in `build_landing.py` and `app_url(loc, lang, src)` in `build_blog.py` tag every link into the app with `?src=&loc=&lang=(&plan=)`; **`loc` MUST keep using the GA4 tracker's own `loc()` vocabulary** (nav/hero/band/pricing_card/footer/cta) or the click and the signup report different sections and the funnel splits in half, and **`build_help.py` must keep passing `src="help"`** — help pages render through the blog chrome, so the default would file all 369 of them as blog traffic. Ampersands are emitted as `&amp;` because `lang` is a real named character reference. Every tagged href still starts with the tracker's bare `APP` constant, so `cta_click` detection is unchanged — do not add a trailing slash to `APP`. Client side: `apps/mobile/src/services/attribution.{ts,types.ts,web.ts,native.ts}` (the `secureStorage`/`fileExport` three-file split) reads the params on **first arrival** into `localStorage` (**first touch wins**, never overwritten — so this says where a visit STARTED and is NOT cross-session attribution), called from `index.js` **before `expo-router` mounts** because the router owns the URL from its first render; native is a deliberate no-op (an install carries no query string — that needs the Play Install Referrer API). The value is attached in `auth.api.ts`, NOT threaded through `authStore.register`'s signature, so `/auth/register` and `/auth/google` both carry it by construction — a Google signup never passes through `register()`/`verifyEmail()` and would otherwise read as unattributed. Stored as four nullable columns `acquisition{Source,Location,Language,Plan}` + `@@index([acquisitionSource])` (migration `20260827120000_add_user_acquisition`) — flat rather than Json because the admin needs `groupBy`, which Prisma cannot do on Json; NULL is a legitimate "unknown" for every pre-existing user and every native install and must not be backfilled. **`AcquisitionDto` validates charset and length, never an allow-list of the values we emit today** — an allow-list would make the first new section name marketing invents fail every registration that carries it; losing a signup's attribution is acceptable, losing the signup is not, and the web capture drops a malformed value for the same reason. **No admin read surface yet** — the data accumulates with nowhere to be displayed.
- **Desktop web layout** (ABA-289, refined ABA-290): on web at `width >= 1024px` the SPA gets a laptop layout — a **full-width top bar** over everything + a left sidebar nav + the content filling the area right of the sidebar — instead of a stretched full-width mobile screen. Native and narrow web (`<1024`) are **unchanged**. Single source of truth `src/components/webLayout.constants.ts` (`DESKTOP_MIN_WIDTH=1024`, `CONTENT_MAX_WIDTH=1080` (charts cap only — see below), `SIDEBAR_WIDTH=240`, `TOP_BAR_HEIGHT=56`, `COLUMN_HORIZONTAL_PADDING=16`; pure `isDesktopWeb(width)` + `useIsDesktopWeb()` — gate is `Platform.OS === 'web' && width >= 1024`, so native never qualifies). `WebShell` (`src/components/WebShell.tsx`) wraps the root `<Stack>` in `app/_layout.tsx` (single insertion; overlays `UpdatePrompt`/`UpgradeGate`/`StatusBar` stay outside it) — off-desktop it returns `<>{children}</>` (the byte-for-byte mobile guarantee); on desktop it renders a separate `DesktopShell` child. **Why the `DesktopShell` split**: it keeps the route/auth/theme hooks off the native render path AND stays Rules-of-Hooks-safe when a browser resizes across 1024 (moving those hooks after an early return in one component would crash on resize — hook count would change). `DesktopShell`: unauthenticated / `(auth)` routes → a centered 480px card, no chrome; otherwise → `WebTopBar` + a row of `WebSidebar` + a **full-width content area** (no centered column, so the screen's own scrollbar sits at the window's right edge — a "general" page scroll with no dead gutters). `WebTopBar` (`src/components/WebTopBar.tsx`) = brand + active-section title (derived from `usePathname()`) on the left; account switcher + currency pill + alerts bell (badge from `alertStore`) + settings on the right. `WebSidebar` (`src/components/WebSidebar.tsx`) = the 5 tab routes only (brand + alerts/settings live in the top bar), active-route via `usePathname()`. `(tabs)/_layout.tsx` on desktop web hides BOTH the bottom tab bar (`tabBarStyle: { display: 'none' }`) and the per-screen tab header (`headerShown: !isDesktopWeb`, since `WebTopBar` replaces it); native/narrow preserved. `(tabs)/index.tsx` on desktop hides the hero control row + divider (top bar carries them), neutralizes the quick-actions overlap margin, and **lays the home widgets out in two balanced columns** (`webTwoCol`/`webCol`; widgets collected into an array, split by index parity). Charts/measured components size via `useContentWidth()` (`src/hooks/useContentWidth.ts` — capped to `CONTENT_MAX_WIDTH` on desktop web, raw `window.width` otherwise so mobile is unchanged): `WalletMonthlyChart`, `InteractiveLineChart` (former module-scope `Dimensions.get()` moved into the component body), `InteractiveBarChart`, `InsightCarousel`. Analytics tab stays single-column (only the home dashboard is 2-column). Gate + width logic unit-tested.
- **Navigation**: Expo Router. Screens in `app/`, tabs in `app/(tabs)/` — home, expenses, budgets, analytics, chat. `(tabs)/_layout.tsx` fires `Haptics.selectionAsync()` on every `tabPress` via `screenListeners` (`expo-haptics`, web no-op).
- **Root layout decomposition** (ABA-354): `app/_layout.tsx`'s `RootNavigator()` was the busiest file in the mobile app by commit count — every new push type/deep-link/cross-cutting feature added another `useEffect` to one component body. Decomposed into 7 focused hooks in `src/hooks/` (mirrors the `useHomeScreenData.ts` "Home screen decomposition" extraction): `useAppBootstrap` (fonts + db/language/auth init + splash-hide), `useColdStartGate` (pure `computeColdStartGate({isInitializing,isAuthenticated,fontsLoaded})` predicate + memoized hook — the single source of truth for the "app fully ready" gate, replacing a hand-copied boolean expression across two effects), `useAuthenticatedBootstrap` (subscription load + delayed push-registration/language-sync), `useBankNotificationCapture` (Android bank-notification capture subscribe), `useNotificationDeepLink` (cold-start push-notification response capture/flush), `useTripInviteDeepLink` (trip-invite universal-link auto-accept), `useGenericDeepLink` (`budget://`/`https://` deep links, deliberately gated WITHOUT `fontsLoaded` — do not "fix" that). `RootNavigator()` is now a thin composition of these hook calls plus the unchanged `<Stack.Screen>` route tree. Both deep-link flush effects (`useNotificationDeepLink`, `useTripInviteDeepLink`) consume the SAME `useColdStartGate()` value — the "keep the two deep-link paths gated symmetrically" invariant is now structural, not manual. `src/hooks/__tests__/useColdStartGate.test.ts` covers the gate predicate (previously zero test coverage on this logic). Any new cross-cutting mobile concern (new push type, new deep link, new bootstrap step) should get its own hook here, not another `useEffect` inline in `_layout.tsx`.
- **Header actions must be `textInverse` (ABA-450)**: the root stack paints every header `theme.colors.primary` (`headerStyle` in `app/_layout.tsx`) with `headerTintColor: theme.colors.textInverse`, so a `headerRight` painted `theme.colors.primary` is orange-on-orange (**1.0:1**) and one painted `theme.colors.danger` is `#FF6B6B` on `#E37F2B` (**1.04:1**) — both invisible, not merely low-contrast. Nine such actions across eight screens shipped that way and were recoloured to `textInverse`; a destructive one (the shopping list's "Clear checked", which soft-deletes every ticked item with no confirmation and no undo) was among them. **Do not colour a header action with a semantic token** — red does not read as destructive when it cannot be seen; the confirmation dialog carries that meaning. Also: `headerTitleAlign: 'center'` is global, so a centred title is constrained by the wider of the two side elements — a long `headerRight` **label** silently truncates the title (`Lista zaku...`), which is why the shopping list's action moved into the screen body rather than being recoloured in place. Prefer a compact icon in `headerRight`; put a long labelled action in the screen.
- **First-run onboarding**: a brand-new user is routed once to `app/get-started.tsx` — a primary "scan a receipt" card plus rows for voice / manual entry / import (each just `router.push`ing an existing screen; no new transaction-entry logic anywhere), plus a "do this later" link that `router.replace`s onward — instead of landing on an empty dashboard. Pure predicate `shouldShowFirstRun({gateOpen, seen, hasTransactions, canEdit})` (`src/features/onboarding/shouldShowFirstRun.ts`) is a strict AND of all four; any single one being false suppresses the screen (viewers excluded because they cannot create a transaction at all; `seen` is a one-time MMKV flag in `firstRunStore`). Wired by `useFirstRunOnboarding(gateOpen)` (`src/hooks/`), one of the cross-cutting hooks `RootNavigator` composes (see **Root layout decomposition** above) — it takes `useColdStartGate()`'s value as `gateOpen`, the same gate the deep-link hooks use. **Trigger placement**: deliberately NOT on the email-verification screen — Google sign-in routes straight to `/(tabs)` and never passes through verification, so hanging the trigger there would silently exclude every Google sign-up; `RootNavigator` is the one point every sign-in path converges on. **Count source**: `countTransactions(accountId)` (`src/db/expenseRepository.ts`) reads SQLite directly (expenses `is_deleted=0 AND NOT is_planned` + incomes `is_deleted=0`), never the Zustand stores — the stores fill from SQLite only *after* the cold-start gate opens, so an established user's in-memory list is empty for a moment on every cold start, long enough to route them into onboarding on top of their own data. `useHydrationStore.isHydrating` cannot substitute: it defaults `false`, flips `true` only while `hydrateTransactions()` runs, and is `false` again once it finishes — false both before hydration starts and after it ends, i.e. false in exactly the window this check needs to detect. **An established user must never reach this screen — TWO guards, both load-bearing**: (1) when the count comes back **> 0** the hook calls `markSeen()` itself and returns, because `seen` is otherwise set only by `get-started.tsx`'s own handlers, which an established user never visits — so before this the flag stayed `false` for the entire installed base forever, and every re-evaluation was one empty local account away from firing; (2) the whole check is evaluated **at most once per app session** via a `checked` ref set the moment the query is *issued* (not when it resolves). Guard 2 is what makes the account-switch path structurally unreachable rather than merely improbable: `currentAccountId` is (correctly) an effect dependency, and the account switcher sits in every tab header **and** the home hero, plus `useTripInviteDeepLink` and push deep-links switch accounts too — an account the user has never opened has no local rows, counts zero, and used to route them straight into onboarding. A **failed** count returns without marking seen (not evidence either way; burning the flag on one transient SQLite error would cost a genuinely new user the screen forever). **Web bails outright** (`Platform.OS === 'web'`): `db/client.web.ts`'s `executeSql` returns `[]`, so the count is always 0 there and every established web user got the screen — other SQLite-backed features degrade on web by showing *less data*, this one degraded by interrupting the user with a screen whose premise is false (precedent: `captureCurrentLocation`, `NotificationCapture.ios.ts`). **`welcome` is a revenue guard, not a convenience**: `app/welcome.tsx` is a pricing screen (Free/Pro/Business plan comparison) despite its name, and only the email-verification redirect routes through it; `get-started.tsx`'s `finish()` sends the user to `/welcome` **only** when it is asked to, else straight to `/(tabs)` — Google sign-in has never reached the pricing screen and this feature must not start showing it to that audience as a side effect. That destination is carried **out-of-band**, in a non-persisted `firstRunStore.nextAfter` set *synchronously by `verify-email.tsx` before* it navigates (the `?next=welcome` param is kept too, and `finish()` honours either) — the hook stands down whenever `nextAfter` is set. A pathname guard cannot do this job: nothing orders a SQLite promise's continuation against React committing a route change, so a param-based hand-off could only narrow the window in which this hook's param-less `router.replace('/get-started')` clobbers the email path's and silently deletes the pricing screen from the registration funnel. Setting a flag *before* navigating, rather than observing the route *after*, is deterministic. **Do NOT reintroduce `usePathname()` here**: `RootNavigator` renders 93 `<Stack.Screen>` elements with freshly allocated inline `options`, and that subscription re-rendered all of it on every navigation in the app, on the JS thread during transition animations. **Auto-advance fires on focus, not on the count change**: `get-started.tsx` records `pendingFinish` when the expense+income count goes from zero, and acts on it in a `useFocusEffect` — `finish()` is a global `router.replace`, and the entry screens are `push`ed on top, so firing it immediately replaced `expense/receipt.tsx` underneath its own open success Alert (and raced `expense/new.tsx`'s `router.back()`, parking the user back on `/get-started` right after their first expense).
- **"What's New" spotlight** (ABA-470): a one-time, dismissible discovery nudge for already-shipped features that have no other announcement path — Safe-to-Spend, Inflation Shield, Personal Inflation Index, Community Price Map, Fat Finder, Financial Wrapped, receipt price-check, the shopping-list "where's cheapest" compare, and the AI chat's shopping-list tools all shipped silently and are otherwise only found by reading Settings closely or stumbling into the right screen. **Static, client-only, no migration, no endpoint**: `src/features/whatsNew/whatsNewEntries.ts` is an ordered, append-only `WHATS_NEW_ENTRIES: WhatsNewEntry[]` (oldest → newest; `id` permanent once shipped — it is what the persisted last-seen pointer references). Every entry carries a `route` (a live feature screen) or a `helpSectionId` (must exist in `src/help/sections.ts`, resolves to `/help/{id}`) — never neither, so "Tell me more" always goes somewhere; an optional `tier` (`'pro'|'business'`) drives a plain informational chip only — it does **not** gate navigation, since the destination screen already gates itself via `useUpgradeStore` exactly as any other entry point would. **Entry copy is plain English content, not an i18n key** — same treatment as blog/help markdown, deliberately kept out of the 9-locale system; only the mechanism's own chrome (`whatsNew.*`) is localized. **Show-at-most-one-and-never-a-digest**: pure `resolveWhatsNewOutcome({gateOpen, lastSeenId, firstRunSeen, entries})` (`src/features/whatsNew/resolveWhatsNewOutcome.ts`, mirrors `shouldShowFirstRun`'s pure-predicate-plus-hook split) always surfaces only the single newest entry, regardless of how many versions a returning user skipped — simplicity was chosen explicitly over a "digest of everything you missed" to keep the mechanism structurally incapable of becoming noisy. `useWhatsNewStore` (`src/stores/whatsNewStore.ts`, MMKV id `whats-new`, pure `resolveLastSeenId` mirrors `firstRunStore.resolveSeen`) persists only `lastSeenId: string | null` — `null` means "never evaluated on this device," which is genuinely ambiguous between "brand-new install" and "existing user meeting this mechanism for the first time after an app update introduces it," so the decision reads `firstRunStore.seen` to disambiguate: not yet onboarded (`seen === false`) silently seeds `lastSeenId` to the newest entry with **no nudge shown** (a "look at this new feature" prompt before the user's first transaction would be noise stacked on unfinished onboarding, not discovery), while an already-onboarded existing user falls through to seeing the newest entry — exactly the target audience the idea describes. `useWhatsNewSpotlight(gateOpen)` (`src/hooks/`) wires the pure decision to the stores and to navigation, evaluated **at most once per app session** via a `checked` ref (same guard shape as `useFirstRunOnboarding`), composed into `RootNavigator` alongside the other cross-cutting hooks (see **Root layout decomposition**). `WhatsNewSpotlight` (`src/components/whatsNew/`) is a self-contained bottom-sheet component (mirrors `SafeToSpendSheet`'s slide-up `Modal` style, never a blocking center-screen modal) mounted unconditionally in `app/_layout.tsx` next to `<UpdatePrompt />`; both `dismiss()` and the "Tell me more" CTA call `markSeen`. **Browsable on demand**: `app/whats-new.tsx` lists the full history newest-first (card style mirrors `app/help/index.tsx`), reachable any time from a Settings-hub row — browsing this list never calls `markSeen`, only the one-time spotlight does. A future headline feature should append one entry to `WHATS_NEW_ENTRIES` as part of its own PR, the same review weight as its i18n/help/CLAUDE.md update steps — see `docs/contracts/whats-new-spotlight.md`.
- **MainActivity must call `super.onCreate(null)`** (ABA-188): `apps/mobile/android/app/src/main/java/com/budget/assistant/MainActivity.kt` MUST pass `null` (NOT `savedInstanceState`) to `super.onCreate`. `react-native-screens` cannot restore its fragments — when Android restores the Activity after the OS kills the backgrounded process (common on Samsung deep-sleep), passing `savedInstanceState` throws `IllegalStateException: Screen fragments should never be restored` in native `MainActivity.onCreate`, **before any JS runs**, so the JS `ErrorBoundary` never catches it → app crashes on launch ("crashing frequently"). RN/Expo own screen state on the JS side. Do NOT let an `expo prebuild` regress this back to `savedInstanceState`. (Note: the mobile app has **no crash reporting** — `@sentry/react-native` is not a real dep, `ErrorBoundary.componentDidCatch` only `console.error`s — so native crashes are only visible via Play Console Android vitals / `adb logcat`.)
- **Keyboard handling**: **pure-JS, no native module** (builds with the local Windows Android toolchain — `react-native-keyboard-controller`'s Fabric codegen blows past the Windows 260-char MAX_PATH limit; do NOT reintroduce it or any keyboard native module). `src/hooks/useKeyboardHeight.ts` tracks height via RN `Keyboard` events (works under New Arch + Android edge-to-edge, where `KeyboardAvoidingView`/`adjustResize` are unreliable). Two wrappers in `src/components/`: `KeyboardAwareScreen` (drop-in for a form's root `ScrollView`: Android pads content by keyboard height, iOS `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets`) and `KeyboardAvoidingScreen` (drop-in for `KeyboardAvoidingView` — pads bottom to lift docked inputs/footers; accepts+ignores `behavior`). Form screens import `KeyboardAvoidingScreen as KeyboardAvoidingView`.
- **Metro crawl scoping on Windows** (ABA-255): Watchman has no Windows builds, so Metro falls back to its slower JS Node crawler. `apps/mobile/metro.config.js` keeps `watchFolders = [workspaceRoot]` (needed for hoisted deps + `packages/shared-*`) but extends `config.resolver.blockList` to exclude paths the mobile bundle never imports — `apps/api`, `apps/admin` (each with its own `node_modules`) and the `node_modules/@budget/(api|mobile)` + `node_modules/admin` workspace back-symlinks. `blockList` IS the file-map `ignorePattern` and the Node crawler applies it **at directory level** (skips the whole subtree before recursing), so this is what keeps the cold-start crawl from walking the entire ~150k-file monorepo and hanging the dev bundler before `index.bundle` can build. The default Expo `blockList` is preserved (combined into one RegExp with the default's flags to avoid Metro's `combine()` "different flags" error); `packages/shared-*` and `react-native` are NOT excluded (the app imports them); patterns use `[\\/]` so they match on Windows AND Linux/CI (web export). Do NOT widen `watchFolders` back to bare `workspaceRoot` without this blockList, and do NOT exclude `shared-types`/`shared-utils`.
- **State**: 29 Zustand stores in `src/stores/` — `authStore`, `accountStore`, `alertStore`, `expenseStore`, `incomeStore`, `budgetStore`, `categoryStore`, `tagStore`, `projectStore`, `walletStore`, `chatStore`, `insightsStore`, `exchangeRateStore`, `subscriptionStore`, `themeStore`, `widgetVisibilityStore`, `quickActionStore`, `debtStore`, `encryptionStore`, `gamificationStore`, `goalStore`, `investmentStore`, `referralStore`, `reportStore`, `scenarioStore`, `userSubscriptionStore`, `merchantSuggestionStore`, `merchantRulesStore`, `upgradeStore`
- **walletStore split** (ABA-447): `walletStore.ts` had grown to 765 lines mixing wallet-balance CRUD, currency-exchange CRUD, account-transfer CRUD, and read-side aggregation (`loadWallet`/`computeWalletSummary`/`loadAccountSummaries`/`loadMonthlyHistory`) — the same shape of problem `docs/tech-debt/expense-store-god-class.md` (closed) already fixed for `expenseStore.ts`. Mirroring the **actual** `expenseStore.ts`→`expenseSync.ts` extraction (function modules sharing the store's `set`/`get`, not literal separate Zustand stores — that would force edits across the ~19 files importing `useWalletStore`), the CRUD bodies moved out into `walletBalanceActions.ts` (`setInitialBalanceAction`/`updateInitialBalanceAction`/`removeBalanceAction`), `currencyExchangeActions.ts` (`addExchangeAction`/`updateExchangeAction`/`deleteExchangeAction`), `accountTransferActions.ts` (`addTransferAction`/`updateTransferAction`/`deleteTransferAction`), and the server-pull/merge half of `loadWallet` into `walletSync.ts` (`syncWalletFromServer`). `walletStore.ts` is now a 283-line composition: same `WalletState` shape, same `useWalletStore` public API (zero consumer changes), each action a one-line delegate into its module, keeping only the cross-cutting aggregation logic inline. A new wallet-adjacent CRUD feature should extend one of the three action files, not regrow `walletStore.ts`.
- **authStore split** (ABA-467): `authStore.ts` had grown to 826 lines with one flat `AuthState` mixing session lifecycle (`initialize`/`login`/`register`/`googleLogin`/`biometricLogin`/`logout`), password-recovery/verification (`forgotPassword`/`resetPassword`/`verifyEmail`/`resendVerification`), and user-preference setters (`setCurrency`/`setPaymentInfo`/`setPaymentMethods`) — the same shape the `walletStore` split above fixed. `AuthState` (plus `AuthStoreSet`/`AuthStoreGet` zustand-typed aliases) moved to `authStore.types.ts` so the two new action files can import the type without a runtime circular dependency on `authStore.ts` itself. Session-lifecycle bodies (+ the module-scope `persistSession`/`applyRestoredSession` helpers and the `isLoggingOut` guard) moved to `authSessionActions.ts` (`initializeAction`/`loginAction`/`registerAction`/`googleLoginAction`/`biometricLoginAction`/`logoutAction`); recovery bodies moved to `authRecoveryActions.ts` (`forgotPasswordAction`/`resetPasswordAction`/`verifyEmailAction`/`resendVerificationAction`). `authStore.ts` is now a ~110-line composition: same `AuthState` shape (re-exported from `authStore.types.ts`), same `useAuthStore` public API (zero consumer changes), each action a one-line delegate; `updateUser`/`setCurrency`/`setPaymentInfo`/`setPaymentMethods`/`setTokens`/`clearError` stay inline since they already delegate to `utils/currency.ts`/`utils/paymentInfo.ts`. A new auth-adjacent feature (2FA, another OAuth provider) should extend `authSessionActions.ts` (or a new sibling action file), not regrow `authStore.ts`.
- **`quickActionStore`/`widgetVisibilityStore` share one factory** (ABA-456): both are an MMKV-backed Zustand store holding a `Record<K, boolean>` visibility map + a `K[]` order array, with `toggle`/`setVisible`/`reorder`/`resetOrder` actions — the two had drifted into near-duplicate hand-written implementations with one undocumented behavioral difference. `src/stores/orderedVisibilityStore.ts` exports `createOrderedVisibilityStore<K>(keys, options)`, the shared implementation; `quickActionStore.ts` and `widgetVisibilityStore.ts` are now thin call sites (same public exports, zero consumer changes — `QUICK_ACTION_KEYS`/`DEFAULT_VISIBILITY`/`useQuickActionStore`/`resolveVisibility`/`resolveOrder` and `WIDGET_KEYS`/`useWidgetVisibilityStore`). The one real difference — a missing-from-persisted-order key is **appended** (quickActionStore) vs **inserted at its intended position** so a new high-priority widget like `familyFeed` surfaces at the top for existing users (widgetVisibilityStore) — is now an explicit, documented `insertMissingByPosition` factory option (default `false`), not a silent divergence. A third reorderable-visibility settings surface should call the factory, not copy either store file.
- **Monetization / upgrade paywall** (ABA-265/266): the mobile `Paywall` (`src/components/Paywall.tsx`) is surfaced app-wide via `upgradeStore` (Zustand: `visible`/`feature`/`requiredTier` + `show()`/`hide()`) and a single `<UpgradeGate>` modal mounted once in `app/_layout.tsx` — no per-screen modal duplication. Any `403` with `code: 'TIER_REQUIRED'` / `requiredTier` (or the monthly AI-limit 403 in `chatStore.sendMessage`) calls `useUpgradeStore.getState().show()`. Gated surfaces: AI chat monthly limit, Spending Story, Fat Finder, Analytics AI-Insights (compact "Pro" lock via `AiInsightsSection` `proGated` prop), and the member-invite limit (`account/invite.tsx`). **API tier gating**: `SubscriptionTierGuard` + `@RequireTier('pro')` gate `GET /insights/ai-charts`, `POST /insights/story`, `POST /insights/fat-finder` (business passes via numeric tier ranking); the guard throws `{ message, requiredTier, currentTier, code: 'TIER_REQUIRED' }`. **Subscription row upsert race (ABA-314)**: `SubscriptionsService.getOrCreateSubscription(userId)` is the funnel for `getCurrent`/`getUsageStats`/all AI-limit checks; Prisma `upsert` is NOT atomic under concurrency, so a new user's parallel startup requests could both INSERT and one hit P2002 on the unique `userId` (crashed `GET /subscriptions/usage`). It now catches P2002 and re-fetches the row the concurrent request created — apply the same catch-P2002-then-findUnique pattern to any hot-path `upsert` on a unique key. Trial free AI limit is `TRIAL_REQUEST_LIMITS.free = 50`; `trial-reminder.cron` sends both a T-3 and a T-1 reminder (`trialReminderIn3*` in `notification-i18n`, 9 langs). Plan feature bullets render from **local** i18n `subscription.features.*` keys, NOT the English `getPlans()` `features[]` strings. New i18n keys `subscription.limitReachedBody` + `subscription.startTrial` in all 9 locales. Note: Story/Fat Finder/AI-Insights were previously ungated (free) — this changes positioning to Pro-only; keep `user_docs/12-subscription.md` in sync.
- **Base/display currency** (ABA-187): the app-wide base currency is `user.currencyCode`. Change it via `authStore.setCurrency(code)`, which wraps the pure `src/utils/currency.ts` `applyCurrencyChange(next, deps)` helper — optimistic `updateUser({ currencyCode })` first (this is what `exchangeRateStore`'s subscription on `user.currencyCode` watches → it reloads rates and recomputes all converted totals), then fire-and-forget `api.updateProfile({ currencyCode })` (`console.warn` on failure, offline-tolerant). Both the `AccountSwitcher` currency chips and `settings/profile.tsx` route through `setCurrency` — do NOT duplicate the optimistic/persist logic.
- **Theme customization** (ABA-372): light/dark stay the built-in bases; on top, a user-chosen **accent color** recolors only brand tokens. **Persisted per-user in the DB** (`User.themeMode String @default("system")` + `User.accentColor String?` — null = default `#E37F2B`; migration `20260721000000_add_user_theme_prefs`), synced via `PATCH /users/me` (server validates `themeMode ∈ {light,dark,system}` and `accentColor` = `#RRGGBB`|null; both returned by `getProfile` + all 5 `auth.service.ts` response `user` blocks); `AuthResponse.user` + `User` entity carry both. Mirrors the `currencyCode` pattern: `themeStore` setters (`setMode`/`setAccent`) write MMKV then call the pure `utils/theme.ts` `applyThemePatch` (optimistic `authStore.updateUser` + fire-and-forget `api.updateProfile`, `console.warn` on failure); `setCustomAccent` is MMKV-only (last custom color, not synced). **Source-of-truth precedence** in `ThemeContext`: `mode = user?.themeMode ?? localMode ?? 'system'` (falls through to the MMKV mirror so a pre-feature explicit choice survives upgrade); `accent = user ? (user.accentColor ?? null) : (localAccent ?? null)` (user is sole source when logged in — do NOT let accent fall through to local, it would defeat an explicit clear). **Colors**: pure `theme/deriveAccent.ts` (`hexToHsl`/`hslToHex`/`relativeLuminance`/`readableOn`/`deriveAccentColors`) maps one accent → 10 brand tokens (`primary`/`primaryDark`/`primaryLight`/`secondary`/`accent`/`textLink`/`tabBarActive`/`messageBubbleUser` + on-accent `textInverse`/`messageBubbleUserText` chosen by luminance); `primaryDark` darkens in light mode, lightens in dark. `ThemeContext` merges `{ ...base, ...deriveAccentColors(base, accent, isDark) }` so `getTabBarTheme`/`getStackHeaderTheme` inherit the accent free. **`textInverse` overload gotcha**: it's the on-accent fg on `primary` surfaces (correctly accent-derived) AND the fg on fixed `success`/`danger` buttons (must NOT follow the accent) — so `colors.ts` has an always-white `onSemantic` token and semantic-colored confirm buttons use it (`income/*`, `goals/*`) — a full audit confirmed those were the ONLY `textInverse`-on-semantic sites (every other solid `success`/`danger`/`warning` button already uses `textPrimary`/hardcoded `#FFFFFF`/`onSemantic`, or is a tint/legend/progress-fill with no `textInverse` fg). **Quick-action icons** (`QuickActionIcon`) default their symbol + rounded-frame color to `theme.colors.primary` (accent) so the home quick-action strip follows the accent too — the baked orange `ICON_COLOR` in the inlined SVG strings is a render-time placeholder, replaced with the resolved color; the green income/invoice variants still pass an explicit `color`. The `ColorPicker` live preview shows a sample button + tab dot + chat bubble (spec §4). **Legacy migration**: `seedLegacyThemeFromLocal()` (themeStore, one-time via MMKV `themeSeededV1` flag, called from `useAuthenticatedBootstrap`) pushes a pre-feature explicit MMKV mode to the server so biometric-login (which overwrites `user.themeMode` from `getProfile`) doesn't reset it. **UI**: `settings/appearance.tsx` Accent section — preset swatches (`theme/presetAccents.ts`: `DEFAULT_ACCENT` + `PRESET_ACCENTS`) + a Custom swatch opening the in-house `components/ColorPicker.tsx` (hue slider + S/L square via `PanResponder` + hex input + live preview; NO native color-picker dep — `expo-linear-gradient` only) in a bottom-sheet. Accent is a personal preference → NOT `canEdit`-gated (available to viewers, like currency). 7 `settings.accent*`/`settings.customColor`/`settings.pickColor`/`settings.applyColor`/`settings.resetAccent`/`settings.invalidColor` i18n keys × 9 locales. Tests: `deriveAccent` (hex↔HSL, luminance on-accent pick, light/dark direction), `applyThemePatch` (optimistic + gated persist), API `users.controller.spec` (hex/mode validation).
- **Local DB**: SQLite via Drizzle ORM. Schema in `src/db/schema/index.ts`. 18 repositories in `src/db/*Repository.ts` use raw `executeSql()` — `account`, `accountTransfer`, `budget`, `budgetCategory`, `category`, `chat`, `currencyExchange`, `encryption`, `expense`, `expenseItem`, `gamification`, `income`, `investment`, `project`, `split`, `syncMetadata`, `tag`, `wallet`
- **API client**: `src/services/api.ts` — barrel that re-exports a composed `api` singleton. Base infrastructure lives in `src/services/http-client.ts` (`HttpClient` class: `request()`, `getAuthToken()`, `refreshToken()`, `setAccountIdGetter()`, `setLogoutHandler()`). Domain methods are split across 17 files: `auth.api.ts`, `users.api.ts`, `expenses.api.ts`, `incomes.api.ts`, `budgets.api.ts`, `categories.api.ts`, `analytics.api.ts`, `ai.api.ts`, `accounts.api.ts`, `wallet.api.ts`, `investments.api.ts`, `encryption.api.ts`, `reports.api.ts`, `subscriptions.api.ts`, `import-bank.api.ts`, `userSubscriptions.api.ts`, `alerts.api.ts`. All stores import `{ api }` from `api.ts` unchanged. Auto-injects `X-Account-Id` header, auto JWT refresh, 401 → logout.
- **Offline-first**: write to SQLite first, queue sync via `syncQueue` table, sync to server when online. `SyncService.pushChanges()` processes the `changes[]` array in parallel batches of 10 (ABA-152) so large mobile resyncs are faster without contention from unbounded parallelism. **Logging (ABA-157)**: a failed server **push** while offline is an *expected* outcome (the row stays `pending` and re-syncs later) — log it with `console.warn`, NEVER `console.error`. RN's dev LogBox renders any `console.error` as a blocking full-screen red overlay, which users perceive as an offline-add crash. The `.catch` on fire-and-forget `api.create*/update*/delete*` calls in `expenseStore`/`incomeStore` uses `warn`; only genuine local-SQLite failures stay `console.error`. (`no-console` ESLint rule allows `warn`/`error`.) **Server-side create idempotency (ABA-316)**: because the client resends the same create (retry, double-tap, lost response) with the same `localId`, direct `POST` create endpoints that write `clientId` under a `@@unique([accountId, clientId])` MUST be idempotent — pre-check `findUnique({ accountId_clientId })` and return the existing row, and catch a concurrent-race `P2002` **outside** the `$transaction` (Postgres poisons the tx — [ABA-313]) to re-fetch. Done for `BudgetsService.create`. **Audit complete (ABA-392)**: `expenses`/`incomes`/`projects`/`tags` are idempotent by construction (`upsert` on their `clientId` unique) and `budgets` catches P2002 — `categories` was the one real gap, and it slipped through precisely because its unique is `@@unique([accountId, name, type])` rather than one on `clientId` (`Category` has no `clientId` at all), so the upsert-on-clientId pattern never applied to it. **`CategoriesService.create` (ABA-392)**: the duplicate lookup must NOT filter `isDeleted` — the unique covers soft-deleted rows too, so one `findFirst` has to find both cases, and private `reuseExisting` then returns a **live** row untouched (copying the incoming icon/color over would silently restyle a category the user already customised) or revives a soft-deleted one with the incoming values. Only checking `isDeleted: true` (what it did before) let a live duplicate fall through to `create()` and throw P2002 as an unhandled **500** — reachable from the app, from AI `create_category`, and from all three bots, where the throw fails the whole confirm request. `type` defaults to `'expense'` as the schema does, because the non-controller callers pass a bare object with no DTO validation and an `undefined` type drops the filter from the Prisma `where`, matching a same-named category of the *other* type. Mobile mirror: `categoryStore.createCategory` returns `getCategoryByName(name, type)` when it hits instead of writing a second local row — the POST is fire-and-forget with its result discarded, so a rejected duplicate would otherwise leave a local row **permanently** unreconcilable (no `clientId` for the pull to match on). **Child-table lookups must use the RESOLVED server PK (ABA-374)**: the client addresses every row by its **local** id (server `clientId`), so an endpoint that resolves `OR:[{id},{clientId}]` for the parent and then reuses the **raw route param** for a child table keyed by the parent's PK silently matches nothing. This is what broke expense line items — `ExpensesService.getItems/createItem/updateItem/removeItem` passed the raw `:id` into `expense_items.expenseId` (an FK to `expenses.id`), so items vanished on read, `createItem` would have thrown FK `P2003`, and update/remove 404'd. Fixed by a private `resolveExpensePk(accountId, id)` (light `select:{id}` mirror of `getReceiptImage`'s lookup) used by all four handlers. **Audit completed (ABA-454)**: the same class was still open in four more modules — `InvestmentsService` (`removeHolding`/`createTransaction`/`updateTransaction`/`removeTransaction`/`getTransactions`/`getAssetPriceHistory`, new `resolveHoldingPk`), `ProjectsService` (`findOne`/`update`/`remove`/`addExpense`/`removeExpense`/`addIncome`/`removeIncome`/`getAnalytics`, new `resolveProjectPk`/`resolveExpensePk`/`resolveIncomePk`), `DebtsService.recordRepayment` (now stores the *resolved* server PK in `relatedDebtExpenseId`/`relatedDebtIncomeId`, not the raw possibly-local `debtId`), and `BudgetsService.findOne` (every other budget method funnels through it, so this one fix covers the whole module). All four now resolve `OR:[{id},{clientId}]` before reusing an id as a foreign key, mirroring `TagsService.resolveExpensePk`/`resolveIncomePk`. **This class of bug is native-invisible**: `db/client.web.ts` is a no-op mock, so web has no SQLite fallback and the API is the only source — a wrong-key server read shows up as missing data on web while native happily reads its local copy. When a bug reproduces only on web, suspect an id-resolution mismatch on the server before suspecting the web bundle. Companion mobile guard: `expenseStore.loadExpenseItems` keeps the in-memory items when the server fetch **throws** (offline, or the fire-and-forget create hasn't landed — a just-scanned receipt 404s for a moment) and clears them only on a *successful* empty response, so a genuine delete still propagates. **Test coverage (ABA-356)**: `apps/api/src/modules/sync/sync.service.spec.ts` covers `processExpenseChange`/`processIncomeChange`/`processTagChange` happy paths, `syncVersion` conflict resolution, `processRelationChange` for `expense_tag` (the ABA-167 payload-id resolution path), `tripExpenseShare`, and `shopping_list`/`shopping_list_item`. **`processBudgetChange`/`processCategoryChange` deliberately reject the change (ABA-423)** — budgets/categories go through their own REST endpoints, not this sync-queue path, and a `'budget'`/`'category'` `SyncChange` should never be emitted by the mobile app; the two handlers now log an error and return `status:'error'` instead of the old silent `status:'success'` no-op (which would have marked a real such change synced on the client while the server discarded it). Do not assume these entity types are actually synced through this path, and do not "fix" the error return by implementing real persistence unless budgets/categories genuinely need offline-queue sync — the REST endpoints are the source of truth. **Sync service split (ABA-468)**: `sync.service.ts` had grown to 990 lines mixing `pushChanges`/`pullChanges` orchestration with 12 independently-evolving per-entity handlers. Each `process<Entity>Change` (expense, expense_item, budget, category, income, tag, project, shopping_list, shopping_list_item, portfolio_holding, investment_transaction, tripExpenseShare) is now a standalone exported function under `modules/sync/handlers/`, one file per entity, taking a uniform `(ctx: SyncHandlerContext, accountId, userId, change)` shape — `SyncHandlerContext` (new `sync-types.ts`, alongside the previously-inline `SyncResult`/`ExpenseRecord`/`BudgetRecord`/`CategoryRecord`) bundles `prisma`/`expensesService`/`incomesService`/`communityPrices`/`logger` so handlers are plain functions, not Nest-injectable classes. `handlers/index.ts` exports a `SYNC_ENTITY_HANDLERS` registry map keyed by `entityType` that `processChange` looks up instead of a growing switch. `processRelationChange` (the 5 junction-table relation types) stays inline in `sync.service.ts` alongside `pushChanges`/`pullChanges`/`processChange` — each of its branches is a thin, near-identical upsert, not an independently-evolving entity, so it wasn't split further. `sync.service.ts` is now 328 lines; `sync.controller.ts`'s `import { SyncService, SyncResult } from './sync.service'` is unchanged (re-exported from `sync-types.ts`). A new sync entity handler should get its own file under `handlers/` plus a `SYNC_ENTITY_HANDLERS` entry, not another private method on `SyncService`.
- **Expense ↔ project association**: an expense's `projectId` is **not** an `expenses` column — it lives in the `project_expenses` join table (mirrored on server as `ProjectExpense`). The generic `updateExpense(id, updates)` store action canNOT manage it (the field isn't a column, and `undefined` is dropped from the JSON body so clearing is impossible). Use `expenseStore.setExpenseProject(expenseId, projectId | null)` instead — it updates in-memory state, the local SQLite join table (`addExpenseToProject`/`removeExpenseFromProject` in `projectRepository`), and the server via `api.updateExpense(id, { projectId })` (explicit `null` clears; `UpdateExpenseDto.projectId` accepts `string | null`, service soft-deletes the old row). Both `expense/new.tsx` (create) and `expense/[id].tsx` (edit) render the shared `ProjectPicker` component.
- **Expense merchant field** (ABA-140): `Expense.merchant?: string` is a plain free-text column (Prisma `merchant String?` + `@@index([accountId, merchant])`; mobile SQLite `merchant TEXT`). Auto-populated from receipt OCR (mobile `expense/receipt.tsx`, Telegram/WhatsApp `photo.handler.ts`) and bank/Wise import commit; manually editable via the shared `MerchantInput` component (free text + autocomplete suggestions from `expenseStore.getDistinctMerchants()`). Encrypted client-side **like `description`** — it's in `ENCRYPTION_FIELDS.expense.tier1`, so push paths (`addExpense`, `syncPendingExpenses`) run it through `maybeEncrypt` and the `loadExpenses` pull merge reads `decrypted.merchant`. Shown on the detail screen + as a secondary line on Expenses-tab rows. Filtering is **client-side only** (no `?merchant=` API param): `ExpenseFilters.merchants: string[]` (multi-select, default `[]`) — matches any of the selected merchants; the search box also matches merchant substring. The Expenses-tab category + merchant filters share **one filter row** (ABA-142): `[Category ▾] [Merchants N ▾]  ……  −total`. The merchant picker is **multi-select** (toggle stays open; "All merchants" clears; "Done" closes). The right-aligned total is `sumConverted(filteredItems, baseCurrency, rates)` from `src/utils/total.ts`. Income tab shows `[Category ▾] …… +total` (no merchant pill). Clearing on edit sends `''` (treated as absent everywhere). No backfill; income has no merchant field.
- **Date pickers on web — always go through `src/components/DatePicker.tsx` (ABA-381)**: `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` (8.4.4) ships **no web implementation** — the package has `datetimepicker.{android,ios,windows}.js` but no `.web.js`, so Metro resolves the fallback `src/datetimepicker.js`, whose whole body is `console.warn('DateTimePicker is not supported on: web'); return null`. Every date field in the web app was therefore **silently dead**: tapping the row flipped the `show…` flag and rendered nothing (reported as "дату не получается нажать"). Fix is a platform split following the repo's existing `.web.tsx` convention (`ExpenseMapView`, `ShareImageCard`): `DatePicker.tsx` wraps the native picker, `DatePicker.web.tsx` renders a real `<input type="date">` (raw DOM is fine in a `.web.tsx` — it is only bundled for web, under React DOM) that best-effort `showPicker()`s on mount so one tap opens the browser calendar, and treats **blur as dismissal** by calling `onChange(undefined)` — which the call sites' existing Android "close on change" branch already handles, so no extra prop. `onChange(date | undefined)` intentionally mirrors the underlying lib's contract so call sites keep owning their own close logic; `iosDisplay?: 'spinner' | 'inline'` exists solely to keep `goals/new`/`trip/new` iOS-identical. **All 8 pickers across 6 screens** now route through it (`expense/new` ×2, `income/new` ×2, `ExpenseDetailsCard`, `income/[id]`, `goals/new`, `trip/new`) and **no screen may import `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` directly again** — that import existing outside `DatePicker.tsx` is the bug. Several call sites closed on `Platform.OS === 'android'`, which left the web input stuck open; those are now `=== 'ios'` / `!== 'ios'`. Date↔`YYYY-MM-DD` conversion lives in pure, unit-tested `src/utils/dateInput.ts` (`toDateInputValue`/`fromDateInputValue`) and **must never go through `toISOString()`/`new Date(string)`**: those route via UTC and shift the calendar day for any non-zero offset (in UTC+2 a date picked at local midnight becomes the *previous* day). `fromDateInputValue` returns `null` for empty/partial/impossible input (e.g. `2026-02-31`, which JS would roll into March) so a half-typed value can't put an `Invalid Date` into a store. The same UTC bug was found and fixed in `goals/new.tsx`, which sent its `deadline` as `deadlineDate.toISOString().split('T')[0]`.
- **Transaction date is a create-form field (ABA-380)**: `expense/new.tsx` and `income/new.tsx` both hardcoded `date: new Date()` in their `addExpense`/`addIncome` payload with no date control anywhere on the form, so logging a transaction for a past day meant saving it today and then editing the date on the detail screen. Both now carry a `date` state defaulting to `new Date()` plus a `DateTimePicker` field (label `expenseNew.date`/`incomeNew.date`, placed after Description/Merchant, `display: spinner` on iOS mirroring every other picker in these files) — `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` was **already imported in both files** for the debt due-date picker, so no new dependency. Deliberately **not** clamped with `maximumDate`: `ExpenseDetailsCard`'s edit-mode picker accepts any date, and a create form that refuses what the edit form allows would be the inconsistency. The receipt/voice/bank-notification capture paths set their own dates and are untouched.
- **Report periods (ABA-409)**: the reports screen's range selection resolves through pure, unit-tested `src/features/reports/reportDateRange.ts` (`resolveReportDateRange`, `buildRecentMonthAnchors`, `formatMonthLabel`, `formatDigestPeriod`) — `app/reports.tsx` holds only the chips, the month picker and the two `DatePicker` fields. Every boundary is formatted with `toDateInputValue` and **never** `toISOString()`: the screen used to build a local-midnight `Date` and ISO-format it, so on any positive UTC offset the start date fell a day early — "this month" began on the last day of the previous month, "this year" on 31 December of the year before, and the end date resolves to *yesterday* on a negative offset, silently dropping today's rows. Same rule, same reason as `src/utils/dateInput.ts` (ABA-381). **`quarter` is the closed PREVIOUS calendar quarter** (Q2 asked from Q3; previous-year Q4 asked from Q1) — it used to be `new Date(y, m-3, 1)`, i.e. ~3.5 months, under a label that says "Ostatni kwartał" in all 9 locales; being closed also means its figures stop moving day to day. Two further modes: `specificMonth` (24-month picker, 1st through the real last day, leap February included) and `custom` (arbitrary pair). `resolveReportDateRange` returns **`null`** for a half-picked or backwards custom range and the screen disables Generate rather than sending it. The resolved `startDate — endDate` is printed under the chips so a preset label can never silently disagree with the generated file's own header. The monthly-digest card is **always the current month** regardless of the selection — it sits directly under the Generate button, so it carries an explicit caption saying so (`reports.monthlyDigestIndependent`). `POST /reports/generate` was unchanged: it always accepted arbitrary dates. Still not exposed by the screen: the DTO's `categoryIds`/`tagIds`/`projectIds`/`currencyCode` filters. The PDF is localized in 8 languages but **Excel sheet names and column headers are hardcoded English**. **Tiering (ABA-410): report generation in ALL THREE formats, plus backup export/restore, is FREE and deliberately stays free** — `ReportsController` has no `@RequireTier`, `ReportsService.generateReport` checks only `encryptionTier`, and neither `reportStore` nor the screen gates a format (the orphaned `tierBadge`/`tierBadgeText` styles in `app/reports.tsx` are dead leftovers of an abandoned gate — do not wire them up). The only paid part is the **scheduled e-mail delivery** in `ReportSchedulerService`: weekly summary = `business`, monthly digest e-mail = `pro`+. The in-app digest card is free. `user_docs` claimed PDF/Excel were Pro in all 9 locales and separately listed the weekly e-mail as free — both corrected; keep marketing copy on "reports are free, only the e-mail schedule is paid". **Analytics hand-off (ABA-411)**: the Analytics tab's "Export report" button passes `{range, month, year}` (the pattern `/story` and `/wrapped` in that same file already used — reports had been pushed with NO params, so paging back to June and exporting produced August), and `reportSelectionFromAnalytics` maps it to a selection: a **still-running** period → the preset that ends today (Analytics charts to `getEndOfMonth`, but a report generated on the 17th must not print "1–31 August"; no data exists past today so the file is identical), a **finished** period → its exact full span. `week` maps to a `custom` range from `getStartOfWeek(now)`, NOT the `week` preset — Analytics means the Monday-based calendar week, the preset means trailing 7 days, and on a Monday those differ by a week. Unparseable/absent params → `null` → the screen keeps its own default, so entering from Settings is unchanged. A seeded month older than the 24-month picker window is appended to the strip and scrolled into view on first layout. **Not carried over**: Analytics' `selectedCurrency` and drill-down state. **File export is a platform split — never call `expo-file-system` from a store again (ABA-412)**: its new `File`/`Directory`/`Paths` API has **no web implementation** (`ExpoFileSystem.web.ts` defines `FileSystemFile` as a class whose constructor only `console.warn`s, with **no methods**, while `FileSystem.ts:84`'s `File` constructor calls `this.validatePath()`), so `new File(Paths.cache, name)` throws `TypeError: this.validatePath is not a function` on web — same class of gap as ABA-381's date pickers. `reportStore` hit it from THREE places (`shareReport`/`downloadReport`/`exportBackup`), so **backup creation on web was broken by the same line**. All three now delegate to `src/services/fileExport.{ts,native.ts,web.ts}` (+ platform-free `fileExport.utils.ts`), the `secureStorage` three-file convention: `saveFile` = Android SAF folder picker → path, iOS/fallback → share sheet, web → real `<a download>` browser download; `shareFile` = share sheet / download. `handleGenerate` calls **`downloadReport`, not `shareReport`** — Generate must export, not open "Share with…"; sharing stays on the report's own row. A **cancelled** picker is now distinguished from a failure (`isPickerCancelled`, matching Expo's `ERR_PICKER_CANCELLED` code OR a `/cancel/i` message) and returns `{status:'cancelled'}` so the UI stays silent, while a real write failure still falls through to the share sheet. **Report currency + PDF row layout (ABA-413)**: `generateReport(accountId, userId, dto, baseCurrency = 'USD')` takes the caller's display currency from the controller (`req.user.currencyCode`) — the ABA-386/387 convention, **never** inferred from a row and no longer `account.currencyCode` — and every amount passes through pure `modules/reports/report-currency.util.ts` (`needsConversion`/`convertRowsToBase`/`buildCategoryTotals`, importing the shared `common/utils/fx.ts`) **before anything is summed**: the old `reduce((s,e) => s + Number(e.amount), 0)` added a `EUR 12.00` charge to a PLN report as 12 zloty, and the category table + percentages + the Excel Summary sheet inherited the blend. Rates are fetched only when the rows actually mix currencies; an unknown rate **excludes** the amount and sets `fxApproximate` (logged); **transaction rows keep their own native currency** (the list is a ledger), so PDF/Excel carry an explicit note — `fxNote` in the PDF's own 8-locale label table, English in the Excel Summary sheet. Separately, the PDF transaction table advanced by a **fixed** `ty += 14` while the description cell wraps at `width: 170`, so a long merchant name printed its second line ON TOP of the next row; the vertical arithmetic now lives in pure `generators/pdf-row-layout.util.ts` (`planTransactionRows` — measured row heights, height-aware page breaks, no infinite loop on a row taller than a page, `NaN` measurement degrades to one line) and the generator only measures + honours the plan. **Still open**: column headers are NOT repeated after a page break; the PDF label table has **no `nl`** (Dutch falls back to English); Excel sheet/column names stay English. A render-and-reparse spec is deliberately absent — `pdf-parse` v2 needs `--experimental-vm-modules` under Jest, so the pure util carries the coverage.
- **Expense currency is editable (ABA-379)**: `ExpenseDetailsCard`'s edit-mode amount row is `[amount input][currency chip]`, the chip expanding the same `SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES` list picker as `expense/new.tsx`; `editCurrencyCode` is reset by the existing `!isEditing` effect and sent as `currencyCode` in `triggerSave`'s `updateExpense()` call. Changing the currency **relabels** the expense — it never converts the amount (same rule the merge screen states as "the survivor keeps its own currency and amount"). Everything downstream already supported the field before this change and needed no edit: `updateExpenseInDb` writes `currency_code`, `UpdateExpenseDto.currencyCode` exists, `ExpensesService.update` persists it. `currencyCode` is **not** in `ENCRYPTION_FIELDS.expense` (either tier) so it stays plaintext — no re-encrypt on the E2EE push path; `expenseTotalsByCurrency`/`totalThisMonth` re-bucket for free via the `useExpenseStore.subscribe` on `expenses`; splits/items/trip-shares carry no currency of their own and inherit the relabel. **`app/income/[id].tsx` has the same chip (tech-debt `income-currency-not-editable`, closed)** — same `[amount input][currency chip]` row wired into `updateIncome()`'s `currencyCode` field, same relabel-not-convert rule; `incomeDetail.currency` i18n key added in all 9 locales.
- **Bulk expense operations** (ABA-173): `PATCH /expenses/bulk` — accepts `{ ids: string[], categoryId?, tagIds?, isDeleted? }`, scoped to `accountId`, guarded by `ViewerBlockGuard`. Validates ownership (fetches only IDs matching `accountId + isDeleted:false`), runs a single `$transaction` (updateMany + per-expense `expenseTag.upsert` loop for tag append), then invalidates chat cache. `BulkUpdateExpensesDto` with 1–500 ID limit. Mobile: long-press any expense row opens the `TransactionActionSheet` (Edit / Duplicate / Delete + **Select multiple** for expenses, ABA-168 — long-press previously jumped straight to multi-select and hid Duplicate/Edit). **Select multiple** enters multi-select mode — checkboxes appear, header switches to count + Cancel + Select All, FAB hidden. Bottom action bar: Set Category (picker sheet), Add Tag (tag multi-picker sheet via `src/components/BulkTagPickerSheet.tsx` — append mode), Delete (confirmation alert). Multi-select state machine lives in `src/hooks/useExpenseMultiSelect.ts`. `expenseStore.bulkUpdateExpenses(ids, patch)` — optimistic in-memory update → SQLite persist (incl. tag links to local `expense_tags`, ABA-167) → fire-and-forget server call (failures log `console.warn` and stay `pending`). 13 new `expenses.bulk*` i18n keys in all 8 locales. **Two correctness fixes (ABA-166/167)**: (1) **route ordering** — `@Patch('bulk')` MUST be declared *before* `@Patch(':id')` in `expenses.controller.ts` (Express matches in declaration order; a `:id` route placed first captures `/expenses/bulk` as `id="bulk"` → 400 → bulk silently no-ops and rows reappear after the next sync pull); (2) **clientId resolution** — the mobile uses local clientIds as its row ids, so `bulkUpdate` resolves both expense ids and tag ids via `OR:[{id},{clientId}]` and writes the resolved server PKs to the junction — matching on `id` only silently no-ops every bulk op for synced (device-created) rows. Tests: `expenses.controller.spec.ts` (routing) + `expense-bulk.service.spec.ts` (clientId resolution) — logic lives in `ExpenseBulkService` (ABA-368, see **Expenses service split** below).
- **Move expense between accounts**: `POST /expenses/:id/move { targetAccountId }` (`ViewerBlockGuard` + `TripArchivedGuard` on the SOURCE via `AccountContextGuard`; `ExpenseCrossAccountService.moveToAccount(sourceAccountId, userId, id, dto)` validates the caller is a **non-viewer** member of the TARGET). Account-scoped associations do NOT travel across the boundary: the category is **remapped by (case-insensitive) name** into the target account (else cleared), while tags/project links/category splits are soft-deleted and trip expense shares deleted; amount/currency/description/merchant/notes/date/items/receipt stay. **E2EE expenses are rejected** (`BadRequestException`) — their `encryptedPayload` is sealed with the source account's key and can't decrypt under the target. On a `clientId` collision in the target (`@@unique([accountId, clientId])`) the moved row's `clientId` is reassigned to a fresh `randomUUID()` — **NOT** nulled: `Expense.clientId` is a required (non-nullable) column, so `clientId: null` throws `PrismaClientValidationError` (ABA-351; the mocked-Prisma unit test hid it). The moving device self-heals — the target-account pull soft-deletes the stale re-homed local row (old clientId no longer returned) and creates the new one. Invalidates chat cache for BOTH accounts + dismisses stale source anomaly alerts. **No migration** (reassigns the existing `accountId` column). Mobile: **online** action (server-authoritative — category remap + membership checks live server-side) via `expenseStore.moveExpense(id, targetAccountId)` — optimistic remove-from-list, `api.moveExpense`, then `moveExpenseAccountInDb` re-homes the local SQLite row (`account_id`→target, `category_id`→NULL, `sync_status:'synced'` — no push queued) so it reappears under the target account on next pull; the optimistic removal is rolled back if the server call fails. Expense-detail screen (`app/expense/[id].tsx`) shows a **swap-horizontal** action + a bottom-sheet account picker (targets = non-viewer accounts ≠ current); hidden when there are no eligible targets or for viewers. `MoveExpenseDto`/`MoveExpenseResponse` in `packages/shared-types/src/dto/expense.ts`. 5 `expenseDetail.move*` i18n keys × 9 locales. Tests: `expense-cross-account.service.spec.ts` (`moveToAccount` — remap/clear category, clientId clash, same-account/encrypted/viewer/non-member/not-found rejections; also covers `mergeExpenses`, see **Expenses service split** below).
- **Expenses service split** (ABA-368): `expenses.service.ts` had grown to 1,164 lines mixing CRUD, bulk ops, cross-account move, and merge. `ExpensesService` now only owns `create`/`update`/`remove`/`findOne`/`findAll`/items/receipt-image/splits and the create-time fire-and-forget hook chain (gamification, anomaly, family-feed, community-prices, shield tracking) — 832 lines. `bulkUpdate` moved to `ExpenseBulkService` (`expense-bulk.service.ts`); `mergeExpenses` + `moveToAccount` moved to `ExpenseCrossAccountService` (`expense-cross-account.service.ts`); `ExpensesController` injects both new services directly for the corresponding endpoints — no route/guard/DTO changes.
