Logic duplicated across the three classes was pulled into standalone utils: `invalidateExpenseChatCache` (`expense-cache.util.ts`) and `resolveExpenseCategoryId` (`expense-category-resolver.util.ts`); `ExpensesService`'s private `invalidateChatCache`/`resolveCategoryId` are now thin wrappers over these. Mirrors the "AI service split" (chat.service.ts) and `admin.service.ts` splits — do NOT let `ExpensesService` re-absorb bulk/cross-account logic; extend `ExpenseBulkService`/`ExpenseCrossAccountService` instead. Tests split 1:1 with the new services (`expenses.service.spec.ts`, `expense-bulk.service.spec.ts`, `expense-cross-account.service.spec.ts`). **Follow-up (regrowth fix)**: the file regrew to 1,056 lines as every new "what happens after create" feature kept adding another `@Optional()` sibling injection and inline fire-and-forget to `create()` (see `docs/tech-debt/expenses-service-regrowth-after-split.md`); that whole hook chain (plus `reconcileNotificationStub`) is now extracted into `ExpenseCreatedHooksService` (`expense-created-hooks.service.ts`, `onExpenseCreated(accountId, userId, expense, learnableItems)`), bringing `expenses.service.ts` to 952 lines — a new such feature should add a branch there, not a new constructor param on `ExpensesService`.
- **Tag clientId reconciliation** (ABA-167): tags created on a device got a server-generated `id` the client never learned (no mapping), so server-side tag links — single (`tags.service` `addToExpense/Income`) and bulk — matched nothing (`expense_tags` was empty across prod). Fix: `Tag.clientId String?` (migration `20260530000000_add_tag_client_id`, `@@unique([accountId, clientId])`); `tags.service.create` is **idempotent on `(accountId, name)`** and stores `clientId`; `findOne` + `add/removeTo{Expense,Income}` resolve the tag AND the expense/income by `OR:[{id},{clientId}]` and write the resolved server PKs to the junction. Mobile: `tagStore.createTag` sends `clientId = local id`; `syncFromServer` upserts under `clientId ?? id` and drops legacy `pending` duplicates by name; `(tabs)/expenses.tsx` calls `loadTags()` on mount (the bulk-tag picker was empty otherwise, ABA-167). **Tests + a second instance of the same mistake (ABA-419)**: `tags.service.spec.ts` covers the id-or-clientId resolution, the idempotent create, and the usage-count bookkeeping in all four link/unlink methods. Writing it caught `update()`/`remove()` making the identical error the ABA-167 fix exists to prevent — they called `findOne(accountId, id)` to resolve the tag but then used the raw (possibly-clientId) `id` param, not the resolved `existing.id`, in the Prisma `where: { id }` unique lookup; a mobile client editing/deleting a tag by its local clientId before its first sync round-trip would hit the wrong key. Fixed to use `existing.id` in both. **When touching any `resolve*Pk`/`findOne`-by-clientId helper anywhere in the codebase, always use the resolved server PK in the following write, never the raw incoming param** — the sibling tech-debt item `clientid-resolution-gap-investments-projects-debts-budgets` tracks the same bug class in other modules.
- **Merchant analytics** (ABA-174): `useAnalytics` returns `merchantSpending: MerchantSpending[]` — computed client-side from `filteredExpenses`, groups by `merchant` (null/empty excluded), sorts descending, top 8 entries + "Other" remainder; percentage relative to total merchant-tagged spending. Rendered by `MerchantBreakdown` component (`src/components/analytics/MerchantBreakdown.tsx`) — same `InteractiveDonutChart` + row-list pattern as `TagBreakdown`; shown in Analytics tab between CategoryBreakdown and TagBreakdown when `merchantSpending.length > 0`. i18n keys: `analytics.byMerchant`, `analytics.merchantOther` (all 8 locales).
- **Income category breakdown** (ABA-178): `useAnalytics` returns `incomeByCategory: IncomeCategorySpending[]` — computed client-side from `filteredIncomes` (same date/currency filter as `filteredExpenses`), groups by `categoryId`, sorts descending, uncategorized income labelled via `analytics.incomeCategoryOther`. Uses `INCOME_CATEGORY_COLORS` (green/teal palette, distinct from expense `CATEGORY_COLORS`). Rendered by `IncomeCategoryBreakdown` component (`src/components/analytics/IncomeCategoryBreakdown.tsx`) — same `InteractiveDonutChart` + row-list pattern, no vsAverage chip. Shown in Analytics tab between `AiInsightsSection` and `SpendingTrendChart` when `incomeByCategory.length > 0`. i18n keys: `analytics.byIncomeCategory`, `analytics.incomeCategoryOther` (all 8 locales).
- **Merchant management + capture reconciliation** (ABA-141): Settings → **Merchants** screen (`app/settings/merchants.tsx`, header registered in `app/_layout.tsx`) lists distinct merchants with counts (`expenseStore.getMerchantCounts()` → `getMerchantCounts` util) and supports **rename / merge / delete**: `expenseStore.renameMerchant(from, to | null)` (rename-to-existing merges; `null` clears) updates matching in-memory rows + one `bulkRenameMerchant` SQL `UPDATE` (account-scoped, marks rows `pending`) then fires `syncPendingExpenses()` (re-encrypts for E2EE). **Capture reconciliation**: receipt OCR (`expense/receipt.tsx`) and voice (`expense/voice.tsx`) pre-fill the merchant via `resolveExistingMerchant(input, getDistinctMerchants())` (exact case-insensitive/trim match snaps to the existing canonical value) and render the editable `MerchantInput` (suggest+confirm; no fuzzy auto-map). **Voice now stores its merchant in `merchant`, not `notes`** (legacy voice rows keep it in `notes`, not retro-migrated). All three reconciliation/management helpers live in `src/utils/merchant.ts`. **Multi-merge + grouping suggestions** (ABA-254): the Merchants screen has a **selection mode** (checkboxes + count header + bottom Merge bar) and a **merge modal** (editable canonical target + moved-expense count) backed by `expenseStore.mergeMerchants(sources, target)` → `bulkMergeMerchants(accountId, sources, target)` (one account-scoped SQL `UPDATE`, skips rows already named target, marks `pending`, re-encrypts on sync) — mirrors `renameMerchant`. Above the list, **suggest-only** banners from the pure heuristic `suggestMerchantGroups()` / `merchantFingerprint()` (first alphabetic token ≥4 chars that is NOT in `FINGERPRINT_STOPWORDS` — generic venue/category words like Restauracja/Apteka/Sklep, legal forms, PL cities — → brand key; groups variants sharing a fingerprint, ≥2 members; canonical = title-cased fingerprint) propose merges the user confirms (Merge / Dismiss). Visible banners are capped at the **top 3** by spend. **Dismissals persist across sessions** via `merchantSuggestionStore` (MMKV id `merchant-suggestions`, keyed by fingerprint; pure `resolveDismissed` helper unit-tested in `stores/__tests__/`). The heuristic **never auto-merges** — only the API dictionary normalizes automatically. All write affordances `canEdit`-gated; `merchants.*` i18n adds 11 keys (`select`/`selected`/`merge`/`mergeTitle`/`mergeInto`/`mergeCount`/`merged`/`selectToMerge`/`suggestionTitle`/`suggestionMerge`/`dismiss`) across 9 locales.
- **Local-first tab hydration**: list-bearing tabs (`(tabs)/index`, `expenses`, `analytics`) call `hydrateTransactions()` (in `src/stores/hydrateTransactions.ts`) from `useEffect([currentAccountId])`. It runs `loadExpenses` then `loadIncomes` **sequentially** (parallel contends on the single SQLite connection — reads went 65ms→65-602ms spiky) and exposes `useHydrationStore.isHydrating`. Its re-entry guard collapses the ~5 parallel call sites to one cycle. Pull-to-refresh and `settings/data.tsx` "Sync now" pass `{ force: true }`. `loadExpenses`/`loadIncomes` each also have a re-entry guard + 30s per-account skip window (`force` bypasses). Server-pull merge is 4 phases: A) dedup unique categories/projects, B) decrypt rows in parallel (`Promise.all`), C) build entities in JS, D) one `withTransaction` for ALL writes. The post-merge `loadCategories+loadProjects` cascade is **awaited**. `categoryStore.loadCategories` caches seeded accounts in a module-level `Set<accountId>` (first call seeds+patches ~60ms, later ~5ms re-read). Stores read SQLite first, set `isLoading: false` immediately, then server-pull — tab paints from cache instantly. Receipt-image fetch in `expense/[id]` deferred via `InteractionManager`. Empty + `isLoading` → spinner; empty + `!isLoading` → "Add your first…" empty state.
- **Viewer role — mobile UI**: `useAccountStore(s => s.canEdit())` returns `false` for `viewer` role. In all reference-data screens (categories, merchants, tags, projects) and any write-action UI: hide `+` / pencil / trash buttons by wrapping them in `{canEdit && ...}`; pass `onPress={canEdit ? handler : undefined}` and `activeOpacity={canEdit ? 0.7 : 1}` on touchable row content so the row gives no press feedback. API already blocks writes server-side — the mobile guard is UI-only.
- **Reference data screens** (ABA-158/159) — `settings/categories.tsx`, `settings/merchants.tsx`, `tags/manage.tsx`, `projects/index.tsx` all follow the same pattern: section header with title + optional `+` icon (inside ScrollView, same as categories), single `card` with rows and `divider` lines, row content is a `TouchableOpacity` (tap to edit → bottomsheet modal), trash icon on the right. `projects/[id].tsx` shows project expenses from `expenseStore` + edit/delete in Stack header. Modal style: backdrop + bottom-sheet with handle bar, Cancel + Save buttons. Merchants cannot be manually created (derived from expenses) so no `+` button.
- **Widget order** (ABA-189): `widgetVisibilityStore` exposes `order: WidgetKey[]` (MMKV key `widget-order`, defaults to `WIDGET_KEYS`), `reorder(newOrder)`, `resetOrder()`. Validation on load: unknown keys dropped; a widget **missing** from the persisted order (a newly-added `WidgetKey`) is inserted at its intended position, not appended to the end — see the shared-factory note (ABA-456) above; `reorder()` itself still appends any missing key, since a live drag session can only omit a key the user just removed. `settings/widgets.tsx` shows all 12 widgets in stored order with drag handles (PanResponder + `LayoutAnimation`); `scrollEnabled` disabled during drag; hidden widgets are grayed-out (opacity 0.45) but still reorderable; "Reset to default order" button at bottom. Home screen `(tabs)/index.tsx` renders widgets via `widgetOrder.map(key => renderHomeWidget(key, ctx))` (see **Home screen decomposition** below) — do NOT revert to sequential if-blocks. Investment portfolio card is above the ordered widget list and is not part of the order system. `'safeToSpend'` is a `WidgetKey` (ABA-293) — `SafeToSpendWidget` shows today's safe-to-spend number; tap opens breakdown bottom-sheet.
- **Configurable quick actions** (ABA-207): the home-screen quick-action strip is data-driven from `quickActionStore` (`src/stores/quickActionStore.ts`) — MMKV `id: 'quick-actions'`, same shape as `widgetVisibilityStore` (`visibility` + `order` + `setVisible`/`reorder`/`resetOrder`/`toggle`) but with **per-key default visibility** via `DEFAULT_VISIBILITY`: `voice_income` and `scan_invoice` default **off**, the other 7 (`add_expense`, `scan_receipt`, `voice_expense`, `exchange`, `converter`, `transfers`, `subscriptions`) on. The 9 `QUICK_ACTION_KEYS` define the default order. Visibility/order resolution lives in two pure exported helpers — `resolveVisibility(read)`, `resolveOrder(raw)`, re-exported from the shared `orderedVisibilityStore.ts` factory (ABA-456) — wired to the real MMKV getter in the store, so defaults are unit-tested without mocking MMKV (`__tests__/quickActionStore.test.ts`; the factory's own append/positional-insert modes are covered generically in `__tests__/orderedVisibilityStore.test.ts`). Per-key visibility is namespaced with a `vis:` prefix (`mmkv.set(\`vis:${key}\`, ...)`); order under `quick-action-order`. `HomeQuickActionStrip` renders `order.filter(k => visibility[k]).map(...)` — static route/label maps are module-scoped there; the icon renderer (`renderQuickActionIcon`) is keyed by action and closes over `theme`/`styles`. The strip wrapper AND the hero header's bottom padding (`heroHeaderNoStrip`) collapse when nothing is visible (`showQuickActions = canEdit && visibleQuickActions.length > 0`) so there's no empty bar; still `canEdit`-gated (viewer role unaffected). The drag-reorder + toggle UI is the shared generic `ReorderableToggleList` component (see below). **Shopping hub (ABA-332)**: the near-identical `shopping` + `purchase_request` cart actions were merged into one `shopping_hub` key (default visible) that, instead of navigating, opens a bottom-sheet menu with two rows — "Shopping list" (`/shopping-list`) and "Purchase request" (`/purchase-requests`). `HomeQuickActionStrip` owns the `Modal` sheet; removing the two old keys is handled gracefully by `resolveOrder` (drops unknown, appends the new). New i18n `dashboard.shoppingListFull` (menu-row label) in all 9 locales.
- **ReorderableToggleList** (ABA-207): `src/components/ReorderableToggleList.tsx` — generic (`<K extends string>`) presentational drag-reorder + visibility-toggle list extracted from the former inline PanResponder logic in `settings/widgets.tsx`. Props: `keys`/`order`/`visibility`/`labels`/`onReorder`/`onToggle`/`onDraggingChange`. Owns the local-order drag state machine (PanResponder + Animated `translateY` + per-key responder cache); reports drag start/stop via `onDraggingChange`. **Invariant**: `order` must be a referentially-stable array (read straight from the Zustand store, never a fresh `.slice()`/literal each render) or the during-render sync loops. `settings/widgets.tsx` is now a **two-section** screen — "Quick actions" (quickActionStore) + "Widgets" (widgetVisibilityStore) — each a `ReorderableToggleList` with its own reset button; the ScrollView `scrollEnabled` is false while EITHER list drags. Section headers `settings.quickActionsTitle`/`settings.widgetsTitle`; quick-action row labels reuse `dashboard.*` keys.
- **Home screen decomposition** (ABA-304): `(tabs)/index.tsx` was a 1,145-line god file (most-churned file in the repo) mixing ~15 store subscriptions, the widget switch, the quick-action strip, the hero header, and the Safe-to-Spend sheet. Split into a thin 169-line composition root plus: `src/hooks/useHomeScreenData.ts` (all Zustand store subscriptions, derived totals, the account-change + focus-effect data loading, `onRefresh`); `src/components/home/HomeHeroHeader.tsx` (orange hero — account/currency pills, alerts bell, settings, safe-to-spend hero row); `src/components/home/HomeQuickActionStrip.tsx` (quick-action grid, route/label maps, icon renderer — moved out of the screen); `src/components/home/SafeToSpendSheet.tsx` (breakdown bottom sheet); `src/components/home/HomeWidgetSwitch.tsx` (`renderHomeWidget(key, ctx)` — the thin dispatcher over all 13 `WidgetKey` cases). `DashboardScreen` now just calls the hook and composes these five pieces + the existing `NetProfitWidget`/`NetCapitalWidget`/`CalendarWidget`/`FinancialHealthWidget`/`FamilyFeedWidget`/`FatFinderCard`/`GoalsCard`. **Widget switch regrowth fix**: `HomeWidgetSwitch.tsx` itself regrew to 704 lines as each `WidgetKey` case's card component was written inline in it; the six that had their own layout — `InvestmentCard`, `GamificationCard`, `MonthlyBudgetCard`, `IncomeExpensesCard`, `DebtsCard`, `WalletsSection` — now each live in their own file under `src/components/home/widgets/`, each owning its own `createStyles(theme)`; the shared prop type moved to `src/components/home/HomeWidgetContext.ts` (imported by every widget file plus the switch, which still re-exports it for `(tabs)/index.tsx`). `HomeWidgetSwitch.tsx` is back to being only the `renderHomeWidget` switch + the `InvestmentCard` re-export (`(tabs)/index.tsx` renders `InvestmentCard` directly, above the ordered widget list). Any new home widget should get its own file under `home/widgets/` and a case in `renderHomeWidget` — do NOT write a new card's JSX/styles inline in `HomeWidgetSwitch.tsx`, and do NOT add cases back into `(tabs)/index.tsx`. Same rule applies to `HomeQuickActionStrip.tsx` for quick actions.
- **Account detail screen decomposition** (ABA-414): `app/account/[id].tsx` was a 796-line screen mixing account info edit/delete, member role management, pending invitations, trip-lifecycle actions, and the financial-month anchor-day sheet. Split into a 430-line thin composition plus three components in `src/components/account/`: `FinancialMonthSheet.tsx` (the anchor-day `Modal` + `ANCHOR_DAYS`, mirrors `home/SafeToSpendSheet.tsx`'s bottom-sheet pattern), `MembersSection.tsx` (member list with role-change/remove + pending invitations + invite button), and `TripSection.tsx` — which exports **two** components, `TripActionsCard` (settle-up/payment-settings/trip-map nav rows) and `TripArchiveButton` (owner-only archive action with the unconfirmed-settle-up force-retry alert), kept in one module because they share the archive-trip lifecycle logic but render in non-adjacent JSX slots (`TripActionsCard` in its own section, `TripArchiveButton` inside the Danger Zone alongside delete/leave). `AccountDetailScreen` now only owns account info edit/delete/leave and passes handlers down as props — mirrors the `(tabs)/index.tsx` decomposition above. Any new trip-lifecycle or member-management feature should extend `TripSection.tsx`/`MembersSection.tsx`, not `[id].tsx` directly.
- **Debts screen decomposition** (ABA-424): `app/debts/index.tsx` was a 722-line screen mixing tab/filter state with five inline render functions (`renderSummaryCards`/`renderSegmentedTabs`/`renderFilterChips`/`renderDebtItem`/list header/empty state — all closures recreated every render) and a full-screen add-debt `Modal`. Split into a 168-line thin composition plus `src/features/debts/debtDisplay.ts` (pure `getStatusColor`/`getStatusBackgroundColor`/`formatDueDate`, theme passed as a parameter so they're unit-testable independent of the screen) and components in `src/components/debts/`: `DebtSummaryCards`, `DebtSegmentedTabs`, `DebtFilterChips`, `DebtListItem`, `AddDebtSheet` (mirrors `home/SafeToSpendSheet.tsx`'s bottom-sheet pattern), `DebtsEmptyState`, `DebtsBottomNav` (the screen's own bottom tab bar). `DebtsScreen` now only owns tab/filter/refresh/add-sheet state and the `FlatList`. Any new debt-list feature should extend one of these components, not `debts/index.tsx` directly.
- **Fat Finder screen decomposition** (ABA-439): `app/fat-finder.tsx` was a 777-line screen with four inline render functions (`renderFinding`/`renderMonthPicker`/`renderHeader`/`renderFooter`, all closures recreated every render) plus the `TYPE_ICONS`/`SEVERITY_LABELS` lookup tables in the same file. Split into `src/features/fat-finder/fatFinderDisplay.ts` (pure, theme-parameterized `TYPE_ICONS`/`SEVERITY_LABELS`/`getSeverityColor`/`getSeverityBgColor`/`formatFatFinderDate`/`getMonthLabel`, mirrors `src/features/debts/debtDisplay.ts`) and components in `src/components/fat-finder/`: `MonthPicker` (nav row + `AiUsageBadge`, reused by the loading/error/empty states and by `FatFinderHeader`), `FatFinderHeader` (summary card + findings count), `FatFinderFooter` (generatedAt + regenerate button), `FindingCard` (the finding-card layout — expand/collapse moved from two id-keyed maps on the screen to local `useState` per card, safe because `FlatList`'s `keyExtractor` already keys each card by `finding.id`). `FatFinderScreen` is now a 262-line thin composition owning only data loading, month navigation, and the AI-cost-confirmation flow. Any new finding type or UI tweak should extend one of these components, not grow `fat-finder.tsx` inline again.
- **Receipt scan screen decomposition** (ABA-448): `app/expense/receipt.tsx` was an 868-line screen mixing the pre-scan capture UI, the post-scan confirm card, the whole category-split state machine (server-split seeding, manual reassignment, proposed-category bookkeeping), and the two save paths (create expense / hand off to the manual form) in one component body. Split into two hooks — `src/hooks/useReceiptCategorySplit.ts` (the `itemCategories`/`splitDropped`/`serverSplits`/`hasEditedCategories`/`showSplitSheet` state, the server-split seeding effect, and the `currentSplits`/`proposedNamesInPlay`/`proposedNamesToCreate`/`sheetItems` derivations — mirrors `src/features/debts/debtDisplay.ts`'s "pure logic, screen owns the JSX" split) and `src/hooks/useReceiptSave.ts` (`handleConfirmExpense`/`handleEditExpense`, plus the GPS-capture ref/effect that only that save path consumes) — and two presentational components in `src/components/receipt/`: `ReceiptCaptureView.tsx` (instructions, free-text prompt, processing spinner, camera/gallery/PDF buttons) and `ReceiptConfirmCard.tsx` (the receipt preview, editable fields, `CategorySplitChips`/`PriceFindingsCard`, line items, confidence row, and the save-image/edit/confirm/retry actions), each with its own `createStyles`. The `compressAndEncodeImage` helper moved to `src/features/receipt/receiptImage.ts`. `ReceiptExpenseScreen` is now a 190-line thin composition owning only the simple screen-local UI state (`showConfirm`/`saveImage`/`userPrompt`/`merchant`) and the error-alert/scan-success effects; `ItemCategorySheet`'s modal stays screen-level since it renders as an overlay regardless of where in the tree it is mounted. Any new receipt-scan feature should extend one of these hooks/components, not grow `receipt.tsx` inline again.
- **Wallet transfer screen decomposition** (ABA-469): `app/wallet/transfer.tsx` was a 699-line screen with 11 separate `useState` hooks mixing create-mode form state, currency/amount-sync effects, the live FX-rate lookup, frequent-route chip prefill, and the per-account balance/`Max`-button logic. Split into `src/hooks/useTransferForm.ts` (all form state, the account-default/currency-sync effects with the `prefillFromRef`/`prefillToRef` guards, `fetchRate`, the amount↔rate cross-fill handlers, `applyFrequentTransfer`, `applyMaxAmount`, `handleSubmit` — pure logic, screen owns the JSX, mirrors `src/features/debts/debtDisplay.ts`'s split) and three presentational components in `src/components/wallet/`: `FrequentTransferChips.tsx` (the frequent-route chip row), `TransferAccountCard.tsx` (the account-picker + optional currency-picker + amount-input card, shared by both the "from" and "to" sides via a `footer` slot — the "from" side alone passes `TransferAvailableRow`), `TransferAvailableRow.tsx` (the available-balance text + `Max` button + insufficient-balance warning). `TransferScreen` is now a 279-line thin composition with zero local `useState`, owning only the exchange-rate/date/notes/count-as-income cards and the submit button (small enough not to warrant their own components). The edit screen `app/wallet/[id].tsx` is a separate file with its own form state and was NOT touched by this split. Any new transfer-form feature should extend `useTransferForm.ts` or one of these components, not grow `transfer.tsx` inline again.
- **Receipt image memory (ABA-463)**: Google Play enforces dynamic-memory (anonymous RSS + swap), bitmap and DEX thresholds from **February 2027** — an app over them loses Play visibility and publishing. The DEX half (>=25% optimization coverage) is already satisfied: `android/gradle.properties` sets `android.enableMinifyInReleaseBuilds=true` + `enableShrinkResourcesInReleaseBuilds=true` and `app/proguard-rules.pro` carries only two narrow `-keep` rules — **do not add a blanket `-keep class **`**, it would silently drop us under the bar. The memory half was all on the receipt path. **Rule: a receipt photo is downscaled before it is ever base64-encoded or handed to `<Image>`.** `src/features/receipt/receiptImageSizing.ts` is the pure, unit-tested home of the caps (`RECEIPT_OCR_MAX_WIDTH` 1600, `RECEIPT_STORED_MAX_WIDTH` 800) and `resolveResizeWidth`, which returns `null` to pass through rather than upscale a source already under the cap, and resizes when the width is **unknown** (the document-picker path reports none) — guessing wrong on a small image costs a modest upscale, guessing wrong on a 12 MP one costs tens of MB, so the memory-safe default wins. 1600 is chosen because the vision model downsamples to ~2048px on its longest side anyway; receipts are narrow, so capping WIDTH keeps the small print legible. `src/features/receipt/receiptImage.ts` is the only place that resizes (`downscaleForOcr` + `compressAndEncodeImage` over one private `toJpeg`) — it replaced a **third** inline copy that lived in `ReceiptSection` as `compressImageToBase64`; do not add a fourth. `toJpeg` runs the manipulation even when no resize is needed, since the pickers can return HEIC on iOS. `downscaleForOcr` **never throws** (falls back to the original URI — a scan that works but uses more memory beats a scan that does not work). `useReceiptScanner.processImage` downscales BEFORE encoding and sets `state.imageUri` to the downscaled file, so one shrunk copy feeds the OCR request, the preview, and the stored 800px copy; both pickers pass `asset.width` through. `app/income/receipt.tsx` shares the hook and inherits this. **Stored receipts render from a file URI, never a `data:` URL**: `src/features/receipt/receiptImageCache.ts` `materializeReceipt`/`releaseReceipt` writes the base64 to a cache file so Fresco can sample the bitmap down to the size actually on screen (a `data:` URL made the 200pt thumbnail decode at full resolution) and so the multi-MB string does not sit in component state; the filename carries a module-level revision counter because `<Image>` caches by URI and a reused path would show the previous photo after a replace. Web keeps the data URL (`expo-file-system/next` `File` has no working web implementation — same platform branch as `utils/fileBase64.ts`), which is fine: the threshold is a Play requirement. **Still open**: the **Restore Credentials API** (zero-tap sign-in on device migration) is enforced from **April 2027** — both stages have now landed (see the dedicated `Restore credentials` bullet above) but the round trip is unverified on real hardware; before this, sign-in was a JWT in `expo-secure-store` only, so a new device meant logging in again. Our real numbers against the memory thresholds are unmeasured — read them from Play Console's Android Vitals dynamic-memory breakdown rather than inferring them.
- **Financial Health Score** (ABA-193): `src/features/analytics/useFinancialHealthScore.ts` — pure `useMemo` hook computing a 0–100 score from four equally-weighted components (0–25 pts each): budget adherence (% of active budgets not over limit), savings rate (linear: 0%→0 pts, 20%+→25 pts, excluded when no income), goal progress (% of active goals on-track by deadline, excluded when no goals), debt health (always included; full 25 pts when no debts). Score normalises over included components only. `hasEnoughData=false` when <2 components have data. Color: red (<40), yellow (40–69), green (70+). Widget `FinancialHealthWidget` (`src/components/widgets/`) shows a circular gauge (pure `View`/`StyleSheet` border trick, no native deps) + a tap-to-open explanation `Modal` bottom-sheet. `WidgetKey` `financialHealth` is one of the `WIDGET_KEYS` entries (default on; `storeArrival` now leads the list — see below). Zero server calls, zero AI cost — entirely client-side.
- **i18n**: 9 locales in `src/i18n/locales/` — `en.ts` (source), `de.ts`, `es.ts`, `fr.ts`, `pl.ts`, `ru.ts`, `ua.ts`, `be.ts`, `nl.ts` (Dutch, ABA — added via `claude/dutch-language-support`). When adding keys, update ALL 9 files. Registered in `src/i18n/index.ts` (`SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES`, `intlLocaleMap` → `nl-NL`, `resources`).
- **Help system**: In-app help screen (`app/help/index.tsx`, `app/help/[id].tsx`) is powered by **auto-generated** `src/help/content.ts`. NEVER create or edit `content.ts` manually. Workflow: (1) write/edit markdown in `user_docs/<lang>/NN-slug.md` for all 9 languages, (2) add the section id to `scripts/generate-help-content.js` SECTIONS array and to `src/help/sections.ts`, (3) run `npm run generate:help` from the project root. Do NOT create a manual `app/help.tsx` file — the route is handled by `app/help/index.tsx`. **Public web help center (ABA-284)**: the SAME `user_docs/<lang>/NN-slug.md` files ALSO feed a static, crawlable help center at `ai-budget.pl/help` via `docs/marketing/help/build_help.py` (mirrors the blog pipeline; REUSES `build_blog.py`'s chrome by `import build_blog`). 333 pages = 37 sections × 9 langs at `/help/<lang>/<slug>/` (slug = section id minus `NN-`, identical across langs so hreflang pairs by slug, x-default=en) + per-lang `/help/<lang>/` index + a `noindex` `/help/` JS dispatcher. Thin pages (<250 words) get `noindex,follow` + drop from the help sitemap. Cross-doc `./NN-slug.md` links become real `/help/<lang>/<slug>/` links (legacy `telegram-bot`/`whatsapp-bot` → `chat-bots`); `../img/X.jpg` → `/help/assets/img/`. `build_landing.py`'s apex sitemap merges `/help` URLs; `web-deploy.yml` ships `apex/help`; landing + blog footers carry a localized Help link. Regenerate after editing any `user_docs` file: `python docs/marketing/help/build_help.py` (then re-run landing with the prod env `LANDING_BASE= ROBOTS="index,follow,max-image-preview:large"` so the apex sitemap merges the new `/help` URLs — the env-less default builds a `noindex` preview that would clobber prod). **A NEW section must ALSO be added to `build_help.py`'s OWN hardcoded `SECTIONS` list** — a THIRD registration place, separate from `scripts/generate-help-content.js` and `src/help/sections.ts` (the in-app generator). Miss it and the web help silently omits the section (this is how `38-shopping-list` drifted out of sync until ABA-336 backfilled it). The web help is separate from the in-app `content.ts` — both read the same markdown. **Mobile rendering (ABA-288)**: the shared blog/help CSS in `build_blog.py` constrains content screenshots with `article img{width:100%;max-width:320px;height:auto;…}` (portrait phone shots are 591×1280 — without this they overflow the viewport); the landing (`build_landing.py`) and blog/help headers each carry a `@media(max-width:760px)` block (brand 16px `nowrap`, tighter nav gap/padding, compact button) + a `lang-full`/`lang-short` language-switcher swap (full native name → uppercase lang code at ≤480px, so a long name like "Беларуская" doesn't overflow the header) + `html,body{overflow-x:hidden}`. CSS is inlined per page, so editing it re-touches every committed `site/` HTML on regenerate. **PageSpeed/a11y (ABA-366)**: Lighthouse on the apex flagged uncompressed HTML + 30-day static cache + contrast/CLS. **Prod nginx** (`/opt/ai-budget-web/default.conf`, bind-mounted into `ai-budget-web-prod`): `gzip on` for html/css/js/svg/json/xml + static-asset cache `expires 30d`→`1y` (NO `immutable` — filenames aren't content-hashed, so reload-revalidation stays possible). **Gotcha**: it's a single-*file* bind mount, so a `mv`+reload keeps the OLD inode — an in-place config change needs `docker restart ai-budget-web-prod` to take effect. Verified live: HTML 22 KB→7 KB gzip, assets `Cache-Control: max-age=31536000`; scoped to ai-budget (shared-nginx + eksiegowyai untouched). **Generators**: landing `page()` now wraps the body in `<main>` (the homepage was the one page missing it); lightbox `<img>` carry `width`/`height` via a new `shot_dims()` helper (CLS); the orange used for **text/buttons** darkened to WCAG-AA `#B45309` (landing `.btn.p`/`.brand span`/card numbers/`.pop-badge`/`.pfeat` check/`.see`/blogcta/legal links; blog+help `--o` redefined `#F58320`→`#B45309`, `article a`/`.cc .ok`→`var(--o)`) while bright `#F58320` stays for decorative borders/gradients/`:hover` + the dark-banner consent link (now underlined); muted greys darkened (`#9a9aa3`→`#6b6b73`, `#8a8a93`→`#63636b`). Deferred: WebP/AVIF for the ~48 KiB below-fold lazy lightbox images. HTML/CSS fixes deploy on push via `web-deploy.yml`; the nginx change is applied directly on the VPS (not in the repo). **Startup Fame badge (ABA-367)**: the footers of BOTH generators carry a `STARTUP_FAME_BADGE` constant (embed `<a href="https://startupfa.me/s/ai-budget?utm_source=ai-budget.pl">` → `featured-badge.webp` 171×54) in a centered `.f-badge` row above the company line — `build_landing.py` `footer_html()` (apex + all landing pages) and `build_blog.py` `foot()` (shared blog + help footer). Required for the free Startup Fame "Verified" listing (their bot fetches `ai-budget.pl` and checks for a link to `startupfa.me`). If the dedicated-page slug changes, edit the constant in both files and regenerate all three sites.
- **Services**: `api.ts` (barrel), `http-client.ts` (base HttpClient), 14 domain api files (see **API client** above), `notifications.ts`, `secureStorage.native.ts` / `secureStorage.web.ts`, `widgetData.ts`, `appVersion.ts` (raw fetch wrapper around the public `/app-versions/check`, used pre-login)
- **Screens**: `(auth)/` login/register/forgot-password/reset-password, `(tabs)/` main tabs, `expense/`, `income/`, `budget/`, `account/`, `analytics/`, `calendar/`, `projects/`, `tags/`, `wallet/` (index, set-balance, exchange/index, exchange/[id], transfer, transfers, exchanges, [id]), `debts/`, `goals/`, `settings/` (index hub, profile, appearance, ai, widgets, notifications, security, categories, `reference` (ABA-153: reference-data hub linking categories/merchants/tags/projects; navigation-only, links to existing routes), data, about, ai-usage-details, `bots` (unified Telegram + WhatsApp connection screen), `import/` (transactions-import hub: index, preview, mapper, request-bank), `auto-capture` (Android notification auto-capture opt-in — linked from import hub; Android-only, shows "Android only" note on other platforms), change-email — 2-step flow with 6-digit code, pending state persisted in `secureStorage` under key `pendingEmailChange` with 30-min TTL so the screen resumes at step 2 after app restart), `subscription.tsx`, `referral.tsx`, `admin.tsx`, `story.tsx`, `fat-finder.tsx`, `scenario-simulator.tsx`, `subscriptions/` (index, new, [id]), `expense/merge.tsx` (ABA-296: two-expense merge screen, entry via `possible_merge` alert or import-preview)
- **Wallet balance history** (ABA-257 daily, superseded by ABA-258 monthly): two endpoints. (1) Daily `GET /wallet/balance-history?days=30` (capped 90; derives daily per-currency snapshots: current balance from `getSummary()`, back-calculate `startBalance = currentBalance – rangeDelta`, walk forward) → `WalletBalanceHistoryResponse { points, currencies }`. **Kept only for already-released app versions** — the mobile client no longer calls it. (2) **Monthly `GET /wallet/balance-history/monthly?months=6`** (ABA-258, `getMonthlyBalanceHistory`, clamp 1–12) sums **per-currency net change per calendar month** from income(+)/expense(−)/exchange(±)/transfers(±, inbound `countAsIncome:false`) → `WalletMonthlyHistoryResponse { months: WalletMonthlyDeltaPoint[] /* {month:'YYYY-MM', deltas} */, currencies }`, one entry per month incl. empty months. No new table. Mobile: `walletStore.monthlyHistory`/`selectedMonths`/`loadMonthlyHistory(6|12)`; `WalletBalanceCard` (`src/components/wallet/WalletBalanceCard.tsx`, replaced `WalletSparklineCard`) converts each month's deltas to a `displayCurrency` prop via `convertAmount` (current FX rates) and renders `WalletMonthlyChart` (`src/components/wallet/WalletMonthlyChart.tsx` — gifted-charts `BarChart` with **signed** bars, negatives below axis via `mostNegativeValue`/`noOfSectionsBelowXAxis`, per-bar green≥0/red<0, value on tap, NO per-bar labels — fixes the prior per-point label overlap), plus a 6M/12M chip toggle and the latest-month delta summary. **Wallet-local currency toggle** (ABA-258): `app/wallet/index.tsx` has a `SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES` chip row driving a local `displayCurrency` state (default `user.currencyCode`) for the total card + chart via `convertAmount` — **local-only, not persisted, never touches the global `user.currencyCode`**; resets to base on re-entry. Per-currency balance cards stay native.
- **Components**: `charts/` (Bar, Donut, Pie, Weekday, GroupedBar), `interactive-charts/` (drill-down with ChartRenderer; **`InteractiveLineChart`'s SVG wrapper (`chartClip`) must stay auto-height with `overflow:hidden` — NOT a fixed height** (ABA-352): gifted-charts draws the negative below-x-axis region *beyond* the `height` prop (`svgHeight = height + noOfSectionsBelowXAxis * height/noOfSections`), so a fixed-height clip hides all negatives; `noOfSectionsBelowXAxis` is proportional 1..4 to the most-negative point), `insights/`, `wallet/` (`WalletBalanceCard` + `WalletMonthlyChart` — monthly balance-change bars with 6M/12M selector + display-currency conversion, see **Wallet balance history** above), `widgets/` (NetProfitWidget, NetCapitalWidget, CalendarWidget, FinancialHealthWidget), `story/`, `investment/` (ABA-238: `PerformanceCard`, `AllocationCard`, `GainersCard`, `BenchmarkCard`, `FormulaModal` — sub-components extracted from `app/investment/analytics.tsx`; each owns its styles), `chat/` (ActionConfirmationCard, ActionResultCard, `ChatMessageItem` — single message bubble with markdown/action cards, `ChatHistorySheet` — conversation history modal bottom-sheet), `analytics/` (per-section components, each owns its styles, consumed by `(tabs)/analytics.tsx`), `expenses/` (ABA-176: `ExpenseListItem`, `IncomeListItem`, `ExpenseFilterBar` — extracted from the expenses tab screen; `ExpenseFilterBar` manages period chips, category dropdown, merchant picker modal, and month navigator; internalises `showCategoryPicker`/`showMerchantPicker` state and resets on `activeTab` change), `AccountSwitcher` (ABA-187: also the **global currency switcher** — its trigger pill shows the base-currency symbol after the account name (`Personal · $ ▾`, symbol never truncates), and its menu always opens (even with one account) with a "Display currency" chip section (one chip per `SUPPORTED_CURRENCIES`) that calls `authStore.setCurrency`. Currency is a user preference, not account-scoped → available to all roles incl. `viewer`, no `canEdit` gate. **ABA-243** (unified header across home + all tab screens): `showCurrency` prop (default `true`) hides the inline symbol. The home hero header AND every tab header (`(tabs)/_layout.tsx` `screenOptions`) render `<AccountSwitcher showCurrency={false} />` + the standalone `CurrencyPill` (same file, opens just the currency chips, also routes through `setCurrency`) so account and display-currency are **separate pills**; other consumers keep the combined pill. Tab headers use a **custom `header`** (the `TabHeader` component in `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, not RN's default header — the default vertically centres the side controls, which left a long title overlapping them): a two-row layout — controls row (compact account + currency pills left; alerts bell with unread badge from `useAlertStore` + settings rounded buttons right), then a centered white divider, then the **page title BELOW the divider** (regular weight, own row — so a long name like "Транзакции" never collides with the pills). Home hero header has **no greeting** (removed) and the same style: controls row + centered white separator (`heroDivider`) above the quick-action strip. Both dividers are `width: '95%'` inside a 16px-padded container so they line up with page content. **ABA-244**: the home quick actions are a **centered wrapping grid** (fixed-width cells, `flexWrap` + `justifyContent: center`) — extra actions wrap to a new row that pushes page content down instead of hiding behind a horizontal scroll (the old `ScrollView` + `expo-linear-gradient` scroll-hint were removed). The first row overlaps up into the orange hero (`marginTop: -22`); each icon sits on a near-invisible `surface` backing so it stays readable on the overlap. Icons render via `QuickActionIcon` — brand outline SVGs from `assets/icons/*.svg` inlined as `react-native-svg` `SvgXml` strings (no svg-transformer / metro change); every symbol gets a uniform orange rounded-square frame (the 4 source SVGs that shipped with a built-in frame have it stripped + viewBox tightened), and each renders at its true aspect ratio so wide icons (e.g. transfers) stay centered; `color` recolors the green income/invoice variants), `AiUsageBadge`, `BulkTagPickerSheet` (standalone tag multi-picker sheet used from the expenses multi-select flow), `CreateCategoryModal`, `KeyboardAwareScreen` + `KeyboardAvoidingScreen` (pure-JS keyboard wrappers — see **Keyboard handling**), `HydrationProgressBar` (2.5px indeterminate slider mounted in `(tabs)/_layout.tsx`, driven by `useHydrationStore.isHydrating`), `Paywall` (ABA-265: mounted as a bottom-sheet `Modal` in `(tabs)/chat.tsx`, shown when an AI-limit **403** in `chatStore.sendMessage` flips the new `showUpgrade` flag; the catch maps 403 → `subscription.limitReachedBody` instead of the generic `errors.chatError`, and the card now shows a prominent `subscription.startTrial` badge above the plan cards — previously the component was a dead export, never mounted, so hitting the AI limit showed only a confusing error with no upgrade path), `ProjectPicker`, `SplitEditor`, `TagPicker`, `TagChip`, `TransactionActionSheet`, `UpdatePrompt` (Play/App Store update modal, mounted at root in `app/_layout.tsx`, force/soft modes, dismissal persisted per-version in `secureStorage` key `skippedUpdateVersion`; soft Update also writes the key before opening store; required prompts never dismiss locally, rely on `AppState`-active force version-check)
- **Hooks**: `src/hooks/usePortfolioAnalytics.ts` (ABA-238) — pure computation hook for investment analytics; takes `PortfolioAnalyticsResponse | null`, returns `performanceHistory`, `allocation`, `benchmarkComparison`, `topGainers`, `topLosers`, `latestValue`, `earliestValue`, `periodReturn`, `isPeriodPositive`; used by `app/investment/analytics.tsx`. `src/hooks/usePeriodNavigation.ts` — manages `selectedMonth`/`selectedYear` state with `isCurrentPeriod`, `getPeriodLabel`, `goToPrevPeriod`, `goToNextPeriod`, `resetToCurrentPeriod`; used by `(tabs)/analytics.tsx`. `src/hooks/useCalendarData.ts` — shared hook for calendar grid computation, date filtering, category breakdowns with multi-currency conversion. Used by `CalendarWidget` (home screen) and `app/calendar/index.tsx` (full-screen page). `src/hooks/useAiCostConfirmation.ts` — one-time confirmation dialog before expensive AI operations (cost >= 2.0), stores dismissal per feature in AsyncStorage. `src/hooks/useAppVersionCheck.ts` — calls public `/app-versions/check` on mount and on `AppState` `active`, 6h in-memory cache, web no-op, fail-silent (never blocks app boot), exports `__resetAppVersionCheckCache()` test seam. `src/hooks/useOrientationLock.ts` — locks portrait on phones (`Math.min(width, height) < 600dp`) and unlocks on tablets/foldables, called once from `app/_layout.tsx`. App-level `orientation` is `"default"` so the manifest doesn't restrict large screens (Android 16 ignores manifest orientation lock on large screens anyway); the JS hook restores phone-only portrait UX. Web is a no-op. `src/hooks/useExpenseMultiSelect.ts` (ABA-176) — encapsulates expenses-tab multi-select state machine (`isMultiSelect`, `selectedIds`, bulk pickers, `isBulkProcessing`) and the three bulk action handlers; called from `(tabs)/expenses.tsx`. `src/hooks/useChatPolling.ts` (ABA-198) — wraps `startPolling`/`stopPolling` + `useFocusEffect` for the chat tab; pure side-effect, no return value. `src/hooks/useMentionBar.ts` (ABA-198) — manages `pendingMentions`, `@`-detection in input text, member lookup; returns `{mentionCandidates, insertMention, getActiveMentions, resetMentions}`; used by `(tabs)/chat.tsx`. `src/hooks/useTelegramBot.ts` / `useWhatsAppBot.ts` / `useSlackBot.ts` (ABA-240) — each encapsulates one bot's state, `useEffect`/`useFocusEffect` reload, and all action handlers; types (`WhatsAppStatus`, `WhatsAppLinkCode`, `SlackStatus`, `SlackLinkCode`) live in the respective hook file; `API_ORIGIN` derivation lives in `useSlackBot` (Slack OAuth install URL only); `BotsSettingsScreen` is a thin coordinator that calls all three.
- **Features**: `src/features/analytics/useAnalytics.ts` — analytics computations hook. Returns `AnalyticsSummary` (incl. `vsAverage: number` — signed % vs trailing 3-month rolling average, computed locally from SQLite expense store). `CategorySpending` items include `vsAverage: number | null` — per-category delta vs 3-month trailing avg shown as green/red chip below the percentage in the category list (month view only; `null` = new category or non-month view, chip hidden; ±5% noise threshold, computed offline from SQLite). `src/features/scenario/useScenarioProjection.ts` — scenario simulator projection hook (pure client-side, reads expense/income stores, projects savings over 3/6/12 months)
- **Scenario persistence**: `src/stores/scenarioStore.ts` — MMKV-backed store (`scenario-storage` id). Persists `SavedScenario[]` (id, name, expenseAdj, incomeAdj, extraIncomes, horizon, createdAt). Free tier: 5 scenarios max; Pro/Business: unlimited. Actions: `saveScenario(name, snapshot, isPro) → 'ok'|'limit_reached'`, `deleteScenario(id)`, `canSave(isPro)`. `scenario-simulator.tsx` has Save modal, load bottom sheet, and native Share button (text summary via `Share.share()`). No API endpoint — device-local only.

### Admin (Next.js)
- **Pages**: Dashboard (`/`), Login (`/login`), Investor Metrics (`/metrics`), Acquisition (`/acquisition`), Users (`/users`, `/users/[id]`), AI Usage (`/ai-usage`), Subscriptions (`/subscriptions`), Communications (`/communications`), App Versions (`/app-versions`), Referrals (`/referrals`), Audit Log (`/audit-log`), Settings (`/settings`)
- **App Versions page**: per-platform tabs (Android/iOS); list of past releases with "Current" badge on the most recent; "New release" Dialog form with semver-pattern inputs and 8-locale release-notes textareas (EN required); delete-confirm via `Dialog` (codebase has no `AlertDialog` primitive). Each release row's content is a clickable button (ABA-202) opening a read-only detail `Dialog` (latest/min version, clickable store URL, full release notes per locale) — the trash button stays separate. Hooks: `src/hooks/use-app-versions.ts`.
- **Users page**: filters (search debounced 300ms, tier, status) + paginated table. Sortable column headers (ABA-202) on Name, Email, Registered (`createdAt`), Last Login (`lastSyncAt`) via a `SortableHead` component with chevron indicators — clicking toggles asc/desc, switching column resets to asc, both reset to page 1. `sortBy`/`order` flow through `useUsers` → API `admin/users` (allow-list `name`/`email`/`createdAt`/`lastSyncAt` in `admin.service.ts`). Tier/Status/AI-Requests are not sortable (relation/computed fields).
- **Tech**: Next.js 16 App Router, React Query 5 (data fetching), shadcn/ui components, Recharts (charts), Socket.io-client (real-time), ky (HTTP client)
- **API client**: `src/lib/api-client.ts` — ky instance, auto-injects Bearer token, 401 → logout. Base URL from `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` (default `http://localhost:3000/api/v1`)
- **Auth**: `src/lib/auth.ts` — login via `POST /auth/login`, tokens in localStorage (`admin_token`, `admin_refresh_token`)
- **Real-time**: `src/lib/socket.ts` — Socket.io namespace `/admin`, events: `new_user`, `ai_request`, `error`, `subscription_change`
- **Dashboard features**: KPI cards, subscription distribution pie chart, registration trends, AI cost by feature, live activity feed, top AI spenders
- **Communications page** (ABA-420): 5 tabs — Send Push, Send Email, Broadcast, Scheduled, History. `apps/admin/src/app/communications/page.tsx` is a thin `Tabs` shell (45 lines); each tab is its own component under `apps/admin/src/components/communications/` — `SendPushTab.tsx`, `SendEmailTab.tsx`, `BroadcastTab.tsx`, `ScheduledTab.tsx`, `HistoryTab.tsx` — so each tab's form/filter/pagination state is local to its own component instead of one god component tracking all five at once (the admin app's first per-page decomposition, mirroring the mobile app's repeated screen splits e.g. `(tabs)/index.tsx`/`app/account/[id].tsx`). History tab has summary stats cards, type filter (push/email/broadcast), expandable rows with recipient details (name/email), body preview, broadcast filters, delivery success bar, and paginated list with relative dates — all state (page/typeFilter/expandedId) owned by `HistoryTab.tsx` alone.
- **User detail page decomposition**: `apps/admin/src/app/users/[id]/page.tsx` (646 → 387 lines) had its five independent user actions each pulled into `apps/admin/src/components/users/` — `ChangeTierDialog.tsx`, `AiLimitCard.tsx` (an inline, non-modal control — it was never a `Dialog`, unlike the other four), `SendPushDialog.tsx`, `SendEmailDialog.tsx`, `DeactivateDeleteDialog.tsx` (bundles Deactivate + the Delete confirmation dialog, both "danger zone" actions). Each owns its own local state + mutation hook, taking `userId` (plus `userName`/`userEmail`/`isActive`/`customAiLimit` as needed) as props. `page.tsx` stays a thin composition: `useUserDetail`/`useUserNotificationHistory` + the profile/subscription/actions cards, accounts table, AI usage chart, recent expenses, notification history. Any new user-detail action should get its own component under `components/users/`, not another inline dialog in `page.tsx`.

### Shared Types
- Entities: `packages/shared-types/src/entities/index.ts` — 30+ domain interfaces
- DTOs: `packages/shared-types/src/dto/index.ts` — API request/response shapes
- API types: `packages/shared-types/src/api/index.ts` — API endpoint types
- Types use `PascalCase` interfaces, enums use string literal unions (e.g., `type AccountRole = 'owner' | 'editor' | 'viewer'`)
- Key enums: `Currency` (USD/EUR/PLN/GBP/UAH/RUB/**BYN**), `AccountRole` (owner/editor/viewer), `AccountType` (personal/business/shared/investment), `ExpenseSource` (manual/voice/ocr/import/telegram/whatsapp/slack), `BudgetPeriod` (daily/weekly/monthly/yearly/custom), `SubscriptionTier` (free/pro/business), `SyncStatus` (pending/synced/conflict/error)
- **Sync DTOs** (ABA-177): `packages/shared-types/src/dto/sync.ts` — `SyncChange` is a **discriminated union** keyed on `entityType`. Each entity type has its own payload interface (e.g. `SyncTagPayload`, `SyncProjectPayload`, `SyncPortfolioHoldingPayload`). `sync.service.ts` handlers take `Extract<SyncChange, { entityType: '...' }>` for compile-time payload validation. Do NOT add a local `SyncChange` interface in the API — always import from `@budget/shared-types`.

### Shared Utils
- Validation: `packages/shared-utils/src/validation/index.ts` — Zod schemas for auth, expenses, incomes, budgets, categories, tags, projects, sync
- Formatting: `packages/shared-utils/src/formatting/index.ts`
- Constants: `packages/shared-utils/src/constants/index.ts`

## Dependency Order for Changes

When modifying features that span multiple packages, follow this order:

1. `packages/shared-types` — entity interfaces and DTOs
2. `packages/shared-utils` — Zod validation schemas (if needed)
3. `apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma` — database schema, then `npx prisma migrate dev --name <name>` + `npx prisma generate`
4. `apps/api/src/modules/*` — API services, controllers, guards
5. `apps/mobile/src/db/schema/index.ts` — SQLite schema (independent from API DB)
6. `apps/mobile/src/db/*Repository.ts` — data access layer
7. `apps/mobile/src/stores/*` — Zustand stores
8. `apps/mobile/src/services/api.ts` — API client methods
9. `apps/mobile/app/*` — screens and UI
10. `apps/mobile/src/i18n/locales/*.ts` — translations (all 9 files)

Mobile SQLite changes (step 5-6) are independent from API Prisma changes (step 3-4) and can run in parallel.

## Commands

```bash
npm install                    # Install all dependencies
npm run dev                    # Start all dev servers (turbo)
npm run build                  # Build all packages
npm run lint                   # Lint all code
npm run typecheck              # TypeScript check all code
npm run test                   # Run tests
npm run format                 # Prettier format
npm run clean                  # Clean all builds + node_modules
npm run dev:web                # Start mobile app in browser (Expo web on http://localhost:8081)

# API specific (run from apps/api/)
npx prisma generate            # Regenerate Prisma client
npx prisma migrate dev --name X  # Create DB migration
npx prisma studio              # Visual DB editor

# Mobile specific (run from apps/mobile/)
npx expo start                 # Start Expo dev server
npx expo start --web           # Start web preview

# Admin specific (run from apps/admin/)
npm run dev                    # Start admin dashboard on port 3001

# Help content (run from project root)
npm run generate:help          # Regenerate apps/mobile/src/help/content.ts from user_docs/
```

## Environment Variables

See `.env.example`:
- `DATABASE_URL` — PostgreSQL connection string
- `REDIS_URL` — Redis connection string
- `JWT_SECRET`, `JWT_EXPIRES_IN` — JWT token config
- `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_IDS` — comma-separated Google OAuth client IDs (web,ios,android) accepted as ID-token audiences for `POST /auth/google` (ABA-282). Client-side IDs are baked into the app via `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_WEB_CLIENT_ID` / `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_IOS_CLIENT_ID` / `EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_ANDROID_CLIENT_ID`
- `OPENAI_API_KEY` — OpenAI for AI features (Whisper, GPT)
- `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, `FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY`, `FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL` — push notifications
- `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` — API URL for mobile app
- `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` — Stripe API key (apiVersion pinned to `2026-01-28.clover`, must match SDK in `package-lock.json`)
- `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` — the **user-facing assistant bot** (`MiCodeAIBudgetAssistantBot`, `TelegramBotService`): expenses, chat, voice, photo. `TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` is now legacy/unused by the API (ops moved to the ops bot below).
- **Ops bot split (ABA-319)**: `OPS_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `OPS_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` — a **separate** MiCode ops bot + channel. ALL operational Telegram alerts go here, NOT the assistant bot: `TelegramService` (the ops notifier in `modules/telegram/`, injected by auth/subscriptions/referrals/import-bank) reads these for new-registration / new-subscription / referral / request-a-bank alerts, and `uptime-check.yml`/`backup-db.yml`/`infra-watch.yml` use the same-named **GitHub Actions secrets** for downtime/backup/infra alerts. **No fallback** — if `OPS_*` is unset, ops alerts are skipped (never leak to the assistant bot). Optional `OPS_PROJECT_NAME` prefixes every message (`<b>[name]</b> ` in the API, `*[AI Budget]*` in workflows) so a shared MiCode ops channel aggregating multiple projects can tell them apart. Prereq: create the ops bot + channel, set `OPS_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`OPS_TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` in `.env.production` (force-recreate `api`) AND as GitHub secrets.
- `WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, `WHATSAPP_BUSINESS_ACCOUNT_ID`, `WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN`, `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET`, `WHATSAPP_BUSINESS_PHONE_NUMBER`, `WHATSAPP_API_VERSION` — WhatsApp Business Cloud API. Token scope `whatsapp_business_messaging` only. `WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET` is the HMAC key for inbound webhook signature verification. `WHATSAPP_BUSINESS_PHONE_NUMBER` is shown in the mobile app as a `wa.me` deep link. Webhook URL is `https://api.ai-budget.pl/whatsapp/webhook` (no `/api/v1` prefix — excluded in `main.ts`).
- `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`, `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`, `SLACK_APP_ID`, `SLACK_BOT_USER` — Slack bot. `SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET` is the HMAC key for inbound webhook signature verification (`v0=` scheme). Bot is a no-op when these are unset. `POST /slack/events` and `POST /slack/interactivity` are excluded from the `/api/v1` prefix in `main.ts`. `main.ts` also registers `express.urlencoded({ verify })` so that `rawBody` is available on the urlencoded interactivity endpoint for HMAC verification. Prod event URL: `https://api.ai-budget.pl/slack/events`.
- `SLACK_CLIENT_ID` — OAuth app Client ID (from Slack App Settings → Basic Information); required for multi-workspace install.
- `SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET` — OAuth app Client Secret (same page); used server-side in the `oauth.v2.access` code exchange; never exposed to clients.
- `SLACK_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URL` — must match exactly what is registered under OAuth & Permissions → Redirect URLs in the Slack App dashboard (prod value: `https://api.ai-budget.pl/slack/oauth/callback`).
- `SLACK_TOKEN_ENC_KEY` — 32-byte (256-bit) random hex key for AES-256-GCM encryption of `SlackInstallation.accessToken` at rest; generate with `openssl rand -hex 32`; rotation requires re-encrypting existing rows.
- `SENTRY_DSN` — optional. When set, `apps/api/src/instrument.ts` initializes `@sentry/node` (must be imported FIRST in `main.ts`, before any other module). When unset, Sentry is a no-op.

## Production

Hetzner VPS (Hetzner cloud), Docker Compose. Stack defined in `docker-compose.prod.yml`:
- `budget-db-prod` (postgres:16-alpine, 512M), `budget-redis-prod` (redis:7-alpine, 320M — `maxmemory 256mb allkeys-lru`), `budget-api-prod` (1G, `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=768`), `budget-admin-prod` (256M), plus shared `accounting-nginx` (separate stack) reverse-proxying `api.ai-budget.pl` and `admin.ai-budget.pl`. API container has **no host port mapping** — reach it only through nginx or the docker network. **API memory (ABA-163)**: without an explicit `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size`, Node auto-caps the V8 heap at ~half the container limit (~256M at 512M), which OOM-crashed the backup export. Compose sets heap to 768 and `deploy.resources.limits.memory` to 1G — keep them in sync (heap + ~150M non-heap must fit under the limit).
- Volumes: `ai-budget_postgres_data`, `ai-budget_redis_data` (preserve across deploys; never `docker volume prune` without filters).
- **DB backups (ABA-163, Phase 1)**: `.github/workflows/backup-db.yml` — nightly cron (`0 2 * * *` UTC, off-host so it survives a VPS outage) + `workflow_dispatch`. SSHes to the VPS, runs `pg_dump -Fc` inside `budget-db-prod`, streams it to the runner, validates it (size ≥10 KB + `pg_restore --list` object count), `age`-encrypts (asymmetric — runner holds only `AGE_PUBLIC_KEY`; the private key is **offline**, never in CI), and publishes it as a Release asset (`ai_budget-YYYY-MM-DD.dump.age`, tag `backup-YYYY-MM-DD`) in the private repo `BACKUP_REPO` via `BACKUP_REPO_TOKEN` (PAT, `contents:write`). Logic lives in `scripts/backup-db.sh` (dump+sanity+encrypt; mockable via `LOCAL_DUMP`) and `scripts/prune-backups.sh` (GFS retention 7 daily/4 weekly-Sunday/6 monthly-1st; mockable via `RELEASES_FILE`/`DRY_RUN`). Failure → Telegram alert (same pattern as `uptime-check.yml`). Restore: `docs/ops/restore-runbook.md` (decrypt → scratch-restore → verify → prod `pg_restore --clean --if-exists`). RPO ≤24 h. Redis is **not** backed up (cache/ephemeral). Receipt images are in-DB `Bytes`, so the dump captures them (and grows with them — revisit object storage if a dump exceeds a few hundred MB). Phases 2–4 (infra fault tolerance, HA, mobile local backup) deferred — see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-29-postgres-backup-phase1-design.md`.
- Deploy: push to `development` triggers `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` → SSH → `scripts/deploy.sh` (`git reset --hard`, `npm install` with lock, build, `prisma migrate deploy`, `up -d --force-recreate api admin`, then `docker image prune -f` + `docker builder prune -af`). Verify-step polls `https://api.ai-budget.pl/api/v1/health` for up to 120 s. **Pre-deploy guard (ABA-317)**: `deploy.yml` runs `scripts/check-no-shared-utils-runtime-import.sh` on the runner *before* the SSH step — a runtime `import`/`require` of `@budget/shared-utils` in `apps/api/src` fails the deploy (the API has no build step for workspace packages, so it loads the shared-utils TS barrel and prod Node ESM crash-loops on `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_DIR_IMPORT`). Mirrored by an `apps/api` ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule (`allowTypeImports`). `import type` is fine; copy runtime values into an API-local util. **Build-cache pruning matters**: images are built on the VPS each deploy and the build cache accumulated ~4 GB → disk hit 89% (ABA-165); the per-deploy `builder prune` is NOT enough on its own — **`deploy.yml` is path-filtered** (`apps/api/**`, `apps/admin/**`, `packages/**`, `docker/**`, `docker-compose.prod.yml`, `scripts/deploy.sh`), so a stretch of mobile/marketing/docs work means `deploy.sh` never runs and the cache grows untouched. That is how ABA-165 recurred as **ABA-432** a week after the last API deploy: 3.35 GB of cache + a 1.8 GB `buildkit/metadata_v2.db` put `/` at 88%. The scheduled **`.github/workflows/docker-gc.yml`** (`scripts/docker-gc.sh`, Sunday 01:00 UTC an hour before `backup-db.yml`, plus `workflow_dispatch`) is what actually bounds it; it no-ops below 70% and alerts only when the disk is **still** above 85% after cleaning. `.github/workflows/infra-diagnostics.yml` (manual `workflow_dispatch`) dumps a `docker system df` + `du` breakdown over SSH when disk needs investigating. **One Docker daemon is shared with `marketing-ai-*`, `accounting-*`, `legalka-bot` and `shared-nginx`, so `docker volume prune` (any filter) and `docker image prune -a` are both forbidden here** — the volumes include `ai-budget_postgres_data`, and unused-but-tagged images are another project's rollback target. Runbook: `docs/ops/disk-pressure.md`.
- **Snap-installed Docker is held and disabled** (`snap refresh --hold docker`, `snap disable docker`) after the 2026-04-27 incident where snap auto-refresh hijacked `/var/run/docker.sock` from the apt-installed daemon. Do not re-enable snap docker; only the apt-installed `dockerd` (system data root `/var/lib/docker`) owns prod state.
- Adding env vars: `docker restart` does NOT reload `env_file`. Must `docker compose -f docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file .env.production up -d --force-recreate <service>`.
- **CORS (ABA-151)**: `main.ts` builds `corsOrigins` from `process.env.CORS_ORIGIN.split(',')` (falls back to `localhost:8081`/`:3001` when unset). It must be an **explicit comma-separated origin list** in prod `.env.production` — e.g. `CORS_ORIGIN=https://admin.ai-budget.pl,https://ai-budget.pl`. Do **NOT** use `CORS_ORIGIN=*`: it becomes the array `["*"]`, which Express/Nest `cors` matches as a literal origin (not a wildcard), so the admin browser gets no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` and login silently breaks (and `*` is invalid with `credentials:true`). Native mobile is unaffected (no `Origin` header). After changing it, force-recreate the `api` service (see env-var note above).

## Mobile releases (Android)

- **Bare Expo workflow** — `apps/mobile/android/` is committed, so EAS builds from the native project and does **not** run prebuild or read `app.json` for the version. The version shown in **Google Play comes from `apps/mobile/android/app/build.gradle` `versionName`**, not `app.json`. `eas.json` `appVersionSource: "remote"` + production `autoIncrement: true` only manage the integer `versionCode` (build number) remotely; they never touch `versionName`. (Historically this caused every release to ship as `1.0.0` while `versionCode` quietly incremented — ABA-131.)
- **Before each release**: bump `versionName` in `android/app/build.gradle` (source of truth for Play) AND `version` in `app.json` (kept in sync for the in-app update gate) to the same value. Do not hand-edit `versionCode` — EAS remote autoIncrement owns it.
- **CI guard**: `apps/mobile/scripts/check-release-version.js` runs as a production-only step in `mobile-build.yml` and `mobile-eas-build.yml`. It fails the build if `build.gradle` versionName ≠ `app.json` version, and (best effort) if the version isn't strictly greater than the latest `v*.*.*` git tag (skipped when no release tags exist).
- **Publish**: run the *Mobile Build & Publish* workflow (`mobile-build.yml`, profile `production`, submit `true`) → builds AAB `--local` → `eas submit` to the Play production track. After release, also add the new version in **admin → App Versions** so the in-app `UpdatePrompt` knows about it.
- **Foreground-service declaration blocks the submit, and no amount of retrying fixes it (1.18.0; unblocked on `development` since ABA-438)**: while the app declares `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_LOCATION` (Shopping Mode, ABA-408), `eas submit` fails at the *track update* step — the AAB uploads fine — with `Google Api Error: Invalid request - You must let us know whether your app uses any Foreground Service permissions.` fastlane retries it five times and every attempt fails identically, because this is a **Play Console form, not an API field**: Android 14+ requires a per-FGS-type declaration under **Play Console → the app → Policy → App content → Foreground service permissions**, stating what the feature does, why it must run as a foreground service, and (for the `location` type) usually a short video of the in-app journey. It is declared **per app, not per release**, so once filled in it stops blocking future submits. Recovery without rebuilding: the workflow already uploaded the AAB as the `app-release-production` GitHub artifact (30-day retention) — fill in the declaration, then upload that same file by hand in Play Console. A re-run of the workflow also works but burns another remote `versionCode`. Not to be confused with the much heavier `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` declaration, which this app deliberately does not need. **`development` no longer declares either permission** — ABA-438 moved Shopping Mode to `feature/shopping-mode` precisely so releases stop being hostage to this form — so a submit from the mainline works again without it. The declaration still has to be filled before that branch can re-land, and it is per app, not per release, so filling it early costs nothing.

## Observability

- **Health**: `GET /api/v1/health` (public, no auth) — runs `SELECT 1` on Postgres, returns `{status, db, uptimeSeconds, timestamp}`. 503 if DB fails. Used by Docker `HEALTHCHECK` (requires HTTP 200) and CI verify-step.
- **Uptime**: `.github/workflows/uptime-check.yml` runs every 5 min via cron, hits the public `/api/v1/health`. On non-200, sends a Telegram alert via `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID` GitHub Secrets with HTTP code, response body, and a link to the failed run.
- **Backup alerts (ABA-163)**: `backup-db.yml` posts a Telegram alert on any failed nightly DB backup (`TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`/`TELEGRAM_CHAT_ID`), same channel as the uptime check. A silent backup failure is the classic way to discover at restore time that there are no backups — so failures, not successes, are alerted.
- **Infra watch (ABA-165)**: `.github/workflows/infra-watch.yml` — cron every 30 min, SSHes to the VPS and pipes `scripts/infra-check.sh` (`ssh … 'bash -s' < script`) to check root-disk usage (> 85% alerts) and that all 4 prod containers are running and not `unhealthy`; non-zero exit → Telegram alert. Complements `uptime-check.yml` (5-min `/health`) with disk pressure + per-container granularity. Reuses SSH + Telegram secrets (no new ones).
- **Log rotation (ABA-165, gap found in ABA-432)**: `docker-compose.prod.yml` sets `logging` `json-file` `max-size: 10m` / `max-file: 3` on postgres/redis/api/admin (~30 MB/container) so logs can't fill the disk. **That covers only 4 of the 16 containers on the box.** Everything created outside compose has no limit — including **our own** `ai-budget-web-prod` and `ai-budget-app-prod` (hand-created on the VPS in ABA-213/ABA-269) and `shared-nginx`, whose json.log had reached **244 MB** by 2026-08-24. Recreating them with `--log-opt max-size=10m --log-opt max-file=3` is the fix and is **not yet done** (ABA-432); `shared-nginx` needs a window because it briefly interrupts every site on the box. Also left alone deliberately: **`/root/.gradle` 4.3 GB + `/opt/android-sdk` 2.9 GB** of Android toolchain on a server that only runs API/admin/nginx — mobile builds happen on EAS and GitHub runners, so it is almost certainly a leftover, but it may belong to another project.
- **Disaster recovery**: `docs/ops/restore-runbook.md` (DB-only restore) and `docs/ops/disaster-recovery-runbook.md` (full server rebuild after total VPS loss). Both need the offline `age` private key; full rebuild also needs an **offline copy of `.env.production`** (prod secrets live only on the VPS). Redis is cache-only and intentionally not persisted.
- **Errors**: `@sentry/node` v8 captures unhandled exceptions and Express 5xx errors. Init must remain at the top of `main.ts` via `import './instrument'` — moving it after other imports breaks instrumentation.
- **No Prometheus / Datadog / external APM** at this time.
